## Parameters

- Panel `examples/p2/data/panel_toy.csv`, generation seed **17**, n=300
- PIT publish lag **5** business days
- Adj vs raw: AAA teaching 2:1 split at iloc 200

## Versioning and hashes

The script SHA-256s the file against `panel_toy.sha256`. Reproducibility starts with **same bytes → same hash**.

## Survivorship (numeric)

Today's universe keeps AAA/BBB while the panel still has CCC. Survivor-only equal-weight mean return differs from the full historical universe — replaying history with today's members flatters survivors.

## PIT fundamentals

`FUND_pit = FUND_true.shift(5)`. Aligning on `FUND_true` as if it were knowable that day leaks unpublished prints.

### Crypto PIT audit note (pointer)

Before mining crypto “alpha,” audit **availability time**, not just file existence ([arXiv:2608.25348](https://arxiv.org/abs/2608.25348) on Binance BTCUSDT USD-M perps). Teaching checklist: **no same-bar execution**; **funding lag** (do not shift realized funding backward); **no backfill** to manufacture continuity; if **OI publication time is unverified**, disable OI as an alpha input. Full foil + lab in `frontier-tsfm-altdata-crypto`.

## Timezone / session mislabel

Hand example: shift close labels one business day earlier, then align back — **Tuesday’s row carries Wednesday’s close**. That is “off-by-one session ≈ one day of look-ahead.”

## Adj vs raw

Across the split, raw return ≈ **-50.8%**, adj ≈ **-1.6%**. Research must declare which field is used.

## Further reading: data entry points (sources read)

- [Binance Vision](https://data.binance.vision/) hosts bulk historical market files; [sera00111’s tooling note](https://x.com/sera00111/status/2108332340753969191) says minute/hour research can start from Vision and CCXT-style free entry points, and to choose data granularity after you know the strategy.
- [OpenBB quantitative docs](https://docs.openbb.co/odp/python/extensions/data-processing/quantitative) describe `obb.quantitative` stats/factor tools on **already aligned** series — they assume you fixed timestamps first. In the same open-source stack, **PyFolio** (a portfolio performance / tear-sheet library — it turns returns into risk and attribution charts) often sits next to OpenBB and Alphalens for post-trade reporting.
- [PyQuant News “45 APIs for finance”](https://x.com/pyquantnews/status/2116517809920327801) introduces a list of finance data APIs (not just prices) and points to the [public-apis finance section on GitHub](https://github.com/public-apis/public-apis#finance) as the grab-link — treat it as a **source map**, not a strategy.


## Why `panel_nav_honest_shift1` equals `signal × next_ret`

Those two NAV prints are equal because they are the **same PnL products shifted by one timestamp**: `shift(1)×ret` vs `signal×ret.shift(-1)`. Equal NAV is **not** a look-ahead test — both already use next-period returns with a prior signal. Same-bar `signal×ret` would be look-ahead. (See lab print `equality_why`.)

## Information bars vs time bars (AFML ch.2)

**Intuition:** A **time bar** samples the wall clock (every minute, every day). Markets do not speak at a constant rate: a quiet hour and a news burst both become “one bar,” so return variance and microstructure noise look uneven. **Tick / volume / dollar bars** sample *activity* instead — emit a bar after \(N\) trades, \(V\) shares, or \(D\) dollars of notional. Busy periods mint more bars; quiet periods fewer. That is a **research sampling** choice for cleaner statistical clocks — **not** an HFT latency or execution recipe.

**Toy comparison** on one synthetic 5,000-tick tape (`examples/p2/01_data_cleaning.py`):

| Clock | n_bars | mean \|ret\| | std(ret) |
|-------|--------|-------------|----------|
| time/tick every 50 | 100 | ≈0.0045 | ≈0.0058 |
| volume 200 shares | 241 | ≈0.0030 | ≈0.0036 |
| dollar ≈200×mid | 231 | ≈0.0030 | ≈0.0038 |

Activity clocks produce **more** bars with **smaller** per-bar moves on the same tape — the series is closer to an “information clock.”



## Mask-first / upstream contamination (A-share comparison case: limit closes in rolling windows; US analogue: halted / LULD-paused / no-quote rows)

[arXiv:2507.07107](https://arxiv.org/abs/2507.07107) documents a costly A-share pipeline failure: the authors treat limit-up/down closes as **non-executable for their strategy**, yet those closes enter rolling means / correlations / ranks **before** any row filter — **upstream contamination**. **This synthetic toy deliberately sets** those rows as non-executable for the given side/size; the conservative mask is an **experimental protocol**, not a market law that “touching the limit means no fill” (whether a fill happens at a limit depends on side, queue size and counterparties).

**Paper ablation (warning numbers only — not course alpha):** removing the mask **inflates apparent IC by ~+18%** while **cutting realised Sharpe by ~0.44** — the model learns returns it cannot trade.

**Keep three things apart (R33):**

- **Information availability (observation):** was the value public and visible at decision time?
- **Information quality:** is the value clean (not a bad print, not stale, not a filled-in gap)?
- **Executability (tradability, by side and size):** given the session, quotes, trading status and liquidity at that time, could an order of **this side and this size** be filled?

Hitting a limit or a pause affects only the third question, and it depends on side and size; it does not by itself mean the price “did not exist” or “cannot be used as information”. Whether a public, quality-checked historical trade price enters the features is decided by the research protocol; “cannot order now” does not imply “information unavailable”. The US analogue is an explicit state such as **no matching during a pause / no executable quote**, not erasing legitimate prices printed before the pause.

**Boolean mask-first (this toy’s optional conservative protocol):**

1. At load time build `obs[t,i]` (public at decision time), `quality[t,i]` (clean) and `trade[t,i,side,size]` (toy setting: not halted ∧ not at the limit on that side ∧ past seasoning) separately; the toy optionally combines them into a conservative `M[t,i]` (see the three-mask lab below).
2. Every rolling operator reads **only** cells with `M=1`; any `M=0` in the window → mark output unreliable (conservative).
3. **Post-hoc row drops cannot un-poison** aggregates already computed.

### Three independent masks (lab printout, hand-made 4 days × 2 stocks)

`examples/p2/01_data_cleaning.py` builds `obs` (released before the decision), `quality` (not a bad print) and `trade[t,i,side,size]` (by side and size) separately. Features use only `obs ∧ quality`; orders look only at `trade`.

```text
d1 AAA close= 11.0 obs=1 quality=1 feature_uses=1 buy100=0 sell100=1 sell10000=0
d2 BBB close= 99.0 obs=1 quality=0 feature_uses=0 buy100=0 sell100=0 sell10000=0
d3 AAA close= 11.3 obs=0 quality=1 feature_uses=0 buy100=1 sell100=1 sell10000=1
ASSERT_A observable+clean but buy not executable -> feature keeps it, buy order rejected: OK
ASSERT_B observable but bad quality -> excluded from features: OK
ASSERT_C clean but not observable at decision -> excluded from features: OK
ASSERT_D same cell: sell100 ok, sell10000 not, buy not -> trade mask is per side/size: OK
ASSERT_E optional toy protocol M = obs & quality & trade[buy,100] drops 1 extra cell (d1 AAA): OK
three_mask_summary: feature_cells_used 6 of 8; protocol_M_cells 5 of 8
```

Takeaways: d1 AAA is public and clean, so it **still counts as information**, even though an order of that side/size cannot be filled; d2 BBB is public but a bad print, so it stays out of features; d3 AAA is clean but not yet released at decision time, so it stays out. Combining all three into one conservative `M` is only this toy’s **optional conservative protocol**, not a general rule.

### Five-close sketch (lab printout)

| day | close | toy protocol M | naive MA3 | mask-first MA3 |
|-----|-------|-----------|-----------|----------------|
| 0 | 10.0 | 1 | — | — |
| 1 | 10.1 | 1 | — | — |
| 2 | 10.2 | 1 | 10.10 | 10.10 |
| 3 | **11.2** (limit print) | **0** | **10.50** | **10.15** |
| 4 | 10.3 | 1 | **10.57** | **10.25** |

The limit print contaminates the naive MA until it rolls off; mask-first **never reads it**.

### Toy panel numbers (`examples/p2/01_data_cleaning.py`)

Synthetic T=750×N=120; 10-day momentum. **Toy ≠ paper tables:**

| Metric | Contam. apparent | Contam. realisable | Mask-first |
|--------|------------------|--------------------|------------|
| RankIC | ≈0.026 | ≈0.005 | ≈0.002 |
| L/S Sharpe | fantasy ≈3.01 | realisable ≈0.91 | ≈0.81 |

Apparent vs realisable IC inflate ≈ **+394%** (toy); fantasy−realisable Sharpe gap ≈ **2.09**. Keep the mechanism: **dirty windows make limit-chasing look predictable; the same signal collapses under fillability.**

<Callout type="warn" title="Debunk / REJECT">
Do **not** ship the paper's Sharpe **2.05** (synthetic) / **1.63** (real), **213-factor** engine, or **AdjMSE** system as course edge. Keep only the **mask-first optional conservative protocol** + contamination diagnosis.
</Callout>


## Data versions, corporate actions & trading calendars

**Intuition:** the same stock over the same period can show a different monthly return under a different “data version” or calculation convention. A backtest number is reproducible only if you write down **which version you used**.

**Real example (verified on Ken French’s official site):** the [Ken French Data Library](https://mba.tuck.dartmouth.edu/pages/faculty/ken.french/data_library.html) states that CRSP’s Legacy (FIZ) files were discontinued after the December 2024 data release; **beginning with the January 2025 data release**, US research returns are generated from the new Flat File Format 2.0 (CIZ). The site highlights: FIZ monthly returns are “month to month holding period returns with dividends reinvested at month-end”; CIZ monthly returns are “**compounded daily returns** with dividends reinvested on their **ex-dates**.” The site also keeps historical data based on the legacy FIZ format.

**Formula:** with daily total return `r_d = (P_d + D_d) / P_(d−1) − 1` (`D_d` non-zero only on the ex-date), the CIZ-style monthly return is `R_m = Π_(d in m) (1 + r_d) − 1`; the month-end-reinvest style is roughly `(P_end + D) / P_start − 1`.

**Numeric example (`examples/p2/05_data_versions_calendar.py`, seed=7, synthetic):** the same prices and one dividend of 2.0:

```text
Jan month-end reinvest  : -0.102533
Jan ex-date reinvest    : -0.104362
difference (bp)         : -18.28
```

The two conventions differ by about 18 bp — the data are not wrong; the **convention differs**.

**Corporate action (split):** on a 2-for-1 split day the raw price goes from 51.0 to 25.6, producing a fake −49.8% “crash”; the adjusted return that day is only +0.39%:

```text
raw daily ret     : [0.02, -0.498, 0.0117]
adjusted daily ret: [0.02, 0.0039, 0.0117]
```

**Trading calendar:** two markets have different holidays (market B is closed on 04-04). Forward-filling B’s **returns** (ffill) “invents” a return on a closed day; align by each market’s own calendar, or use only the intersection of open days:

```text
A days=8 B days=7 inner-join days=7
ffill rows where B return is a stale copy: 1
```

**Practical rule:** the data sheet states: source + version/release month + return convention (when dividends are reinvested) + adjustment method + trading calendar used. After a version change, rerun the old result first, then compare.

disclaimer: synthetic data; learning only.

## US data sources: who provides it, may you use it, when was it known (checked 2026-11-08)

| Source | What this lesson covers | Do not write |
|------|------|------|
| SEC EDGAR (data.sec.gov) | No API key; 10-digit CIK; no CORS; nightly bulk ZIP ([SEC](https://www.sec.gov/search-filings/edgar-application-programming-interfaces)) | A fixed User-Agent format or rate number (not verified) |
| FRED / ALFRED | API key required ([key](https://fred.stlouisfed.org/docs/api/api_key.html)); ALFRED real-time period ≠ observation date, default is what is known today ([realtime](https://fred.stlouisfed.org/docs/api/fred/realtime_period.html)) | “Public domain so anything goes” |
| Ken French Data Library | CIZ version in `factor-workflow`; -99.99/-999 missing markers ([site](https://mba.tuck.dartmouth.edu/pages/faculty/ken.french/data_library.html)) | A free CRSP stock database |
| CRSP | Active/inactive securities, PERMNO/PERMCO, delisting data; subscription product ([CRSP](https://www.crsp.org/research/crsp-us-stock-databases/)) | Free |
| yfinance | README: not affiliated, endorsed, or vetted by Yahoo; Apache licence; intended for personal use only ([README](https://github.com/ranaroussi/yfinance)) | Official, or free to redistribute |
| Massive (formerly Polygon.io) | Free tier is end-of-day data at 5 API calls/min; separate subscriptions per asset class; professional use needs a business licence ([Massive](https://massive.com/stocks)) | Free real-time or 20-year history |
| Alpaca Basic | Free; real-time is IEX only; 30 WebSocket symbols; history since 2016; latest-15-minutes restriction ([Alpaca](https://docs.alpaca.markets/docs/about-market-data-api)) | Free real-time SIP |
| FINRA short interest / daily short volume; SEC FTD | Twice-monthly short positions published ~7 business days later; off-exchange short-sale flow; outstanding fail balances (see `pairs-stat-arb`, [FINRA](https://www.finra.org/finra-data/browse-catalog/equity-short-interest), [SEC](https://www.sec.gov/data-research/sec-markets-data/fails-deliver-data)) | Usable on the settlement date; short volume = short interest; FTD = naked shorting |

**FRED/ALFRED terms (per review item R16):** API access to FRED/ALFRED must be checked against both the service/API terms and each series’ upstream rights; being publicly readable, free, or a series marked public domain does not mean the way the service is accessed, or downstream development, model training, storing, caching, archiving or redistribution, is automatically permitted. As of 2026-11-08 the official terms list restrictions on those uses. This course distributes only self-made FRED/ALFRED-shaped fixtures and provides no default path for real downloads, caching or model training; real use is not performed until the applicable permission is obtained. ([FRED legal terms](https://fred.stlouisfed.org/legal/))

**Lab (offline):** `examples/p2/06_us_data_sources_pit.py` + `data/us_fixtures/*.json` (self-made):

```text
PIT value = 1000 (original filing), naive “latest” = 920 (amendment 1); the 2026-04-15 decision gets None (not yet released)
ALFRED as-known: 2.0 / 2.3 / 2.4; final value 2.4 backfilled into the past → 2 of 3 feature rows differ
SYN_MACRO_2 has no release time → excluded
```

## When is a financial report "known"? Filing deadlines and EDGAR filing dates (checked 2026-11-10)

**The period-end date is not the date you knew the numbers.** On the last day of a quarter, nobody has that quarter's report yet. Per the SEC form instructions, an annual report on Form 10-K is due 60 days after fiscal year-end for large accelerated filers, 75 days for accelerated filers and 90 days for everyone else ([Form 10-K](https://www.sec.gov/files/form10-k.pdf)); the filer categories are SEC size tiers based mainly on the market value of shares held by the public. A quarterly report on Form 10-Q is due in 40 days (large accelerated and accelerated filers) or 45 days (everyone else) ([Form 10-Q](https://www.sec.gov/files/form10-q.pdf)). A current report on Form 8-K, for events such as an acquisition or a CEO change, is generally due within four business days of the event ([investor.gov](https://www.investor.gov/introduction-investing/investing-basics/glossary/form-8-k)). EDGAR (the SEC's filing system) also treats a filing whose transmission starts after 5:30 p.m. Eastern as filed on the next business day ([Reg S-T Rule 13](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-232.13); insider Forms 3/4/5, Schedules 13D/13G and Form 144 get until 10 p.m.). The official "filing date" and the acceptance time can therefore differ by a day, so store both. In a backtest, use the acceptance timestamp when you have it, and act no earlier than the next tradable moment after it (this is what the EDGAR-shaped as-of join in the lab above does). If all you have is the period-end date, push it forward by at least the statutory deadline; never use the period-end date itself. Deadlines are upper bounds: many companies file earlier, so this fallback is conservative, not exact. Institutional holdings on Form 13F (managers with at least $100 million in certain US securities) are due within 45 days after quarter-end; for 3Q 2026 that is 2026-11-16 ([SEC FAQ](https://www.sec.gov/rules-regulations/staff-guidance/division-investment-management-frequently-asked-questions/frequently-asked-questions-about-form-13f)). A 13F-based signal becomes usable on the filing date, not the quarter-end. Status: on 2026-05-05 the SEC proposed letting companies file semiannual reports on a new Form 10-S instead of quarterly 10-Qs ([SEC press release](https://www.sec.gov/newsroom/press-releases/2026-42-sec-proposes-amendments-permit-optional-semiannual-reporting-public-companies)). It was proposed on 2026-05-05 and is not adopted as of 2026-11-11, so quarterly reporting is unchanged.


**Those 60 / 75 / 90 and 40 / 45 clocks belong to official 12b-2 filer categories, with dollar thresholds, a second-quarter measurement date, and hysteresis (checked 2026-11-12).** The deadline numbers and the sentence that filer categories are “SEC size tiers based mainly on the market value of shares held by the public” stay as already taught. So does the Form 10-S proposal sentence. This section only adds the official current-law numbers. Do not edit `examples/p2/09_filing_dates_toy.py`.

Current [17 CFR 240.12b-2](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.12b-2): an **accelerated filer**, after it first meets the conditions as of the end of its fiscal year, had “an aggregate worldwide market value of the voting and non-voting common equity held by its non-affiliates of **$75 million or more, but less than $700 million**, as of the **last business day of the issuer's most recently completed second fiscal quarter**,” has been subject to section 13(a) or 15(d) for at least **twelve calendar months**, has filed **at least one annual report**, and is not eligible for smaller-reporting-company treatment under the listed revenue test. A **large accelerated filer** is the same structure at **$700 million or more**. The year-end determination “governs the deadlines for the annual report to be filed for that fiscal year, the quarterly and annual reports to be filed for the subsequent fiscal year,” and later reports while the issuer remains in that status.

**Hysteresis:** once an accelerated filer, it remains one unless, at fiscal year-end, that public float “was **less than $60 million**” as of the last business day of the most recently completed second fiscal quarter, or it is eligible for the SRC revenue test — then it becomes a non-accelerated filer. Once a large accelerated filer, it remains one unless that public float “was **less than $560 million**” (or the SRC revenue test applies). If, after that large-accelerated test, the float was “**$60 million or more, but less than $560 million**” and the SRC revenue test does not apply, it becomes an accelerated filer; if “**less than $60 million**” (or the SRC revenue test applies), it becomes a non-accelerated filer.

**S7-2026-18** (issued 2026-05-19; comments were due 2026-07-20; last reviewed or updated 2026-07-31) remains **Proposed**. It would, among other things, raise the large-accelerated threshold from the current $700 million to $2 billion and collapse categories; do **not** teach $2 billion, a two-category model, or changed deadlines as current law.

Do not use fiscal year-end market cap as the 12b-2 measurement date. Do not ignore hysteresis (a large accelerated filer does not drop to accelerated at the first print under $700 million). Do not treat a brand-new IPO as immediately a large accelerated filer just because float exceeds $700 million (twelve months and one annual report are official conditions). SRC exists and can affect accelerated / large-accelerated eligibility through the listed revenue test; this lesson names that it exists and does not build a full SRC engine. No filer-status planning advice.

**Missing a 10-K or 10-Q statutory deadline is not automatically the end of the story: Rule 12b-25 requires a Form 12b-25 notification by the next business day, and the report can be deemed timely only if the filer meets the official conditions, including actually filing within 15 calendar days (annual) or 5 calendar days (quarterly) (checked 2026-11-12).** Do not rewrite the 60 / 75 / 90 and 40 / 45 deadline numbers, the 12b-2 thresholds, or the Form 10-S proposal sentence. Do not edit `examples/p2/09_filing_dates_toy.py` or `examples/p2/17_xbrl_pit_10b51_toy.py`. This section adds the official late-filing-notification rule, so a cleaner does not treat a missed statutory date as a hard “no filing exists,” or treat Form 12b-25 itself as the 10-K or 10-Q.

[17 CFR 240.12b-25(a)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.12b-25): if a listed report, including a Form 10-K or Form 10-Q, “is not filed within the time period prescribed for such report, the registrant, no later than one business day after the due date for such report, shall file a Form 12b-25,” with disclosure of its inability to file timely and the reasons in reasonable detail. 12b-25(b): the report “shall be deemed to be filed on the prescribed due date” only if the official conditions are met, including that the reason could not be eliminated without unreasonable effort or expense, that the annual report will be filed no later than the **fifteenth calendar day** following the prescribed due date or the quarterly report no later than the **fifth calendar day**, **and** the report is actually filed within that period. 12b-25(d): even then, the registrant is not eligible to use a 1933 Act registration form whose use is predicated on timely filed reports until the subject report is actually filed. EDGAR often labels the notification NT 10-K or NT 10-Q; the official form is Form 12b-25.

Do not teach that every late 10-K automatically gets 15 extra days, or that the statutory clocks have moved. The 15- and 5-day periods are **calendar** days, not business days; the notification itself is due by the next **business** day. Do not treat Form 12b-25 / an NT filing as the periodic report. No filer-status planning advice. This item is quiz-only; there is no new lab script.

**An issuer is not required to register a class of equity securities under Exchange Act section 12(g) if, on the last day of its most recent fiscal year, it had total assets not exceeding $10 million, or the class was held of record by fewer than 2,000 persons and fewer than 500 of those persons were not accredited investors (banks, savings-and-loan holding companies, and bank holding companies use a fewer-than-2,000-person test without the 500 prong). The statutory trigger is the other side of that test, with a 120-day clock after fiscal year-end (checked 2026-11-12).** Do not rewrite the already-taught 12b-2 filer categories, the 10-K / 10-Q clocks, the Form 13F 45-day sentence, or the Form 10-S proposal sentence (“not adopted as of 2026-11-11”). Do not edit `examples/p2/09_filing_dates_toy.py` or `examples/p2/17_xbrl_pit_10b51_toy.py`. This section adds the official 12(g) / 12g-1 map so a cleaner does not assume every US company with public shareholders is a 12(g) registrant, or invent an inflation-adjusted asset figure the official current texts do not state. No issuer or listing advice. This item is quiz-only; there is no new lab script.

[17 CFR 240.12g-1](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.12g-1): an issuer is not required to register a class of equity pursuant to section 12(g)(1) “if on the last day of its most recent fiscal year: (a) The issuer had total assets not exceeding $10 million; or (b)(1) The class of equity securities was held of record by fewer than 2,000 persons and fewer than 500 of those persons were not accredited investors (as such term is defined in § 230.501(a) of this chapter, determined as of such day rather than at the time of the sale of the securities); or (2)” the class was held of record by fewer than 2,000 persons in the case of a bank, a savings and loan holding company, or a bank holding company.

[15 U.S.C. 78l(g)(1)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/15/78l): the issuer shall, “within 120 days after the last day of its first fiscal year ended on which the issuer has total assets exceeding $10,000,000 and a class of equity security (other than an exempted security) held of record by either— (i) 2,000 persons, or (ii) 500 persons who are not accredited investors (as such term is defined by the Commission),” register that security. Each such registration statement “shall become effective sixty days after filing with the Commission or within such shorter period as the Commission may direct.” Banks, savings-and-loan holding companies, and bank holding companies use a 2,000-holder test without the 500-unaccredited prong, also with a 120-day clock and the $10,000,000 asset test. The SEC Exchange Act reporting page also names listing on a U.S. exchange as a separate registration path (section 12(b)). Official current 12g-1 (eCFR title 17 up to date as of 2026-11-09; 12g-1 last amended December 28, 2016), the statute, and that SEC page still use **$10 million** / **$10,000,000**. Do not invent an inflation-adjusted asset figure.

Do not copy a sloppy “fewer than 2,000 **or** 500” exemption paraphrase over official 12g-1(b)(1), which is fewer than 2,000 **and** fewer than 500 of those persons were not accredited investors (the statutory *trigger* uses 2,000 **or** 500 unaccredited, together with assets exceeding $10,000,000). “Accredited investor” is defined by reference to § 230.501(a) as of that day; do not build a 501(a) inflation engine. “Held of record” is defined in Rule 12g5-1; an employee-compensation-plan carve-out exists; do not build that engine. 12(g)(4) termination is a different official paragraph; name that it exists, do not build a termination engine. Do not flatten the 120-day or 60-day clocks into already-taught 10-K / 10-Q or 12b-2 clocks, or into already-taught Form 10-S proposal wording.


**Exchange Act section 12(g)(4) / Rule 12g-4: registration of a class under 12(g) is terminated ninety days, or such shorter period as the Commission may determine, after the issuer files a certification on Form 15; Rule 12g-4 also has a second official prong, and immediately suspends the duty to file section 13(a) reports upon the Form 15 filing (checked 2026-11-12).** Do not rewrite already-taught 12g-1 / 12(g) registration thresholds, 12b-2 filer categories, 10-K / 10-Q clocks, the Form 13F 45-day sentence, or the Form 10-S proposal sentence (“not adopted as of 2026-11-11”). Do not edit `examples/p2/09_filing_dates_toy.py` or `examples/p2/17_xbrl_pit_10b51_toy.py`. enrich25 named 12(g)(4) termination as existing and did not build it. This section adds the official termination map so a cleaner does not treat a Form 15 as ending 12(g) registration on the filing date, invent an inflation-adjusted asset figure, or miss the official 500-person / $10 million three-fiscal-year prong that appears in the rule. No issuer or listing advice. This item is quiz-only; there is no new lab script.

[15 U.S.C. 78l(g)(4)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/15/78l): “Registration of any class of security pursuant to this subsection shall be terminated ninety days, or such shorter period as the Commission may determine, after the issuer files a certification with the Commission that the number of holders of record of such class of security is reduced to less than 300 persons, or, in the case of a bank, a savings and loan holding company (as defined in section 1467a of title 12), or a bank holding company, as such term is defined in section 1841 of title 12, 1,200 persons persons. [1]” The LII official-statute copy prints the typographical artifact “1,200 persons persons. [1]” — quote the operative sentence; do not silently tidy it if that clause is quoted. The Commission shall after notice and opportunity for hearing deny termination if it finds the certification is untrue; termination is deferred pending final determination on the question of denial.

[17 CFR 240.12g-4](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.12g-4) (eCFR title 17 up to date as of 2026-11-09; last amended May 10, 2016): termination takes effect 90 days, or such shorter period as the Commission may determine, after the issuer certifies on Form 15 that the class is held of record by (1) fewer than 300 persons, or 1,200 persons for a bank, a savings and loan holding company, or a bank holding company; **or (2) fewer than 500 persons, where the total assets of the issuer have not exceeded $10 million on the last day of each of the issuer’s most recent three fiscal years.** 12g-4(b): the issuer’s duty to file any reports required under section 13(a) “shall be suspended immediately upon filing a certification on Form 15”; if the certification is subsequently withdrawn or denied, the issuer shall, within 60 days, file all reports which would have been required had the certification not been filed. Official current 12g-4 still uses **$10 million** (last amended May 10, 2016). Do not invent an inflation-adjusted $10 million figure.

Do not flatten the statute and the rule: the official 500-person / $10 million three-fiscal-year prong, Form 15, the immediate 13(a) suspension, and the 60-day restoration clock appear in Rule 12g-4 and are not a rewrite of already-taught 12g-1. Do not treat the Form 15 filing date as the termination date (termination takes effect 90 days later, or sooner if the Commission so determines; the 13(a) duty is what is suspended immediately). Do not build a 12g5-1 held-of-record or 501(a) engine. Do not rewrite already-taught 12g-1, 12b-2, 10-K / 10-Q, or Form 13F sentences, and do not bump the Form 10-S “not adopted as of 2026-11-11” date.

## 17 CFR 240.12g-2: Securities deemed to be registered pursuant to section 12(g)(1) upon termination of exemption pursuant to section 12(g)(2)(A) or (B) (checked 2026-11-19)

Already-taught 12g-1 / 12g-4 sentences stay as written. Do not bump the already-shipped 12g-4 “up to date as of 2026-11-09” date only. Packed 12d2-1 and packed 12b-6 stay as written. This box is a different rule: a class that would have been required to be registered pursuant to section 12(g)(1), except that it was exempt because it was listed and registered on a national securities exchange under section 12(g)(2)(A), or because it was issued by a registered investment company under section 12(g)(2)(B), shall upon the termination of that listing and registration or of that company registration, and without the filing of an additional registration statement, be deemed registered pursuant to section 12(g)(1) if at the time of such termination official (a) or official (b) is met. Official typography is “without the filing of an additional registration statement.” Official (a) is an unwithdrawn election to be regulated as a business development company pursuant to sections 55 through 65 of the Investment Company Act of 1940 (official U.S.C. typography is “15 U.S.C. 80a-54 through 64”). Official (b) typography is held of record by 300 or more persons, or 1,200 or more persons in the case of a bank, a savings and loan holding company as defined in section 10 of the Home Owners' Loan Act, or a bank holding company as defined in section 2 of the Bank Holding Company Act of 1956. Official typography italicizes the letter l in 15 U.S.C. 78<em>l</em>. Official typography is “1,200 or more persons” and “Home Owners' Loan Act” (ASCII apostrophe). Official CITA is “[81 FR 28705, May 10, 2016].” Official API XML has no SECAUTH; do not invent one. Locked 12g5-1 is named only; do not pack it. Do not flatten this box into packed 12g-1, packed 12g-4, packed 12d2-1, or packed 12b-6. Learning only; no issuer, filer, or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.12g-2](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.12g-2): “Any class of securities that would have been required to be registered pursuant to section 12(g)(1) of the Act (15 U.S.C. 78<em>l</em>(g)(1)) except for the fact that it was exempt from such registration by section 12(g)(2)(A) of the Act (15 U.S.C. 78<em>l</em>(g)(2)(A)) because it was listed and registered on a national securities exchange, or by section 12(g)(2)(B) of the Act (15 U.S.C. 78<em>l</em>(g)(2)(B)) because it was issued by an investment company registered pursuant to section 8 of the Investment Company Act of 1940 (15 U.S.C. 80a-8), shall upon the termination of the listing and registration of such class or the termination of the registration of such company and without the filing of an additional registration statement be deemed to be registered pursuant to section 12(g)(1) of the Act if at the time of such termination:” Official (a) “The issuer of such class of securities has elected to be regulated as a business development company pursuant to sections 55 through 65 of the Investment Company Act of 1940 (15 U.S.C. 80a-54 through 64) and such election has not been withdrawn; or” Official (b) “Securities of the class are not exempt from such registration pursuant to section 12 of the Act (15 U.S.C. 78<em>l</em>) or rules thereunder and all securities of such class are held of record by 300 or more persons, or 1,200 or more persons in the case of a bank; a savings and loan holding company, as such term is defined in section 10 of the Home Owners' Loan Act (12 U.S.C. 1461); or a bank holding company, as such term is defined in section 2 of the Bank Holding Company Act of 1956 (12 U.S.C. 1841).” Official source line: “[81 FR 28705, May 10, 2016].” Official API XML has no SECAUTH. Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-17.

Packed 12g-1 / 12g-4 / 12d2-1 / 12b-6 exist and are named only. Locked 12g5-1 is named only.

## 17 CFR 240.12a-5: Temporary exemption of substituted or additional securities (checked 2026-11-19)

Already-taught 12g-1 / 12g-4 / 12g-2 sentences stay as written. Do not bump the already-shipped 12g-4 “up to date as of 2026-11-09” date only. Packed 12d2-1 and packed 12b-6 stay as written. This box packs the whole current official 12a-5 section: official (a)(1) temporarily exempts a substituted or other security from section 12(a) on an issued or “when-issued” basis, and an additional amount of the original security on a “when-issued” basis, when holders of a security admitted to trading on a national securities exchange obtain the right to acquire them. Official (a)(2) requires all three official (a)(2)(i)–(iii) conditions. Official typography uses typographic U+201C/U+201D around “when-issued.” Official (b) terminates on the earliest of the listed dates, including official typography “When registration of the exempt security on the exchange become effective” (official “become”; do not tidy into “becomes”), “The close of business on the tenth day after,” and “The close of business on the one hundred and twentieth day after” (official capital T). Official typography italicizes <em>Provided, however,</em> including the comma. Official (e) typography is “section 12(a) of the act.” Official CITA is “[13 FR 8185, Dec. 22, 1948, as amended at 19 FR 669, Feb. 5, 1954; 20 FR 2081, Apr. 2, 1955; 53 FR 41206, Oct. 20, 1988].” Pack the official full SECAUTH (Secs. 3, 12, 48 Stat. 882, 892; 15 U.S.C. 78c (12), 78<em>l</em>) (space before `(12)`; italic l). Official Form 26 and official 10b-1 are named only; do not pack them as second cores. This is not packed 12g-2, packed 12d2-1, or packed 12b-6. Learning only; no issuer or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.12a-5](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.12a-5): Official (a)(1) “Subject to the conditions of paragraph (a)(2) of this section, whenever the holders of a security admitted to trading on a national securities exchange (hereinafter called the original security) obtain the right, by operation of law or otherwise, to acquire all or any part of a class of another or substitute security of the same or another issuer, or an additional amount of the original security, then:” Official (a)(1)(i) “All or any part of the class of such other or substituted security shall be temporarily exempted from the operation of section 12(a) to the extent necessary to render lawful transactions therein on an issued or “when-issued” basis on any national securities exchange on which the original, the other or the substituted security is lawfully admitted to trading; and” Official (a)(1)(ii) “The additional amount of the original security shall be temporarily exempted from the operation of section 12(a) to the extent necessary to render lawful transactions therein on a “when-issued” basis on any national securities exchange on which the original security is lawfully admitted to trading.” Official (a)(2) “The exemptions provided by paragraph (a)(1) of this section shall be available only if the following conditions are met:” Official (a)(2)(i) “A registration statement is in effect under the Securities Act of 1933 to the extent required as to the security which is the subject of such exemption, or the terms of any applicable exemption from registration under such act have been complied with, if required;” Official (a)(2)(ii) “Any stockholder approval necessary to the issuance of the security which is the subject of the exemption, has been obtained; and” Official (a)(2)(iii) “All other necessary official action, other than the filing or recording of charter amendments or other documents with the appropriate State authorities, has been taken to authorize and assure the issuance of the security which is the subject of such exemption.” Official (b) “The exemption provided by this section shall terminate on the earliest of the following dates:” Official (b)(1) “When registration of the exempt security on the exchange become effective;” Official (b)(2) “When the exempt security is granted unlisted trading privileges on the exchange;” Official (b)(3) “The close of business on the tenth day after (i) withdrawal of an application for registration of the exempt security on the exchange; (ii) withdrawal by the exchange of its certification of approval of the exempt security for listing and registration; (iii) withdrawal of an application for admission of the exempt security to unlisted trading privileges on the exchange; or (iv) the sending to the exchange of notice of the entry of an order by the Commission denying any application for admission of the exempt security to unlisted trading privileges on the exchange;” Official (b)(4) “The close of business on the one hundred and twentieth day after the date on which the exempt security was admitted by action of the exchange to trading thereon as a security exempted from the operation of section 12 (a) by this section, unless prior thereto an application for registration of the exempt security or for admission of the exempt security to unlisted trading privileges on the exchange has been filed.” Official (c) “Notwithstanding paragraph (b) of this section, the Commission, having due regard for the public interest and the protection of investors, may at any time extend the period of exemption of any security by this rule or may sooner terminate the exemption upon notice to the exchange and to the issuer of the extension or termination thereof.” Official (d) “The Exchange shall file with the Commission a notification on Form 26 promptly after taking action to admit any security to trading under this section: <em>Provided, however,</em> That no notification need be filed under this section concerning the admission or proposed admission to trading of additional amounts of a class of security admitted to trading on such exchange.” Official (e) “Section 240.10b-1 shall be applicable to all securities exempted from the operation of section 12(a) of the act by this section.” Official caption typography is “Temporary exemption of substituted or additional securities.” Official Form 26 footnote typography is “Copy filed with the Federal Register Division..” Official source line: “[13 FR 8185, Dec. 22, 1948, as amended at 19 FR 669, Feb. 5, 1954; 20 FR 2081, Apr. 2, 1955; 53 FR 41206, Oct. 20, 1988].” Official SECAUTH typography is (Secs. 3, 12, 48 Stat. 882, 892; 15 U.S.C. 78c (12), 78<em>l</em>). Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-17.

Official Form 26 and official 10b-1 are named only. Packed 12g-2 / 12d2-1 / 12b-6 exist and are named only. Do not bump the already-shipped 12g-4 “up to date as of 2026-11-09” date only.

**Lab:** section BH of the same `16_index` script (after section BG / 14d-8; 225/225 after BH):

```text
=== BH. 17 CFR 240.12a-5: Temporary exemption of substituted or additional securities ===
official caption typography is Temporary exemption of substituted or additional securities.
12a-5(a)(1): Subject to the conditions of paragraph (a)(2) of this section, whenever the holders of a security admitted to trading on a national securities exchange (hereinafter called the original security) obtain the right, by operation of law or otherwise, to acquire all or any part of a class of another or substitute security of the same or another issuer, or an additional amount of the original security, then:
12a-5(a)(1)(i): All or any part of the class of such other or substituted security shall be temporarily exempted from the operation of section 12(a) to the extent necessary to render lawful transactions therein on an issued or “when-issued” basis on any national securities exchange on which the original, the other or the substituted security is lawfully admitted to trading; and
12a-5(a)(1)(ii): The additional amount of the original security shall be temporarily exempted from the operation of section 12(a) to the extent necessary to render lawful transactions therein on a “when-issued” basis on any national securities exchange on which the original security is lawfully admitted to trading.
12a-5(a)(2): The exemptions provided by paragraph (a)(1) of this section shall be available only if the following conditions are met:
12a-5(a)(2)(i): A registration statement is in effect under the Securities Act of 1933 to the extent required as to the security which is the subject of such exemption, or the terms of any applicable exemption from registration under such act have been complied with, if required;
12a-5(a)(2)(ii): Any stockholder approval necessary to the issuance of the security which is the subject of the exemption, has been obtained; and
12a-5(a)(2)(iii): All other necessary official action, other than the filing or recording of charter amendments or other documents with the appropriate State authorities, has been taken to authorize and assure the issuance of the security which is the subject of such exemption.
12a-5(b): The exemption provided by this section shall terminate on the earliest of the following dates:
12a-5(b)(1): When registration of the exempt security on the exchange become effective;
12a-5(b)(2): When the exempt security is granted unlisted trading privileges on the exchange;
12a-5(b)(3): The close of business on the tenth day after (i) withdrawal of an application for registration of the exempt security on the exchange; (ii) withdrawal by the exchange of its certification of approval of the exempt security for listing and registration; (iii) withdrawal of an application for admission of the exempt security to unlisted trading privileges on the exchange; or (iv) the sending to the exchange of notice of the entry of an order by the Commission denying any application for admission of the exempt security to unlisted trading privileges on the exchange;
12a-5(b)(4): The close of business on the one hundred and twentieth day after the date on which the exempt security was admitted by action of the exchange to trading thereon as a security exempted from the operation of section 12 (a) by this section, unless prior thereto an application for registration of the exempt security or for admission of the exempt security to unlisted trading privileges on the exchange has been filed.
12a-5(c): Notwithstanding paragraph (b) of this section, the Commission, having due regard for the public interest and the protection of investors, may at any time extend the period of exemption of any security by this rule or may sooner terminate the exemption upon notice to the exchange and to the issuer of the extension or termination thereof.
12a-5(d): The Exchange shall file with the Commission a notification on Form 26 promptly after taking action to admit any security to trading under this section: Provided, however, That no notification need be filed under this section concerning the admission or proposed admission to trading of additional amounts of a class of security admitted to trading on such exchange.
official Form 26 footnote typography is Copy filed with the Federal Register Division.
12a-5(e): Section 240.10b-1 shall be applicable to all securities exempted from the operation of section 12(a) of the act by this section.
```

## 17 CFR 240.12f-4: Exemption of securities admitted to unlisted trading privileges from sections 13, 14 and 16 (checked 2026-11-19)

Packed 12a-5, packed 12d2-1, packed 12g-2, and packed section 16 boxes stay as written. This box packs the whole current official 12f-4 section: a UTP security is exempt from section 13 unless official (a)(1) or official (a)(2) applies. Official (b) exempts the security from section 14 unless the security is also listed and registered on a national securities exchange or registered pursuant to section 12(g). Official (c)(1) typography is “section 16 of the act” (lowercase “act”) and official (c)(2) typography is “section 16 of the Act”; do not tidy either token into the other. Official typography uses typographic quotes around “registered on a national securities exchange” in official (a). Official CITA is “[30 FR 482, Jan. 14, 1965].” Official SECAUTH typography is (Sec. 3, 78 Stat. 565, 15 U.S.C. 78<em>l</em>) (italic l). This is not packed 12a-5, packed 12d2-1, packed 12g-2, or a packed section 16 box. Official 12f-2 is named only; do not pack it as a second 12f core. Learning only; no issuer or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.12f-4](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.12f-4): Official (a) “Any security for which unlisted trading privileges on any national securities exchange have been continued or extended pursuant to section 12(f) of the Act shall be exempt from section 13 of the Act unless (1) such security or another security of the same issuer is listed and registered on a national securities exchange or registered pursuant to section 12(g) of the Act, or (2) such issuer would be required to file information, documents and reports pursuant to section 15(d) of the Act but for the fact that securities of the issuer are deemed to be “registered on a national securities exchange” within the meaning of section 12(f)(6) of the Act.” Official (b) “Any security for which unlisted trading privileges on any national securities exchange have been continued or extended pursuant to section 12(f) of the Act shall be exempt from section 14 of the Act unless such security is also listed and registered on a national securities exchange or registered pursuant to section 12(g) of the Act.” Official (c)(1) “Any equity security for which unlisted trading privileges on any national securities exchange have been continued or extended pursuant to section 12(f) of the Act shall be exempt from section 16 of the act unless such security or another equity security of the same issuer is listed and registered on a national securities exchange or registered pursuant to section 12(g) of the Act.” Official (c)(2) “Any equity security for which unlisted trading privileges on any national securities exchange have been continued or extended pursuant to section 12(f) of the Act and which is not listed and registered on any other such exchange or registered pursuant to section 12(g) of the Act shall be exempt from section 16 of the Act insofar as that section would otherwise apply to any person who is directly or indirectly the beneficial owner of more than 10 percent of such security, unless another equity security of the issuer of such unlisted security is so listed or registered and such beneficial owner is a director or officer of such issuer or directly or indirectly the beneficial owner of more than 10 percent of any such listed security.” Official (d) “Any reference in this section to a security registered pursuant to section 12(g) of the Act shall include, and any reference to a security not so registered shall exclude, any security as to which a registration statement pursuant to such section is at the time required to be effective.” Official caption typography is “Exemption of securities admitted to unlisted trading privileges from sections 13, 14 and 16.” Official source line: “[30 FR 482, Jan. 14, 1965].” Official SECAUTH typography is (Sec. 3, 78 Stat. 565, 15 U.S.C. 78<em>l</em>). Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-17.

Packed 12a-5 / 12d2-1 / 12g-2 exist and are named only. This is not a packed section 16 box. Official 12f-2 is named only.

## 17 CFR 240.12f-2: Extending unlisted trading privileges to a security that is the subject of an initial public offering (checked 2026-11-19)

The packed 12f-4 box and the section-BI prefix that official 12f-2 is named only stay as written. This later box packs the whole current official 12f-2 section; do not rewrite that prefix. Official (a) lets a national securities exchange extend unlisted trading privileges to a subject security when at least one transaction in the subject security has been effected on the national securities exchange upon which the security is listed and the transaction has been reported pursuant to an effective transaction reporting plan. Official typography italicizes <em>General provision.</em>, <em>Definitions.</em>, <em>subject security</em>, and <em>initial public offering commences</em>. Official (b) typography italicizes the letter l in 15 U.S.C. 78<em>l</em>(f). Official (a) has no 78l citation; do not invent one there. Official (c)(1) typography is 15 U.S.C. 78l(f)(1)(G)(i) (Roman l, Roman 1). Official (c)(2) typography is 15 U.S.C. 78l(f)(<em>1</em>)(G)(ii) (Roman l; italic 1). Do not tidy those three official forms into one another. Official CITA is “[60 FR 20896, Apr. 28, 1995, as amended at 65 FR 53565, Sept. 5, 2000; 70 FR 37618, June 29, 2005].” Official API XML has no SECAUTH; do not invent a source line. Official 12f-1 and packed 12f-4 are named only; do not pack 12f-1. Learning only; no issuer or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.12f-2](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.12f-2): Official (a) “<em>General provision.</em> A national securities exchange may extend unlisted trading privileges to a subject security when at least one transaction in the subject security has been effected on the national securities exchange upon which the security is listed and the transaction has been reported pursuant to an effective transaction reporting plan, as defined in § 242.600 of this chapter.” Official (b) “The extension of unlisted trading privileges pursuant to this section shall be subject to all the provisions set forth in Section 12(f) of the Act (15 U.S.C. 78<em>l</em>(f)), as amended, and any rule or regulation promulgated thereunder, or which may be promulgated thereunder while the extension is in effect.” Official (c) “<em>Definitions.</em> For the purposes of this section:” Official (c)(1) “The term <em>subject security</em> shall mean a security that is the subject of an initial public offering, as that term is defined in section 12(f)(1)(G)(i) of the Act (15 U.S.C. 78l(f)(1)(G)(i)), and” Official (c)(2) “An <em>initial public offering commences</em> at such time as is described in section 12(f)(1)(G)(ii) of the Act (15 U.S.C. 78l(f)(<em>1</em>)(G)(ii)).” Official caption typography is “Extending unlisted trading privileges to a security that is the subject of an initial public offering.” Official source line: “[60 FR 20896, Apr. 28, 1995, as amended at 65 FR 53565, Sept. 5, 2000; 70 FR 37618, June 29, 2005]”. Official API XML has no SECAUTH; do not invent a source line. Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-17.

## 17 CFR 240.12f-3: Termination or suspension of unlisted trading privileges (checked 2026-11-19)

Packed 12f-4 and the later 12f-2 box stay as written. This box packs the whole current official 12f-3 section: official (b) says unlisted trading privileges may be suspended or terminated by such exchange in accordance with its rules. Official (a) lets the issuer, any broker or dealer who makes or creates a market for such security, or any other person having a bona fide interest in the question of termination or suspension, apply to the Commission. Official (a)(4) typography is “Names of issuer.” Official (b) typography is “may be suspended or terminated by such exchange in accordance with its rules.” Official CITA is “[20 FR 6702, Sept. 13, 1955, as amended at 44 FR 75135, Dec. 19, 1979; 45 FR 36076, May 29, 1980; 60 FR 20896, Apr. 28, 1995].” Official SECAUTH typography is (Secs. 12(f) and 23, 15 U.S.C. 78<em>l</em> and 78w) (italic l). Official 12f-1 is named only; do not pack 12f-1. Learning only; no issuer or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.12f-3](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.12f-3): Official (a) “The issuer of any security for which unlisted trading privileges on any exchange have been continued or extended, or any broker or dealer who makes or creates a market for such security, or any other person having a bona fide interest in the question of termination or suspension of such unlisted trading privileges, may make application to the Commission for the termination or suspension of such unlisted trading privileges. One duly executed copy of such application shall be filed, and it shall contain the following information:” Official (a)(1) “Name and address of applicant;” Official (a)(2) “A brief statement of the applicant's interest in the question of termination or suspension of such unlisted trading privileges;” Official (a)(3) “Title of security;” Official (a)(4) “Names of issuer;” Official (a)(5) “Amount of such security issued and outstanding (number of shares of stock or principal amount of bonds), stating source of information;” Official (a)(6) “Annual volume of public trading in such security (number of shares of stock or principal amount of bonds) on such exchange for each of the three calendar years immediately preceding the date of such application, and monthly volume of trading in such security for each of the twelve calendar months immediately preceding the date of such application;” Official (a)(7) “Price range on such exchange for each of the twelve calendar months immediately preceding the date of such application; and” Official (a)(8) “A brief statement of the information in the applicant's possession, and the source thereof, with respect to (i) the extent of public trading in such security on such exchange, and (ii) the character of trading in such security on such exchange; and” Official (a)(9) “A brief statement that a copy of the instant application has been mailed, or otherwise personally provided, to the exchange from which the suspension or termination of unlisted trading privileges is sought, and to any other exchange on which such security is listed or traded pursuant to unlisted trading privileges.” Official (b) “Unlisted trading privileges in any security on any national securities exchange may be suspended or terminated by such exchange in accordance with its rules.” Official caption typography is “Termination or suspension of unlisted trading privileges.” Official source line: “[20 FR 6702, Sept. 13, 1955, as amended at 44 FR 75135, Dec. 19, 1979; 45 FR 36076, May 29, 1980; 60 FR 20896, Apr. 28, 1995]”. Official SECAUTH typography is (Secs. 12(f) and 23, 15 U.S.C. 78<em>l</em> and 78w). Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-17.

## 17 CFR 240.14f-1: Change in majority of directors (checked 2026-11-19)

The later Form 8-K Item 5.02 catalog stays as written. This box packs the whole current official 14f-1 section: if, pursuant to any arrangement or understanding with the person or persons acquiring securities in a transaction subject to section 13(d) or 14(d) of the Act, any persons are to be elected or designated as directors of the issuer, otherwise than at a meeting of security holders, and the persons so elected or designated will constitute a majority of the directors of the issuer, then, not less than 10 days prior to the date any such person take office as a director, or such shorter period prior to that date as the Commission may authorize upon a showing of good cause therefor, the issuer shall file with the Commission and transmit to all holders of record who would be entitled to vote at a meeting for election of directors, information substantially equivalent to Items 6 (a), (d) and (e), 7 and 8 of Schedule 14A of Regulation 14A (§ 240.14a-101 of this chapter). Official typography is “not less than 10 days prior to the date any such person take office as a director” (official “take”; do not tidy into “takes”). Official typography is “Items 6 (a), (d) and (e), 7 and 8 of Schedule 14A of Regulation 14A (§ 240.14a-101 of this chapter).” Official typography is “Eight copies of such information shall be filed with the Commission.” Official CITA is “[33 FR 11017, Aug. 2, 1968, as amended at 34 FR 6101, Apr. 4, 1969; 51 FR 42072, Nov. 20, 1986]”. Official API XML has no SECAUTH; do not invent a source line. Official Schedule 14A / 14a-101 and packed 8-K Item 5.02 are named only; do not pack the Schedule 14A catalog. Learning only; no issuer or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.14f-1](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.14f-1): Official text “If, pursuant to any arrangement or understanding with the person or persons acquiring securities in a transaction subject to section 13(d) or 14(d) of the Act, any persons are to be elected or designated as directors of the issuer, otherwise than at a meeting of security holders, and the persons so elected or designated will constitute a majority of the directors of the issuer, then, not less than 10 days prior to the date any such person take office as a director, or such shorter period prior to that date as the Commission may authorize upon a showing of good cause therefor, the issuer shall file with the Commission and transmit to all holders of record of securities of the issuer who would be entitled to vote at a meeting for election of directors, information substantially equivalent to the information which would be required by Items 6 (a), (d) and (e), 7 and 8 of Schedule 14A of Regulation 14A (§ 240.14a-101 of this chapter) to be transmitted if such person or persons were nominees for election as directors at a meeting of such security holders. Eight copies of such information shall be filed with the Commission.” Official caption typography is “Change in majority of directors.” Official source line: “[33 FR 11017, Aug. 2, 1968, as amended at 34 FR 6101, Apr. 4, 1969; 51 FR 42072, Nov. 20, 1986]”. Official API XML has no SECAUTH; do not invent a source line. Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-17.

Official Schedule 14A / 14a-101 and packed 8-K Item 5.02 are named only; do not pack the Schedule 14A catalog.

**Not every Form 8-K is an Item 2.02 earnings release: official items also include a material definitive agreement (1.01), a material cybersecurity incident (1.05), non-reliance on previously issued financial statements (4.02), and departure or appointment of certain officers or directors (5.02); the usual clock is still four business days, but Item 1.05 starts when the registrant determines the incident is material (checked 2026-11-12).** Do not rewrite the already-taught 10-K / 10-Q clocks, 12b-2, 12b-25, or the Form 10-S proposal sentence (“not adopted as of 2026-11-11”). The Item 2.02 earnings-time box in `backtest-traps` stays as written — cross-link it. This section adds a short official item map so a cleaner does not treat every 8-K as earnings, or start the 1.05 clock on discovery rather than on the official determination. No filer or disclosure advice.

Official [Form 8-K](https://www.sec.gov/files/form8-k.pdf) General Instruction B.1: unless otherwise specified, a report “is to be filed or furnished within four business days after occurrence of the event.” “A report pursuant to Item 1.05 is to be filed within four business days after the registrant determines that it has experienced a material cybersecurity incident.” B.2: information in a report furnished under Item 2.02 or Item 7.01 “shall not be deemed to be “filed” for purposes of Section 18,” unless the registrant states otherwise. Official item captions: **Item 1.01** Entry into a Material Definitive Agreement; **Item 1.05** Material Cybersecurity Incidents (describe the material aspects of the nature, scope, and timing of the incident, and the material impact or reasonably likely material impact; Instruction 1: the materiality determination “must be made without unreasonable delay after discovery of the incident”; (c) allows a delay if the Attorney General determines that disclosure poses a substantial risk to national security or public safety and notifies the Commission in writing — that delay is not the usual clock); **Item 4.02** Non-Reliance on Previously Issued Financial Statements or a Related Audit Report or Completed Interim Review; **Item 5.02** Departure of Directors or Certain Officers; Election of Directors; Appointment of Certain Officers; Compensatory Arrangements of Certain Officers (an appointment under 5.02(c) may be delayed until the day the registrant otherwise publicly announces it). Use the official Item 5.02 caption “Certain Officers,” not Instruction G’s “Principal Officers” wording.

Do not teach that every 8-K is earnings, or that Form 12b-25 applies to an 8-K. Do not start the 1.05 clock on discovery, or teach the Attorney General delay as the usual clock. Do not treat Item 2.02 / 7.01 as “filed” for Section 18 in the usual case. Do not flatten Item 5.07 (the four-business-day period begins on the day the meeting ended; paragraph (d) has a different calendar) or other special clocks into the same trigger. Do not build a complete item-by-item compliance engine, and do not apply Instruction G’s asset-backed omissions to an ordinary equity issuer.

**Form 8-K Item 5.07 is the official special clock the already-taught 8-K box told the student not to flatten: the four-business-day period for reporting under Item 5.07, other than 5.07(d), begins to run on the day the meeting ended; preliminary voting results are disclosed, and an amended 8-K discloses the final results within four business days after the final results are known (checked 2026-11-12).** Already-taught Instruction B.1’s four-business-day default, the Item 1.05 determination clock, and the named 1.01 / 2.02 / 4.02 / 5.02 / 7.01 items stay as written. The existing “do not flatten Item 5.07 or other special clocks into the same trigger” line stays as written — this section adds the official clock rather than deleting that warning. Do not bump the Form 10-S “not adopted as of 2026-11-11” sentence only. No issuer or voting advice.

Official [Form 8-K](https://www.sec.gov/files/form8-k.pdf) (SEC 873 (02-25); OMB Number 3235-0060, Expires November 30, 2027) Instruction 1 to Item 5.07: “The four business day period for reporting the event under this Item 5.07, other than with respect to Item 5.07(d), shall begin to run on the day on which the meeting ended. The registrant shall disclose on Form 8-K under this Item 5.07 the preliminary voting results. The registrant shall file an amended report on Form 8-K under this Item 5.07 to disclose the final voting results within four business days after the final voting results are known. However, no preliminary voting results need be disclosed under this Item 5.07 if the registrant has disclosed final voting results on Form 8-K under this Item.” Official 5.07(d) is the excepted clock: no later than one hundred fifty calendar days after the end of the annual or other meeting at which shareholders voted on the frequency of shareholder votes on the compensation of executives as required by section 14A(a)(2), but in no event later than sixty calendar days prior to the deadline for submission of shareholder proposals under §240.14a-8, by amendment to the most recent Form 8-K filed pursuant to 5.07(b), disclose the company’s decision in light of such vote as to how frequently it will include a shareholder vote on executive compensation in its proxy materials until the next required frequency vote. Name the official (d) 150-calendar-day / 60-calendar-day-before-14a-8 dual clock; do not build a Rule 14a-8 engine, and do not invent a four-business-day clock for (d).

Do not flatten 5.07 into already-taught Instruction B.1 or into Item 2.02 / 1.05. Do not start the 5.07 four-business-day clock on a day other than the day the meeting ended.

## 17 CFR 240.14a-9: False or misleading statements (checked 2026-11-12)

Already-taught Form 8-K Item 5.07, and the named Rule 14a-8 foil under official 5.07(d), stay as written. Do not rewrite those already-shipped boxes except to keep the named 14a-8 foil accurate. This section adds official 14a-9 so a student does not treat Item 5.07’s special clock, or already-taught Rule 14a-8, as the false-or-misleading proxy-statement rule, or tidy official typography into packed 10b-5’s “in the light of the circumstances under which they were made.” Learning only; no issuer or voting advice.

[17 CFR 240.14a-9](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.14a-9)(a): “No solicitation subject to this regulation shall be made by means of any proxy statement, form of proxy, notice of meeting or other communication, written or oral, containing any statement which, at the time and in the light of the circumstances under which it is made, is false or misleading with respect to any material fact, or which omits to state any material fact necessary in order to make the statements therein not false or misleading or necessary to correct any statement in any earlier communication with respect to the solicitation of a proxy for the same meeting or subject matter which has become false or misleading.” Official caption is “False or misleading statements.” Official typography is “in the light of the circumstances under which it is made” (singular “it is made”). Do not tidy that into packed 10b-5’s “in the light of the circumstances under which they were made.” Official (a) is “notice of meeting or other communication, written or oral.” Cite 17 CFR 240.14a-9. Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` is **2026-11-09**. Official (b) (filing with or examination by the Commission is not a finding of accuracy or a merits approval) and official (c) (false or misleading statements in a registrant's proxy materials, a Schedule 14N notice, or a related communication by a nominee, nominating shareholder, or nominating shareholder group) exist; official Note examples a–d exist. Name official (b), official (c), and the Note; do not build official (c) or the Note catalog. Do not flatten 14a-9 into already-taught Rule 14a-8 or Form 8-K Item 5.07. Do not pack a scienter / materiality / judicial-opinion catalog.

## 17 CFR 240.14a-14: Modified or superseded documents (checked 2026-11-19)

Packed 14a-9, packed 14a-10, and packed 12b-23 stay as written. This box is a different rule: official (a) “Any statement contained in a document incorporated or deemed to be incorporated by reference shall be deemed to be modified or superseded, for purposes of the proxy statement, to the extent that a statement contained in the proxy statement or in any other subsequently filed document that also is or is deemed to be incorporated by reference modifies or replaces such statement.” Official (b) “The modifying or superseding statement may, but need not, state it has modified or superseded a prior statement or include any other information set forth in the document that is not so modified or superseded. The making of a modifying or superseding statement shall not be deemed an admission that the modified or superseded statement, when made, constituted an untrue statement of a material fact, an omission to state a material fact necessary to make a statement not misleading, or the employment of a manipulative, deceptive, or fraudulent device, contrivance, scheme, transaction, act, practice, course of business or artifice to defraud, as those terms are used in the Securities Act of 1933, the Securities Exchange Act of 1934 (“the Act”), the Investment Company Act of 1940, or the rules and regulations thereunder.” Official (c) “Any statement so modified shall not be deemed in its unmodified form to constitute part of the proxy statement for purposes of the Act. Any statement so superseded shall not be deemed to constitute a part of the proxy statement for purposes of the Act.” Official typography in (c) is “constitute part” (modified, no “a”) versus “constitute a part” (superseded). Official typography is “may, but need not, state it has modified or superseded a prior statement.” Official typography is “the Securities Exchange Act of 1934 (“the Act”)” with official typographic quotes around “the Act.” Official caption typography is “Modified or superseded documents.” Official source line: “[52 FR 21936, June 10, 1987, as amended at 73 FR 17814, Apr. 1, 2008].” Do not flatten this box into packed 14a-9, packed 14a-10, or packed 12b-23. Learning only; no issuer, filer, or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.14a-14](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.14a-14): official (a) “Any statement contained in a document incorporated or deemed to be incorporated by reference shall be deemed to be modified or superseded, for purposes of the proxy statement, to the extent that a statement contained in the proxy statement or in any other subsequently filed document that also is or is deemed to be incorporated by reference modifies or replaces such statement.” Official (b) “The modifying or superseding statement may, but need not, state it has modified or superseded a prior statement or include any other information set forth in the document that is not so modified or superseded. The making of a modifying or superseding statement shall not be deemed an admission that the modified or superseded statement, when made, constituted an untrue statement of a material fact, an omission to state a material fact necessary to make a statement not misleading, or the employment of a manipulative, deceptive, or fraudulent device, contrivance, scheme, transaction, act, practice, course of business or artifice to defraud, as those terms are used in the Securities Act of 1933, the Securities Exchange Act of 1934 (“the Act”), the Investment Company Act of 1940, or the rules and regulations thereunder.” Official (c) “Any statement so modified shall not be deemed in its unmodified form to constitute part of the proxy statement for purposes of the Act. Any statement so superseded shall not be deemed to constitute a part of the proxy statement for purposes of the Act.” Official caption typography is “Modified or superseded documents.” Official typography in (c) is “constitute part” (modified, no “a”) versus “constitute a part” (superseded). Official typography is “may, but need not, state it has modified or superseded a prior statement.” Official typography is “the Securities Exchange Act of 1934 (“the Act”).” Official source line: “[52 FR 21936, June 10, 1987, as amended at 73 FR 17814, Apr. 1, 2008].” Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-17.

Packed 14a-9, packed 14a-10, and packed 12b-23 exist and are named only.

## 17 CFR 240.14a-5: Presentation of information in proxy statement (checked 2026-11-19)

Packed 14a-3 / 14a-9 / 14a-10 / 14a-14 stay as written. This box packs the whole current official 14a-5 section. Official (e) requires the proxy statement to disclose, under an appropriate caption, the official (e)(1)–(4) dates, including the shareholder-proposal deadline calculated in the manner provided in § 240.14a-8(e)(Question 5). Official typography is “§ 240.14a-8(e)(Question 5).” Official typography italicizes <em>et seq.</em> Official (f) typography is “more than 30 calendar days.” If the date of the next annual meeting is subsequently advanced or delayed by more than 30 calendar days, the registrant shall, in a timely manner, inform shareholders of such change, and the new dates referred to in paragraphs (e)(1) and (e)(2), by including a notice, under Item 5, in its earliest possible quarterly report on Form 10-Q. Official CITA is “[17 FR 11432, Dec. 18, 1952, as amended at 36 FR 8935, May 15, 1971; 37 FR 23179, Oct. 31, 1972; 44 FR 68770, Nov. 29, 1979; 51 FR 42061, Nov. 20, 1986; 61 FR 24656, May 15, 1996; 63 FR 29118, May 28, 1998; 63 FR 46881, Sept. 3, 1998; 73 FR 977, Jan. 4, 2008; 75 FR 56782, Sept. 16, 2010; 86 FR 68379, Dec. 1, 2021; 90 FR 9689, Feb. 18, 2025].” Do not mix 977 into 3b-6’s 973. Official API XML has no SECAUTH; do not invent a source line. Official 14a-8 / 14a-4 / 14a-19 are named only; do not pack those sections, and do not rewrite packed 14a-3 / 14a-9 / 14a-10 / 14a-14. Learning only; no issuer or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.14a-5](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.14a-5): Official (a) “The information included in the proxy statement shall be clearly presented and the statements made shall be divided into groups according to subject matter and the various groups of statements shall be preceded by appropriate headings. The order of items and sub-items in the schedule need not be followed. Where practicable and appropriate, the information shall be presented in tabular form. All amounts shall be stated in figures. Information required by more than one applicable item need not be repeated. No statement need be made in response to any item or sub-item which is inapplicable.” Official (b) “Any information required to be included in the proxy statement as to terms of securities or other subject matter which from a standpoint of practical necessity must be determined in the future may be stated in terms of present knowledge and intention. To the extent practicable, the authority to be conferred concerning each such matter shall be confined within limits reasonably related to the need for discretionary authority. Subject to the foregoing, information which is not known to the persons on whose behalf the solicitation is to be made and which it is not reasonably within the power of such persons to ascertain or procure may be omitted, if a brief statement of the circumstances rendering such information unavailable is made.” Official (c) “Any information contained in any other proxy soliciting material which has been or will be furnished to each person solicited in connection with the same meeting or subject matter may be omitted from the proxy statement, if a clear reference is made to the particular document containing such information.” Official (d)(1) “All printed proxy statements shall be in roman type at least as large and as legible as 10-point modern type, except that to the extent necessary for convenient presentation financial statements and other tabular data, but not the notes thereto, may be in roman type at least as large and as legible as 8-point modern type. All such type shall be leaded at least 2 points.” Official (d)(2) “Where a proxy statement is delivered through an electronic medium, issuers may satisfy legibility requirements applicable to printed documents, such as type size and font, by presenting all required information in a format readily communicated to investors.” Official (e) “All proxy statements shall disclose, under an appropriate caption, the following dates:” Official (e)(1) “The deadline for submitting shareholder proposals for inclusion in the registrant's proxy statement and form of proxy for the registrant's next annual meeting, calculated in the manner provided in § 240.14a-8(e)(Question 5);” Official (e)(2) “The date after which notice of a shareholder proposal submitted outside the processes of § 240.14a-8 is considered untimely, either calculated in the manner provided by § 240.14a-4(c)(1) or as established by the registrant's advance notice provision, if any, authorized by applicable state law;” Official (e)(3) “The deadline for submitting nominees for inclusion in the registrant's proxy statement and form of proxy pursuant to an applicable state or foreign law provision or a registrant's governing documents as they relate to the inclusion of shareholder director nominees in the registrant's proxy materials for the registrant's next annual meeting of shareholders; and” Official (e)(4) “The deadline for providing notice of a solicitation of proxies in support of director nominees other than the registrant's nominees pursuant to § 240.14a-19 for the registrant's next annual meeting unless the registrant is an investment company registered under the Investment Company Act of 1940 (15 U.S.C. 80a-1 <em>et seq.</em>) or a business development company as defined by section 2(a)(48) of the Investment Company Act of 1940 (15 U.S.C. 80a-2(a)(48)).” Official (f) “If the date of the next annual meeting is subsequently advanced or delayed by more than 30 calendar days from the date of the annual meeting to which the proxy statement relates, the registrant shall, in a timely manner, inform shareholders of such change, and the new dates referred to in paragraphs (e)(1) and (e)(2) of this section, by including a notice, under Item 5, in its earliest possible quarterly report on Form 10-Q (§ 249.308a of this chapter), or, in the case of investment companies, in a shareholder report under § 270.30d-1 of this chapter under the Investment Company Act of 1940, or, if impracticable, any means reasonably calculated to inform shareholders.” Official caption typography is “Presentation of information in proxy statement.” Official source line: “[17 FR 11432, Dec. 18, 1952, as amended at 36 FR 8935, May 15, 1971; 37 FR 23179, Oct. 31, 1972; 44 FR 68770, Nov. 29, 1979; 51 FR 42061, Nov. 20, 1986; 61 FR 24656, May 15, 1996; 63 FR 29118, May 28, 1998; 63 FR 46881, Sept. 3, 1998; 73 FR 977, Jan. 4, 2008; 75 FR 56782, Sept. 16, 2010; 86 FR 68379, Dec. 1, 2021; 90 FR 9689, Feb. 18, 2025]”. Official API XML has no SECAUTH; do not invent a source line. Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-17.
