For example, unless expressly permitted or required, disclosure must not be incorporated by reference from a second document if that second document incorporates information pertinent to such disclosure by reference to a third document.

## 17 CFR 240.13a-11: Current reports on Form 8-K (checked 2026-11-19)

Already-taught Form 8-K item clocks / Item 5.07, already-taught Form 6-K promptly-furnished clocks, and packed 14e-8 stay as written. This box is a different rule: official 13a-11(a) requires a current report on Form 8-K “within the period specified in that form unless substantially the same information as that required by Form 8-K has been previously reported by the registrant.” Do not invent a numeric clock inside 13a-11 itself. Official (b) exceptions include foreign private issuers on Form 6-K pursuant to 13a-16 — name already-taught 6-K clocks only — plus ADR issuers, foreign governments, and investment companies required to file under § 270.30a-1, “except where such an investment company is required to file notice of a blackout period pursuant to § 245.104 of this chapter.” Official (c) item typography must be copied exactly: “Item 1.01, 1.02, 1.05, 2.03, 2.04, 2.05, 2.06, 4.02(a), 5.02(e), or 6.03.” Do not invent Item 2.02, Item 5.07, or a bare 4.02 / 5.02 into that carve-out. Official typography is that no such failure “shall be deemed to be a violation of 15 U.S.C. 78j(b) and § 240.10b-5.” Name official 13a-1 only; do not pack 13a-1 as a second core. Do not flatten this rule into already-taught Form 8-K item clocks / Item 5.07 / packed 14e-8. Learning only; no issuer, filer, or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.13a-11](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.13a-11): official (a) “Except as provided in paragraph (b) of this section, every registrant subject to § 240.13a-1 shall file a current report on Form 8-K within the period specified in that form unless substantially the same information as that required by Form 8-K has been previously reported by the registrant.” Official (b) “This section shall not apply to foreign governments, foreign private issuers required to make reports on Form 6-K (§ 249.306 of this chapter) pursuant to § 240.13a-16, issuers of American Depositary Receipts for securities of any foreign issuer, or investment companies required to file reports pursuant to § 270.30a-1 of this chapter under the Investment Company Act of 1940, except where such an investment company is required to file notice of a blackout period pursuant to § 245.104 of this chapter.” Official (c) “No failure to file a report on Form 8-K that is required solely pursuant to Item 1.01, 1.02, 1.05, 2.03, 2.04, 2.05, 2.06, 4.02(a), 5.02(e), or 6.03 of Form 8-K shall be deemed to be a violation of 15 U.S.C. 78j(b) and § 240.10b-5.” Official caption typography is “Current reports on Form 8-K (§ 249.308 of this chapter).” Official source line: “[42 FR 4428, Jan. 25, 1977, as amended at 50 FR 27939, July 9, 1985; 68 FR 4355, Jan. 28, 2003; 69 FR 15618, Mar. 25, 2004; 70 FR 1621, Jan. 7, 2005; 71 FR 53260, Sept. 8, 2006; 75 FR 56780, Sept. 16, 2010; 81 FR 82020, Nov. 18, 2016; 88 FR 51493, Aug. 4, 2023; 90 FR 9689, Feb. 18, 2025].” Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-17.

## 17 CFR 240.12b-21: Information unknown or not available (checked 2026-11-19)

Packed 12b-20 / 12b-23 / 12b-4 stay as written. This box is a different rule: required information “need be given only insofar as it is known or reasonably available to the registrant.” Official caption typography is “Information unknown or not available.” — the caption does not say “reasonably,” though the official opening uses “known or reasonably available.” Official (a) still requires such information as the registrant possesses or can acquire without unreasonable effort or expense, “together with the sources thereof.” Official (b) requires a statement either showing that unreasonable effort or expense would be involved or indicating the absence of any affiliation with the person within whose knowledge the information rests and stating the result of a request made to such person for the information. The official API XML has no CITA element; do not invent a source line. Do not flatten this rule into packed 12b-20 / 12b-23 / 12b-4. Learning only; no issuer, filer, or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.12b-21](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.12b-21): opening “Information required need be given only insofar as it is known or reasonably available to the registrant. If any required information is unknown and not reasonably available to the registrant, either because the obtaining thereof would involve unreasonable effort or expense, or because it rests peculiarly within the knowledge of another person not affiliated with the registrant, the information may be omitted, subject to the following conditions.” Official (a) “The registrant shall give such information on the subject as it possesses or can acquire without unreasonable effort or expense, together with the sources thereof.” Official (b) “The registrant shall include a statement either showing that unreasonable effort or expense would be involved or indicating the absence of any affiliation with the person within whose knowledge the information rests and stating the result of a request made to such person for the information.” Official caption typography is “Information unknown or not available.” The official API XML has no CITA element; this lesson does not invent a source line. Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-17.

Packed 12b-20 / 12b-23 / 12b-4 exist and are named only.

## 17 CFR 240.13a-13: Quarterly reports on Form 10-Q (checked 2026-11-19)

Already-taught Form 10-Q clocks, packed 13a-11, and the new 13a-1 box stay as written. This box is a different rule: official 13a-13(a) requires a quarterly report on Form 10-Q (§ 249.308a of this chapter, not § 249.308) “within the period specified in General Instruction A.1. to that form” for “each of the first three quarters of each fiscal year of the issuer.” Do not invent a fourth-quarter 10-Q. The only numeric clock inside the rule is the first-report alternative: “within 45 days after the effective date of the registration statement or on or before the date on which such report would have been required to be filed if the issuer has been required to file reports on Form 10-Q as of its last fiscal quarter, whichever is later.” Official typography is “has been required,” not “had been required.” Official (b) exceptions include investment companies required to file pursuant to § 270.30a-1, foreign private issuers pursuant to § 240.13a-16, and asset-backed issuers pursuant to § 240.13a-17 — name already-taught 6-K clocks and leftover 13a-17 only; do not build an ABS or mining-company engine out of official (c). Official (d) typography is that Part I financial information shall not be deemed to be “filed” for the purpose of Section 18 of the Act, but shall be subject to all other provisions of the Act, with typographic U+201C / U+201D around “filed.” Name official 15d-13 / 13a-17 only; do not invent a 15d-13 form number, and do not pack them as second cores. Do not flatten this rule into already-taught Form 10-Q clocks, packed 13a-11, or the new 13a-1 box. Learning only; no issuer, filer, or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.13a-13](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.13a-13): official (a) “Except as provided in paragraphs (b) and (c) of this section, every issuer that has securities registered pursuant to section 12 of the Act and is required to file annual reports pursuant to section 13 of the Act, and has filed or intends to file such reports on Form 10-K (§ 249.310 of this chapter), shall file a quarterly report on Form 10-Q (§ 249.308a of this chapter) within the period specified in General Instruction A.1. to that form for each of the first three quarters of each fiscal year of the issuer, commencing with the first fiscal quarter following the most recent fiscal year for which full financial statements were included in the registration statement, or, if the registration statement included financial statements for an interim period subsequent to the most recent fiscal year end meeting the requirements of Article 10 of Regulation S-X and Rule 8-03 of Regulation S-X for smaller reporting companies, for the first fiscal quarter subsequent to the quarter reported upon in the registration statement. The first quarterly report of the issuer shall be filed either within 45 days after the effective date of the registration statement or on or before the date on which such report would have been required to be filed if the issuer has been required to file reports on Form 10-Q as of its last fiscal quarter, whichever is later.” Official (b) “The provisions of this rule shall not apply to the following issuers:” Official (b)(1) “Investment companies required to file reports pursuant to § 270.30a-1;” Official (b)(2) “Foreign private issuers required to file reports pursuant to § 240.13a-16; and” Official (b)(3) “Asset-backed issuers required to file reports pursuant to § 240.13a-17.” Official (c) “Part I of the quarterly reports on Form 10-Q need not be filed by:” Official (c)(1) “Mutual life insurance companies; or” Official (c)(2) “Mining companies not in the production stage but engaged primarily in the exploration for the development of mineral deposits other than oil, gas or coal, if all of the following conditions are met:” Official (c)(2)(i) “The registrant has not been in production during the current fiscal year or the two years immediately prior thereto; except that being in production for an aggregate period of not more than eight months over the three-year period shall not be a violation of this condition.” Official (c)(2)(ii) “Receipts from the sale of mineral products or from the operations of mineral producing properties by the registrant and its subsidiaries combined have not exceeded $500,000 in any of the most recent six years and have not aggregated more than $1,500,000 in the most recent six fiscal years.” Official (d) “Notwithstanding the foregoing provisions of this section, the financial information required by Part I of Form 10-Q shall not be deemed to be “filed” for the purpose of Section 18 of the Act or otherwise subject to the liabilities of that section of the Act, but shall be subject to all other provisions of the Act.” Official caption typography is “Quarterly reports on Form 10-Q (§ 249.308a of this chapter).” Official source line: “[42 FR 24064, May 12, 1977, as amended at 48 FR 19877, May 3, 1983; ## 17 CFR 240.15d-1: Requirement of annual reports (checked 2026-11-19)

Packed 13a-1 stays as written. This box is a different rule: every registrant under the Securities Act of 1933 shall file an annual report “on the appropriate form authorized or prescribed therefor,” for the fiscal year in which the registration statement became effective and for each fiscal year thereafter, unless exempt by section 15(d) of the Act or rules thereunder. Official typography is “Annual reports shall be filed within the period specified in the appropriate report form.” Do not invent a numeric clock, and do not tidy this into packed 13a-1’s “the appropriate form.” Official typography is “therefor”; do not tidy it into “therefore.” Official caption typography is singular “Requirement of annual reports.” — packed 13a-1 is plural “Requirements of annual reports.” Do not rewrite the packed 13a-1 box or tidy captions / CITA into 13a-1. Learning only; no issuer, filer, or investment advice.

Annual reports shall be filed within the period specified in the appropriate report form.” Official caption typography is “Requirement of annual reports.” Official source line: “[47 FR 17052, Apr. 21, 1982, as amended at 61 FR 49960, Sept. 24, 1996].” Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-17.

## SEC XBRL data: the `filed` date is your as-of date (checked 2026-11-11)

**A free, official source of fundamentals.** The SEC serves the financial-statement numbers that companies tag in XBRL (a machine-readable format inside 10-Q, 10-K, 8-K and similar filings) as JSON on `data.sec.gov`, with no API key ([SEC: EDGAR APIs](https://www.sec.gov/search-filings/edgar-application-programming-interfaces)). `companyconcept` returns one company and one tag (for example `us-gaap/NetIncomeLoss`); `companyfacts` returns all tags for one company; `frames` returns one value per company for a calendar period. Fair access: no more than **10 requests per second** in total, and scripts should identify themselves; the SEC may block excessive or unidentified automated traffic ([SEC developer resources](https://www.sec.gov/about/developer-resources)).

**Every fact carries the filing that reported it.** Each record has `start`, `end`, `val`, `accn` (accession number), `fy`, `fp`, `form` and `filed`. The same period appears again in later filings, as comparatives in next year's 10-K, and in amendments. A real example ([Apple, NetIncomeLoss](https://data.sec.gov/api/xbrl/companyconcept/CIK0000320193/us-gaap/NetIncomeLoss.json), read 2026-11-11): for the fiscal year 2008-09-28 to 2009-09-26, the 10-K filed 2009-10-27 reported **5,704 million**; a 10-K/A filed 2010-01-25 reported **8,235 million** for the same year, and the 10-Ks of 2010 and 2011 repeat 8,235. Three traps:

1. **Pick by `filed`.** A decision on 2009-11-02 may only use 5,704 million. Taking the latest value is look-ahead.
2. **`frames` returns the last filed value.** The SEC says the frames API aggregates the fact "that is last filed" for each company and period, so its CY2009 value is 8,235. Fine for today's snapshot, wrong for a historical panel.
3. **`fy` and `fp` describe the filing, not the period.** The same FY2009 number appears with `fy=2010` and `fy=2011`. Identify periods by `start`/`end`.

**Backtest rule:** keep every record; for each decision date, take the latest record for that exact period with `filed` before the date (store `accn` so you can trace it); act no earlier than the next session after acceptance (see the EDGAR time rule above; `filed` is a date only, so the acceptance time comes from the filing index).

**Lab:** `examples/p2/17_xbrl_pit_10b51_toy.py` (the four records are copied from the SEC JSON; no network; sections B–C belong to the insider section below):

```text
=== A. one period, four filings: pick by 'filed', not by 'fy' or 'frame' ===
  10-K   filed 2009-10-27 fy=2009 fp=FY val=5,704m frame=-
  10-K/A filed 2010-01-25 fy=2009 fp=FY val=8,235m frame=-
  10-K   filed 2010-10-27 fy=2010 fp=FY val=8,235m frame=-
  10-K   filed 2011-10-26 fy=2011 fp=FY val=8,235m frame=CY2009
decision 2009-10-20: not yet filed
decision 2009-11-02: 5704m from 10-K filed 2009-10-27
decision 2010-02-01: 8235m from 10-K/A filed 2010-01-25
frames API value for CY2009 = 8,235m (the last filed); using it on 2009-11-02 = look-ahead of the amendment
'fy' describes the FILING (the 2011 10-K repeats FY2009 as a comparative); identify periods by start/end.
=== B. Rule 10b5-1 cooling-off (directors/officers: later of 90 days or 2 business days after the 10-Q/10-K, max 120) ===
  officer, adopted 2026-02-10, Q1 10-Q filed 2026-05-06: cooling-off ends 2026-05-11
  officer, adopted 2026-03-25, Q1 10-Q filed 2026-05-06: cooling-off ends 2026-06-23
  officer, adopted 2026-01-05, Q1 10-Q filed 2026-05-08: cooling-off ends 2026-05-05
  other insider (not D&O), adopted 2026-02-10: cooling-off ends 2026-03-12
=== C. the Form 4 plan check box exists only for filings from 2023-04-01 ===
  Form 4 filed 2022-11-15: unknown (field did not exist; not the same as 'not a plan')
  Form 4 filed 2023-05-02: plan trade
  Form 4 filed 2024-08-20: not marked as a plan trade
```

## Insider and large-holder filings: when are they "known"? (checked 2026-11-10)

Directors, officers and holders of more than 10% of a company's stock ("insiders") report their trades in that company's stock on Form 4 ([investor.gov](https://www.investor.gov/introduction-investing/investing-basics/glossary/forms-3-4-and-5)). The form is due before the end of the second business day after the date of execution; for certain planned transactions the clock starts on the deemed execution date under 16a-3(g)(4) ([17 CFR 240.16a-3](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.16a-3)). So a Friday trade may not appear until Tuesday. An investor who goes above 5% generally files Schedule 13D within five business days and amends it within two business days after a material change. Qualifying institutions may file the shorter Schedule 13G instead, for example within 45 days after quarter-end; 13G deadlines differ by type of filer ([17 CFR 240.13d-1](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.13d-1); [SEC 2023-219](https://www.sec.gov/newsroom/press-releases/2023-219)).

In a backtest, a signal becomes usable once EDGAR has accepted the filing, not on the transaction date printed in the form. Using the transaction date usually looks up to two business days into the future; it can be longer for deemed execution dates (16a-3(g)(4)) or late filings. A filing accepted at 21:40 ET is on time for that day, but the regular session already closed at 16:00, so a daily backtest can act at the next session at the earliest.

One 2026 change: since 2026-03-18, directors and officers of foreign private issuers that have equity securities registered under Section 12 must also file Form 4, but the SEC has granted exemptive orders for several jurisdictions, including Canada, the European Economic Area, the UK and Australia ([SEC](https://www.sec.gov/about/divisions-offices/division-corporation-finance/holding-foreign-insiders-accountable-act-section-16a-reporting-requirements)); the list may change, so read the SEC page rather than copying it. For these companies, "no Form 4 before 2026-03-18" does not mean insiders did not trade; they simply did not have to report. After that date, check the exemption list first.

**Rule 10b5-1 plans: a flag on Form 4 since 2023-04-01.** Insiders often sell under a pre-arranged trading plan adopted under Rule 10b5-1, which gives an affirmative defense against insider-trading liability if its conditions are met. Since the 2022 amendments, a director or officer cannot trade under a new plan until a **cooling-off period** ends: the later of **90 days** after adoption or **two business days** after the 10-Q/10-K for the fiscal quarter of adoption is disclosed, but never more than **120 days**; for other persons (not the company) it is **30 days** ([17 CFR 240.10b5-1(c)(1)(ii)(B)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.10b5-1)). Form 4 has a check box for transactions under a plan intended to satisfy Rule 10b5-1(c), with the plan's adoption date in the explanation ([Form 4, Instruction 10](https://www.sec.gov/files/form4.pdf)); it is required for Forms 4 and 5 filed on or after **2023-04-01** ([SEC press release 2022-222](https://www.sec.gov/newsroom/press-releases/2022-222)). For data work: (1) the box lets you separate planned trades from others; (2) before 2023-04-01 the field does not exist, so "box not checked" in older filings means **unknown**, not "discretionary"; (3) a planned sale was decided at least the cooling-off period earlier. This is about labelling data correctly; this course gives no insider-trading signal. Lab: sections B–C of `17_xbrl_pit_10b51_toy.py` (output in the XBRL section above).

**Form 4 transaction codes are not all open-market trades; Form 3 is the initial statement and Form 5 is the annual catch-up (checked 2026-11-12).** The Form 4 *deadline* (before the end of the second business day after execution, including the deemed-execution branch) is already taught above. Teach the official code table so a backtest does not treat every Form 4 acquisition as an open-market buy. [Form 4, General Instruction 8](https://www.sec.gov/files/form4.pdf): “Use the code that most appropriately describes the transaction.”

- **General:** **P** — “Open market or private purchase of non-derivative or derivative security”; **S** — “Open market or private sale of non-derivative or derivative security.” **V** in the appropriate column marks a transaction “voluntarily reported earlier than required” (otherwise leave that column blank). Do not confuse this optional earlier-reporting flag with a purchase/sale code.
- **Rule 16b-3:** **A** — “Grant, award or other acquisition pursuant to Rule 16b-3(d)”; **D** — “Disposition to the issuer of issuer equity securities pursuant to Rule 16b-3(e)”; **F** — “Payment of exercise price or tax liability by delivering or withholding securities incident to the receipt, exercise or vesting of a security issued in accordance with Rule 16b-3”; **I** — a discretionary transaction under 16b-3(f); **M** — “Exercise or conversion of derivative security exempted pursuant to Rule 16b-3.”
- **Other listed codes include** C / E / H / O / X (derivative, except 16b-3-exempt exercises/conversions), **G** — “Bona fide gift,” L, W, Z, **J** — “Other acquisition or disposition (describe transaction),” **K** used *in addition* for an equity swap or similar instrument (for example “S/K” or “P/K”), and **U** — a tender in a change-of-control transaction.

**Form 3 / Form 5 clocks.** [15 U.S.C. 78p(a)(2)(B)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/15/78p) requires the initial statement “within 10 days after he or she becomes such beneficial owner, director, or officer, or within such shorter time as the Commission may establish by rule.” This lesson does **not** invent a shorter Commission deadline; none was found in [17 CFR 240.16a-3](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/17/240.16a-3). 16a-3(a) assigns that initial statement to Form 3, changes to Form 4, and annual statements to Form 5. [16a-3(f)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/17/240.16a-3): a Form 5 “shall be filed within 45 days after the issuer's fiscal year end” and reports specified holdings and transactions not previously reported; “no Form 5 shall be required where all transactions otherwise required to be reported on the Form 5 have been reported before the due date.” Those “10 days” and “45 days” are as written, not rewritten here as business days. If that calendar last day falls on a Saturday, Sunday, or holiday, Rule 0-3 (already taught in the filing-lag section) lets the filing be made on the next business day — the same Rule 0-3 roll already shown for the 13G 45-day clock in section C. In the lab, FYE 2025-12-31 plus 45 calendar days is 2026-02-14 (Saturday); the next Monday, 2026-02-16, is Washington’s Birthday on the [OPM 2026 Holiday Schedule](https://www.opm.gov/policy-data-oversight/pay-leave/federal-holidays/#url=2026), so the filing may be made on 2026-02-17. Do not add Form 13H to this public Section 16 / 13D / 13G box (already taught as confidential). This course gives no insider-trading signal.

**A Schedule 13G filer who loses 13G eligibility must switch to Schedule 13D within five business days; beneficial ownership is voting and/or investment power, not merely shares of record (checked 2026-11-12).** The initial 13D / 13G *filing lags* are already taught above and are not rewritten here. Teach the **switch** and the **13d-3** test so a backtest does not treat a still-outstanding 13G as proof of a still-passive 5% holder, or treat 4.9% of record as the whole 13(d)/13(g) position.

[17 CFR 240.13d-1(e)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/17/240.13d-1): a person that has reported more than 5% on Schedule 13G under paragraph (b) or (c), or is required to report the acquisition but has not yet filed, “shall immediately become subject to paragraph (a) … and shall file a statement on Schedule 13D … **within five business days** if, and shall remain subject to those requirements for so long as, the person: (i) Has acquired or holds the securities with a purpose or effect of changing or influencing control of the issuer, … and (ii) Is at that time the beneficial owner of more than five percent ….” From the time of that purpose or effect “until the expiration of the **tenth day from the date of the filing** of the Schedule 13D,” that person “shall not: (i) Vote or direct the voting of the securities described therein; or (ii) Acquire an additional beneficial ownership interest in any equity securities of the issuer …, nor of any person controlling the issuer.”

[13d-1(f)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/17/240.13d-1): persons reporting on 13G **pursuant to paragraph (c)** shall file a 13D “**within five business days after the date on which the person's beneficial ownership equals or exceeds 20 percent** of the class,” with the same no-vote / no-additional-acquisition standstill from the 20% acquisition until the tenth day from the 13D filing. Do **not** copy that 20% cap onto every 13G filer; it is the paragraph (c) path. [13d-1(g)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/17/240.13d-1): a paragraph (b) 13G filer who “ceases to be a person specified in paragraph (b)(1)(ii)” or “determines that it no longer has acquired or holds the securities in the ordinary course of business” shall file a 13D or an amendment to 13G, as applicable, “**within five business days thereafter**,” if still above 5%. [13d-1(i)(2)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/17/240.13d-1): “business day means any day, other than Saturday, Sunday, or a Federal holiday, from 12 a.m. to 11:59 p.m., Eastern Time.” In the lab, a paragraph (b) filer who loses eligibility on 2026-10-05 has a fifth business day of 2026-10-13, because 2026-10-12 is Columbus Day on that same OPM 2026 schedule.

[17 CFR 240.13d-3(a)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/17/240.13d-3): a beneficial owner “includes any person who, directly or indirectly, through any contract, arrangement, understanding, relationship, or otherwise has or shares: (1) Voting power … and/or, (2) Investment power ….” [13d-3(c)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/17/240.13d-3): all securities of the same class beneficially owned “shall be aggregated.” [13d-3(d)(1)(i)](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/17/240.13d-3): a person “shall be deemed to be the beneficial owner of a security … if that person has the right to acquire beneficial ownership of such security … **within sixty days**,” including by option, warrant, right, or conversion; but if that right is acquired “with the purpose or effect of changing or influencing the control of the issuer,” the person is deemed the beneficial owner of the underlying “**immediately** upon such acquisition.” Securities “not outstanding which are subject to such options, warrants, rights or conversion privileges shall be deemed to be outstanding for the purpose of computing the percentage … owned by such person but shall not be deemed to be outstanding for the purpose of computing the percentage of the class by any other person.”

Do not revive the pre-2023 “10 calendar days” *filing* clock. The current filing clock on a switch is five business days; “tenth day” is the *standstill* after the 13D is filed, running from the triggering purpose or 20% acquisition until the expiration of the tenth day from the date of that filing. Do not treat shares of record as the 13(d)/13(g) position. This lesson teaches the official test and the 60-day / immediate-control branches; it does not build a full beneficial-ownership compliance engine. No activism or product recommendation.

**Rule 13d-2: a 1% acquisition or disposition is deemed material for a Schedule 13D amendment due within two business days; a 13G generally amends within 45 days after calendar quarter-end if there are material changes, not on that 13D clock (checked 2026-11-12).** The already-taught sentence that a 13D is amended within two business days after a material change, the initial 13D / 13G lags, the switch clocks, and the Form 10-S proposal sentence stay as written. This section adds the official 1% test and the official 13G amendment clocks, so a cleaner does not treat a stale 13G as current-quarter ownership, skip 13D amendments under 1% as automatically immaterial, or copy the 13D 1% / two-business-day clock onto every 13G. No filer or disclosure advice.

[17 CFR 240.13d-2(a)](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.13d-2): if any material change occurs in the facts set forth in the Schedule 13D, the person “shall file or cause to be filed with the Commission an amendment disclosing that change within two business days after the date of such change.” “An acquisition or disposition of beneficial ownership of securities in an amount equal to one percent or more of the class of securities shall be deemed “material” for purposes of this section; acquisitions or dispositions of less than those amounts may be material, depending upon the facts and circumstances.” 13d-2(b): any person who has filed a Schedule 13G pursuant to 13d-1(b), (c), or (d) “shall amend the statement within 45 days after the end of each calendar quarter if, as of the end of the calendar quarter, there are any material changes in the information reported in the previous filing on that Schedule,” provided a paragraph (b) or (c) filer continues to meet those requirements; “an amendment need not be filed with respect to a change in the percent of the class outstanding previously reported if the change results solely from a change in the aggregate number of securities outstanding.” Once an amendment has been filed reflecting beneficial ownership of five percent or less, no additional filings are required unless the person thereafter becomes the beneficial owner of more than five percent and is required to file under 13d-1. 13d-2(c) (a person relying on 13d-1(b)): in addition to (b), file a 13G amendment within five business days after the end of the first month in which beneficial ownership, computed as of the last day of the month, exceeds 10%, and thereafter within five business days after month-end when it increases or decreases by more than five percent. 13d-2(d) (a person relying on 13d-1(c)): in addition to (b), file a 13G amendment within two business days after acquiring, directly or indirectly, greater than 10 percent, and thereafter within two business days after increasing or decreasing beneficial ownership by more than five percent.

Do not apply the 13D 1% / two-business-day clock to every 13G. Do not treat a change solely from a change in shares outstanding as a required 13G percent amendment. Do not treat every sub-1% 13D change as immaterial. Do not build a full amendment-eligibility engine; name the official (c) and (d) branches and point to the rule. Do not reopen 13d-1(e)/(f)/(g), 13d-3, or the Form 5 2026-02-17 / 13d-1(g) 2026-10-13 rows.

## 17 CFR 240.13d-4: Disclaimer of beneficial ownership (checked 2026-11-19)

Packed 13d-1 / 13d-2 / 13d-3 and packed 12b-22 stay as written. This box is a different rule: any person may expressly declare in any statement filed that the filing of such statement shall not be construed as an admission that such person is, for the purposes of sections 13(d) or 13(g) of the Act, the beneficial owner of any securities covered by the statement. Official text “Any person may expressly declare in any statement filed that the filing of such statement shall not be construed as an admission that such person is, for the purposes of sections 13(d) or 13(g) of the Act, the beneficial owner of any securities covered by the statement.” Official typography is “sections 13(d) or 13(g) of the Act” (“or,” not “and”). A 13d-4 disclaimer is not a finding of no beneficial ownership and must not be treated as one when reading Schedule 13D / 13G holder data. Official caption typography is “Disclaimer of beneficial ownership.” Official API XML has no CITA; do not invent a source line. Official SECAUTH typography is “15 U.S.C. 78c(b), 78m(d)(1), 89m(d)(2), 78m(d)(5), 78m(d)(6), 78n(d)(1), 78w”; pack the official `89m(d)(2)` token and do not tidy it into `78m(d)(2)`. Do not flatten this box into packed 13d-1 / 13d-2 / 13d-3 or packed 12b-22 (name 12b-22 as the control-disclaimer analog only). Learning only; no issuer, filer, or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.13d-4](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.13d-4): “Any person may expressly declare in any statement filed that the filing of such statement shall not be construed as an admission that such person is, for the purposes of sections 13(d) or 13(g) of the Act, the beneficial owner of any securities covered by the statement.” Official caption typography is “Disclaimer of beneficial ownership.” Official typography is “sections 13(d) or 13(g) of the Act.” Official API XML has no CITA. Official SECAUTH typography includes `89m(d)(2)`. Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-17.

Packed 13d-1 / 13d-2 / 13d-3 and packed 12b-22 exist and are named only. 12b-22 is the control-disclaimer analog only.

## 17 CFR 240.13d-5: Acquisition of beneficial ownership (checked 2026-11-19)

Packed 13d-1 / 13d-2 / 13d-3 stay as written. This box is a different rule. Official (a) deems a person who becomes a beneficial owner of securities to have acquired that beneficial ownership for purposes of section 13(d)(1), whether such acquisition was through purchase or otherwise. Executors or administrators of a decedent's estate generally will be presumed not to have acquired the beneficial ownership held by the decedent's estate until they are qualified under local law to perform their duties. Official (b)(1)(i) is the group-formation rule: when two or more persons agree to act together for the purpose of acquiring, holding, voting or disposing of equity securities of an issuer, the group formed thereby shall be deemed to have acquired beneficial ownership, for purposes of sections 13(d) and (g) of the Act, as of the date of such agreement, of all equity securities of that issuer beneficially owned by any such persons. Official (b)(1)(ii)–(iii) and official (b)(2)(i)–(ii) then distinguish later member acquisitions from intra-group sales or transfers, and distinguish section 13(d)(3) groups from section 13(g)(3) groups. Official typography is “whether such acquisition was through purchase or otherwise,” “sections 13(d) and (g) of the Act,” “decedent's estate” (ASCII apostrophe), and “through the earlier of {'{x}'} the date of the group's dissolution or {'{y}'} the date of that member's withdrawal from the group.” Pack official {'{x}'} / {'{y}'} as ASCII curly braces (U+007B/U+007D); do not tidy them into `(x)` / `(y)`. Official caption typography is “Acquisition of beneficial ownership.” Official CITA is “[88 FR 76983, Nov. 7, 2023].” Do not flatten this box into packed 13d-3 (the beneficial-owner test) or packed 13d-1 / 13d-2. Learning only; no issuer, filer, or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.13d-5](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.13d-5): official (a) “A person who becomes a beneficial owner of securities shall be deemed to have acquired such beneficial ownership for purposes of section 13(d)(1) of the Act, whether such acquisition was through purchase or otherwise. However, executors or administrators of a decedent's estate generally will be presumed not to have acquired the beneficial ownership held by the decedent's estate until such time as such executors or administrators are qualified under local law to perform their duties.” Official (b)(1)(i) “When two or more persons agree to act together for the purpose of acquiring, holding, voting or disposing of equity securities of an issuer, the group formed thereby shall be deemed to have acquired beneficial ownership, for purposes of sections 13(d) and (g) of the Act, as of the date of such agreement, of all equity securities of that issuer beneficially owned by any such persons.” Official (b)(1)(ii) “A group regulated as a person pursuant to section 13(d)(3) of the Act shall be deemed to have acquired beneficial ownership, as determined under paragraph (a) of this section and for purposes of sections 13(d)(1) and (2) of the Act, if any member of the group becomes the beneficial owner of additional equity securities in the same class beneficially owned by the group after the group's formation. The beneficial ownership so acquired shall be reported as being held by the group through the earlier of {'{x}'} the date of the group's dissolution or {'{y}'} the date of that member's withdrawal from the group.” Official (b)(1)(iii) “Notwithstanding paragraph (b)(1)(ii) of this section, a group regulated under section 13(d)(3) of the Act shall not be deemed to have acquired beneficial ownership, as determined under paragraph (a) of this section, if, after the group's formation, a member of the group becomes the beneficial owner of additional equity securities in the same class beneficially owned by the group through a sale by or transfer from another member of the group.” Official (b)(2)(i) “A group regulated as a person pursuant to section 13(g)(3) of the Act shall be deemed to have become the beneficial owner, for purposes of sections 13(g)(1) and (2) of the Act, if any member of the group becomes a beneficial owner of additional equity securities in the same class held by the group after the group's formation and through the earlier of {'{x}'} the date of the group's dissolution or {'{y}'} the date of that member's withdrawal from the group.” Official (b)(2)(ii) “Notwithstanding paragraph (b)(2)(i) of this section, a group regulated under section 13(g)(3) of the Act shall not be deemed to have become the beneficial owner of additional equity securities in the same class beneficially owned by the group if, after the group's formation, a member of the group becomes the beneficial owner of additional equity securities in the same class beneficially owned by the group through a sale by or transfer from another member of the group.” Official caption typography is “Acquisition of beneficial ownership.” Official source line: “[88 FR 76983, Nov. 7, 2023].” Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-17.

Packed 13d-3 and packed 13d-1 / 13d-2 exist and are named only.

**Lab:** section AY of the same script (after section AX / 13d-4; 184/184 after AY):

```text
=== AY. 17 CFR 240.13d-5: Acquisition of beneficial ownership ===
official caption typography is Acquisition of beneficial ownership.
13d-5(a): A person who becomes a beneficial owner of securities shall be deemed to have acquired such beneficial ownership for purposes of section 13(d)(1) of the Act, whether such acquisition was through purchase or otherwise. However, executors or administrators of a decedent's estate generally will be presumed not to have acquired the beneficial ownership held by the decedent's estate until such time as such executors or administrators are qualified under local law to perform their duties.
13d-5(b)(1)(i): When two or more persons agree to act together for the purpose of acquiring, holding, voting or disposing of equity securities of an issuer, the group formed thereby shall be deemed to have acquired beneficial ownership, for purposes of sections 13(d) and (g) of the Act, as of the date of such agreement, of all equity securities of that issuer beneficially owned by any such persons.
13d-5(b)(1)(ii): A group regulated as a person pursuant to section 13(d)(3) of the Act shall be deemed to have acquired beneficial ownership, as determined under paragraph (a) of this section and for purposes of sections 13(d)(1) and (2) of the Act, if any member of the group becomes the beneficial owner of additional equity securities in the same class beneficially owned by the group after the group's formation. The beneficial ownership so acquired shall be reported as being held by the group through the earlier of {x} the date of the group's dissolution or {y} the date of that member's withdrawal from the group.
13d-5(b)(1)(iii): Notwithstanding paragraph (b)(1)(ii) of this section, a group regulated under section 13(d)(3) of the Act shall not be deemed to have acquired beneficial ownership, as determined under paragraph (a) of this section, if, after the group's formation, a member of the group becomes the beneficial owner of additional equity securities in the same class beneficially owned by the group through a sale by or transfer from another member of the group.
13d-5(b)(2)(i): A group regulated as a person pursuant to section 13(g)(3) of the Act shall be deemed to have become the beneficial owner, for purposes of sections 13(g)(1) and (2) of the Act, if any member of the group becomes a beneficial owner of additional equity securities in the same class held by the group after the group's formation and through the earlier of {x} the date of the group's dissolution or {y} the date of that member's withdrawal from the group.
13d-5(b)(2)(ii): Notwithstanding paragraph (b)(2)(i) of this section, a group regulated under section 13(g)(3) of the Act shall not be deemed to have become the beneficial owner of additional equity securities in the same class beneficially owned by the group if, after the group's formation, a member of the group becomes the beneficial owner of additional equity securities in the same class beneficially owned by the group through a sale by or transfer from another member of the group.
```

## 17 CFR 240.13d-6: Exemption of certain acquisitions (checked 2026-11-19)

Packed 13d-5 and packed 13d-1 stay as written. This box is a different rule. Official (a) exempts from section 13(d) an acquisition by a person who, prior to such acquisition, was a beneficial owner of more than five percent of the outstanding securities of the same class, only if the acquisition is made pursuant to preemptive subscription rights in an offering made to all holders of that class; the person does not acquire additional securities except through the exercise of such person's pro rata share of those rights; and the acquisition is duly reported, if required, pursuant to section 16(a). Official (b) is a separate group exemption for concerted actions relating to a purchase of equity securities directly from an issuer in a transaction not involving a public offering, and only if all four official (b)(1)–(4) conditions are met. Official (b)(1) typography is “persons specified in § 240.13d-1(b)(1)(ii).” Official 13d-1(b)(1)(ii) and official 13d-3(b) are named only; do not pack that catalog. Official typography is “more than five percent,” “preemptive subscription rights,” “such person's pro rata share,” and “duly reported, if required, pursuant to section 16(a) of the Act.” Official caption typography is “Exemption of certain acquisitions.” Official CITA is “[88 FR 76984, Nov. 7, 2023]”; do not tidy that page into 13d-5’s 76983. Do not flatten this box into packed 13d-5 or packed 13d-1. Learning only; no issuer, filer, or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.13d-6](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.13d-6): official (a) “The acquisition of securities of an issuer by a person who, prior to such acquisition, was a beneficial owner of more than five percent of the outstanding securities of the same class as those acquired shall be exempt from section 13(d) of the Act; provided, that:” Official (a)(1) “The acquisition is made pursuant to preemptive subscription rights in an offering made to all holders of securities of the class to which the preemptive subscription rights pertain;” Official (a)(2) “Such person does not acquire additional securities except through the exercise of such person's pro rata share of the preemptive subscription rights; and” Official (a)(3) “The acquisition is duly reported, if required, pursuant to section 16(a) of the Act and the rules and regulations thereunder in this part.” Official (b) “A group shall be deemed not to have acquired any equity securities beneficially owned by the other members of the group solely by virtue of their concerted actions relating to the purchase of equity securities directly from an issuer in a transaction not involving a public offering; provided, that:” Official (b)(1) “All the members of the group are persons specified in § 240.13d-1(b)(1)(ii);” Official (b)(2) “The purchase is in the ordinary course of each member's business and not with the purpose nor with the effect of changing or influencing control of the issuer, nor in connection with or as a participant in any transaction having such purpose or effect, including any transaction subject to § 240.13d-3(b);” Official (b)(3) “There is no agreement among or between any members of the group to act together with respect to the issuer or its securities except for the purpose of facilitating the specific purchase involved; and” Official (b)(4) “The only actions among or between any members of the group with respect to the issuer or its securities subsequent to the closing date of the non-public offering are those which are necessary to conclude ministerial matters directly related to the completion of the offer or sale of the securities.” Official caption typography is “Exemption of certain acquisitions.” Official source line: “[88 FR 76984, Nov. 7, 2023].” Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-17.

Packed 13d-5 and packed 13d-1 exist and are named only. Official 13d-1(b)(1)(ii) and official 13d-3(b) are named only.

## 17 CFR 240.16a-6: Small acquisitions (checked 2026-11-19)

Already-taught Form 4 / Form 5 clocks, packed Section 16(b), and packed 13d-2 stay as written. This box is a different reporting deferral: a qualifying small acquisition (not from the issuer, including not from an employee benefit plan sponsored by the issuer) not exceeding $10,000 in market value, under official (a)(1)–(2), “shall be reported on Form 5.” If it no longer qualifies, official (b) requires a Form 4 “before the end of the second business day following the day on which the conditions of paragraph (a) of this section are no longer met.” Do not tidy that into “within two business days.” Do not apply the deferral to an acquisition from the issuer. Do not flatten this rule into those already-shipped boxes. Learning only; no filer, disclosure, or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.16a-6](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.16a-6): official (a) “Any acquisition of an equity security or the right to acquire such securities, other than an acquisition from the issuer (including an employee benefit plan sponsored by the issuer), not exceeding $10,000 in market value shall be reported on Form 5, subject to the following conditions:” Official (a)(1) aggregates other acquisitions of the same class (including securities underlying derivative securities, but excluding acquisitions exempted by rule from section 16(b) or previously reported on Form 4 or Form 5) within the prior six months, and that total must not exceed $10,000 in market value. Official (a)(2): “The person making the acquisition does not within six months thereafter make any disposition, other than by a transaction exempt from section 16(b) of the Act.” Official (b): “If an acquisition no longer qualifies for the reporting deferral in paragraph (a) of this section, all such acquisitions that have not yet been reported must be reported on Form 4 before the end of the second business day following the day on which the conditions of paragraph (a) of this section are no longer met.” Official caption typography is “Small acquisitions.” Official source line: “[56 FR 7265, Feb. 21, 1991, as amended at 61 FR 30392, June 14, 1996; 67 FR 56467, Sept. 3, 2002].” Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-17. Form 5 is still due 2026-02-17, and 13d-1(g) is still 2026-10-13, as already printed.

Already-taught Form 4 / Form 5 clocks, packed Section 16(b), and packed 13d-2 exist and are named only.

## 17 CFR 240.16a-9: Stock splits, stock dividends, and pro rata rights (checked 2026-11-19)

Already-taught split / dividend price adjustments, packed 16a-6, and packed Section 16(b) stay as written. This box is a different exemption: the increase or decrease in the number of securities from a stock split or a stock dividend applying equally to all securities of a class is exempt from section 16, and so is the acquisition of rights pursuant to a pro rata grant to all holders of the same class registered under section 12. The official Note is part of the packed slice: the exercise or sale of a pro rata right “shall be reported pursuant to § 240.16a-4.” Do not invent that the exercise or sale is also exempt from reporting. Do not flatten this rule into those already-shipped boxes. Learning only; no issuer, filer, or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.16a-9](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.16a-9): “The following shall be exempt from section 16 of the Act:” Official (a) “The increase or decrease in the number of securities held as a result of a stock split or stock dividend applying equally to all securities of a class, including a stock dividend in which equity securities of a different issuer are distributed; and” Official (b) “The acquisition of rights, such as shareholder or pre-emptive rights, pursuant to a pro rata grant to all holders of the same class of equity securities registered under section 12 of the Act.” Official Note: “The exercise or sale of a pro rata right shall be reported pursuant to § 240.16a-4 and the exercise shall be eligible for exemption from section 16(b) of the Act pursuant to § 240.16b-6(b).” Official caption typography is “Stock splits, stock dividends, and pro rata rights.” (Oxford comma). Official source line: “[56 FR 7265, Feb. 21, 1991, as amended at 61 FR 30393, June 14, 1996].” Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-17.

Already-taught split / dividend price adjustments, packed 16a-6, packed Section 16(b), and leftover 16a-4 / 16b-6(b) exist and are named only.

## 17 CFR 240.16a-11: Dividend or interest reinvestment plans (checked 2026-11-19)

Already-taught tax DRIP / Pub 550 / Treas. Reg. 1.1012-1(e), packed 16a-6, and packed 16a-9 stay as written. This box is a different, Section 16 exemption: a qualifying acquisition from the reinvestment of dividends or interest on securities of the **same issuer** is exempt from section 16 if the plan provides for regular reinvestment, broad-based participation, does not discriminate in favor of employees of the issuer, and operates on substantially the same terms for all plan participants. Do not invent that every DRIP is exempt. Do not flatten this rule into the tax-DRIP boxes or into packed 16a-6 / 16a-9. Learning only; no issuer, tax, or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.16a-11](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.16a-11): “Any acquisition of securities resulting from the reinvestment of dividends or interest on securities of the same issuer shall be exempt from section 16 of the Act if the acquisition is made pursuant to a plan providing for the regular reinvestment of dividends or interest and the plan provides for broad-based participation, does not discriminate in favor of employees of the issuer, and operates on substantially the same terms for all plan participants.” Official caption typography is “Dividend or interest reinvestment plans.” Official source line: “[61 FR 30393, June 14, 1996].” Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-17.

Already-taught tax DRIP / Pub 550 / Treas. Reg. 1.1012-1(e), packed 16a-6, and packed 16a-9 exist and are named only.

## 17 CFR 240.16a-4: Derivative securities (checked 2026-11-19)

Already-taught Form 4 transaction codes, packed 16a-6 / 16a-9 / 16a-11, and packed Section 16(b) stay as written. This box packs the whole current section, including the official Note. Official (a) typography italicizes <em>except that</em> with no trailing comma inside the italics (unlike packed 15c1-3 / 15c1-9). Official (b) treats the exercise or conversion of a call equivalent position as a purchase of the underlying and a closing of the derivative; official (c) treats a put equivalent position as a sale of the underlying and a closing of the derivative. Official (d) points to leftover § 240.16b-6(d). The official Note heading typography is Note to § 240.16<em>a</em>-4:, and the Note names leftover § 240.16b-3 or § 240.16b-6(b) only. Do not pack those leftovers. Do not flatten this rule into already-taught Form 4 codes, packed 16a-6 / 16a-9 / 16a-11, or packed Section 16(b). Learning only; no issuer, filer, or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.16a-4](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.16a-4): official (a) “For purposes of section 16 of the Act, both derivative securities and the underlying securities to which they relate shall be deemed to be the same class of equity securities, <em>except that</em> the acquisition or disposition of any derivative security shall be separately reported.” Official (b) “The exercise or conversion of a call equivalent position shall be reported on Form 4 and treated for reporting purposes as:” Official (b)(1) “A purchase of the underlying security; and” Official (b)(2) “A closing of the derivative security position.” Official (c) “The exercise or conversion of a put equivalent position shall be reported on Form 4 and treated for reporting purposes as:” Official (c)(1) “A sale of the underlying security; and” Official (c)(2) “A closing of the derivative security position.” Official (d) “The disposition or closing of a long derivative security position, as a result of cancellation or expiration, shall be exempt from section 16(a) of the Act if exempt from section 16(b) of the Act pursuant to § 240.16b-6(d).” Official Note heading typography is Note to § 240.16<em>a</em>-4:. Official Note: “A purchase or sale resulting from an exercise or conversion of a derivative security may be exempt from section 16(b) of the Act pursuant to § 240.16b-3 or § 240.16b-6(b).” Official caption typography is “Derivative securities.” Official source line: “[56 FR 7265, Feb. 21, 1991, as amended at 56 FR 19927, May 1, 1991; 61 FR 30392, June 14, 1996].” Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-17.

Already-taught Form 4 transaction codes, packed 16a-6 / 16a-9 / 16a-11, packed Section 16(b), and leftover 16b-3 / 16b-6(b) / 16b-6(d) exist and are named only.

## 17 CFR 240.16a-10: Exemptions under section 16(a) (checked 2026-11-19)

Packed 16a-6 / 16a-9 / 16a-11 and packed Section 16(b) stay as written. This box is the official 16(a)→16(b) exemption linkage: “Except as provided in § 240.16a-6, any transaction exempted from the requirements of section 16(a) of the Act, insofar as it is otherwise subject to the provisions of section 16(b), shall be likewise exempt from section 16(b) of the Act.” 16a-6 is the official carve-out, not a 16(b) exemption under 16a-10. Do not typo the section sign away, and do not rewrite “likewise exempt” as “also reported” or as a Form 5 deferral. The official API XML has no source line; do not invent one. Do not flatten this rule into packed 16a-6 / 16a-9 / 16a-11 / Section 16(b). Learning only; no issuer, filer, or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.16a-10](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.16a-10): “Except as provided in § 240.16a-6, any transaction exempted from the requirements of section 16(a) of the Act, insofar as it is otherwise subject to the provisions of section 16(b), shall be likewise exempt from section 16(b) of the Act.” Official caption typography is “Exemptions under section 16(a).” The official API XML has no CITA element; this lesson does not invent a source line. ## 17 CFR 240.16a-2: Persons and transactions subject to section 16 (checked 2026-11-19)

Packed 16a-12 / 16a-13 / 16a-10 / 16a-3 / 16a-11, and packed 16c-3 / 16c-4, stay as written. This box is a different rule: who is subject to section 16. Official opening “Any person who is the beneficial owner, directly or indirectly, of more than ten percent of any class of equity securities (“ten percent beneficial owner”) registered pursuant to section 12 of the Act (15 U.S.C. 78<em>l</em>), any director or officer of the issuer of such securities, and any person specified in section 30(h) of the Investment Company Act of 1940 (15 U.S.C. 80a-29(h)), including any person specified in § 240.16a-8, shall be subject to the provisions of section 16 of the Act (15 U.S.C. 78p). The rules under section 16(a) of the Act do not apply to ten percent beneficial owners of an issuer that is a foreign private issuer, as defined in Rule 3b-4 (§ 240.3b-4 of this chapter). The rules under section 16 of the Act apply to any class of equity securities of an issuer whether or not registered under section 12 of the Act. The rules under section 16 of the Act also apply to non-equity securities as provided by the Investment Company Act of 1940. With respect to transactions by persons subject to section 16 of the Act:” Official typography is “(“ten percent beneficial owner”)” with typographic quotes. Official typography is “The rules under section 16(a) of the Act do not apply to ten percent beneficial owners of an issuer that is a foreign private issuer, as defined in Rule 3b-4 (§ 240.3b-4 of this chapter).” Official typography is also “The rules under section 16 of the Act apply to any class of equity securities of an issuer whether or not registered under section 12 of the Act.” Do not tidy that last sentence into official (c), which still says a ten percent beneficial owner not otherwise subject to section 16 must report only those transactions conducted while the beneficial owner of more than ten percent of a class of equity securities of the issuer registered pursuant to section 12 of the Act. Official (a)–(d), including the note to paragraph (b), are packed. Official 16a-8 and official 3b-4 are named only. Do not pack 16a-1, and do not flatten this box into packed 16a-3 / 16a-10 / 16a-12 or leftover 16b-3 / 16b-6. Official source line: “[56 FR 7265, Feb. 21, 1991, as amended at 61 FR 30392, June 14, 1996; 67 FR 43535, June 28, 2002; 76 FR 71877, Nov. 21, 2011; 91 FR 10333, Mar. 3, 2026; 91 FR 32336, June 1, 2026].” Learning only; no issuer, filer, or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.16a-2](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.16a-2): official opening “Any person who is the beneficial owner, directly or indirectly, of more than ten percent of any class of equity securities (“ten percent beneficial owner”) registered pursuant to section 12 of the Act (15 U.S.C. 78<em>l</em>), any director or officer of the issuer of such securities, and any person specified in section 30(h) of the Investment Company Act of 1940 (15 U.S.C. 80a-29(h)), including any person specified in § 240.16a-8, shall be subject to the provisions of section 16 of the Act (15 U.S.C. 78p). The rules under section 16(a) of the Act do not apply to ten percent beneficial owners of an issuer that is a foreign private issuer, as defined in Rule 3b-4 (§ 240.3b-4 of this chapter). The rules under section 16 of the Act apply to any class of equity securities of an issuer whether or not registered under section 12 of the Act. The rules under section 16 of the Act also apply to non-equity securities as provided by the Investment Company Act of 1940. With respect to transactions by persons subject to section 16 of the Act:” Official (a) “A transaction(s) carried out by a director or officer in the six months prior to the director or officer becoming subject to section 16 of the Act shall be subject to section 16 of the Act and reported on the first required Form 4 only if the transaction(s) occurred within six months of the transaction giving rise to the Form 4 filing obligation and the director or officer became subject to section 16 of the Act solely as a result of the issuer registering a class of equity securities pursuant to section 12 of the Act.” Official (b) “A transaction(s) following the cessation of director or officer status shall be subject to section 16 of the Act only if:” Official (b)(1) “Executed within a period of less than six months of an opposite transaction subject to section 16(b) of the Act that occurred while that person was a director or officer; and” Official (b)(2) “Not otherwise exempted from section 16(b) of the Act pursuant to the provisions of this chapter.” Official Note to paragraph (<em>b</em>): “For purposes of this paragraph, an acquisition and a disposition each shall be an opposite transaction with respect to the other.” Official (c) “The transaction that results in a person becoming a ten percent beneficial owner is not subject to section 16 of the Act unless the person otherwise is subject to section 16 of the Act. A ten percent beneficial owner not otherwise subject to section 16 of the Act must report only those transactions conducted while the beneficial owner of more than ten percent of a class of equity securities of the issuer registered pursuant to section 12 of the Act.” Official (d)(1) “Transactions by a person or entity shall be exempt from the provisions of section 16 of the Act for the 12 months following appointment and qualification, to the extent such person or entity is acting as:” Official (d)(1)(i) “Executor or administrator of the estate of a decedent;” Official (d)(1)(ii) “Guardian or member of a committee for an incompetent;” Official (d)(1)(iii) “Receiver, trustee in bankruptcy, assignee for the benefit of creditors, conservator, liquidating agent, or other similar person duly authorized by law to administer the estate or assets of another person; or” Official (d)(1)(iv) “Fiduciary in a similar capacity.” Official (d)(2) “Transactions by such person or entity acting in a capacity specified in paragraph (d)(1) of this section after the period specified in that paragraph shall be subject to section 16 of the Act only where the estate, trust or other entity is a beneficial owner of more than ten percent of any class of equity security registered pursuant to section 12 of the Act.” Official caption typography is “Persons and transactions subject to section 16.” Official typography is “(“ten percent beneficial owner”)”, and 15 U.S.C. 78<em>l</em> with official italic on the letter l. Official source line: “[56 FR 7265, Feb. 21, 1991, as amended at 61 FR 30392, June 14, 1996; 67 FR 43535, June 28, 2002; 76 FR 71877, Nov. 21, 2011; 91 FR 10333, Mar. 3, 2026; 91 FR 32336, June 1, 2026].” Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-17.

Official 16a-8 and official 3b-4 are named only. Packed 16a-3 / 16a-10 / 16a-12 exist and are named only.

## 17 CFR 240.3b-2: Definition of “officer” (checked 2026-11-19)

## 17 CFR 240.3b-7: Definition of “executive officer” (checked 2026-11-19)

Packed 3b-2 and packed 16a-2 stay as written. “officer” and “executive officer” are official distinct definitions and must not be tidied together, and this typography must not be tidied into named-only official 16a-1(f) (which uses “policy-making,” “vice-president,” and “controller”). Official text “The term <em>executive officer,</em> when used with reference to a registrant, means its president, any vice president of the registrant in charge of a principal business unit, division or function (such as sales, administration or finance), any other officer who performs a policy making function or any other person who performs similar policy making functions for the registrant. Executive officers of subsidiaries may be deemed executive officers of the registrant if they perform such policy making functions for the registrant.” Official typography italicizes <em>executive officer,</em> including the comma. A vice president counts only if in charge of a principal business unit, division or function (such as sales, administration or finance), or if the person otherwise performs a policy making function. Official typography is “policy making” (two words, no hyphen) and “vice president” (two words, no hyphen). Executive officers of subsidiaries may be deemed executive officers of the registrant if they perform such policy making functions for the registrant. Official caption typography is `Definition of “executive officer”.` (typographic U+201C/U+201D). Official CITA is “[47 FR 11464, Mar. 16, 1982, as amended at 56 FR 7265, Feb. 21, 1991].” Official 16a-1 is named only; do not pack 16a-1. Do not flatten this box into 3b-2. Learning only; no issuer, filer, or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.3b-7](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.3b-7): “The term <em>executive officer,</em> when used with reference to a registrant, means its president, any vice president of the registrant in charge of a principal business unit, division or function (such as sales, administration or finance), any other officer who performs a policy making function or any other person who performs similar policy making functions for the registrant. Executive officers of subsidiaries may be deemed executive officers of the registrant if they perform such policy making functions for the registrant.” Official caption typography is `Definition of “executive officer”.` Official typography is “policy making” and “vice president.” Official source line: “[47 FR 11464, Mar. 16, 1982, as amended at 56 FR 7265, Feb. 21, 1991].” Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-17.

## 17 CFR 240.3b-6: Liability for certain statements by issuers (checked 2026-11-19)

Packed 3b-2 / 3b-7 and packed 14a-3 stay as written. This box packs the whole current official 3b-6 section. [17 CFR 240.3b-6](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.3b-6): Official (a) “A statement within the coverage of paragraph (b) of this section which is made by or on behalf of an issuer or by an outside reviewer retained by the issuer shall be deemed not to be a fraudulent statement (as defined in paragraph (d) of this section), unless it is shown that such statement was made or reaffirmed without a reasonable basis or was disclosed other than in good faith.” Official (b) “This rule applies to the following statements:” Official (b)(1) “A forward-looking statement (as defined in paragraph (c) of this section) made in a document filed with the Commission, in Part I of a quarterly report on Form 10-Q, § 249.308a of this chapter, or in an annual report to security holders meeting the requirements of Rules 14a-3(b) and (c) or 14c-3(a) and (b) (§ 240.14a-3(b) and (c) or § 240.14c-3(a) and (b)), a statement reaffirming such forward-looking statement after the date the document was filed or the annual report was made publicly available, or a forward-looking statement made before the date the document was filed or the date the annual report was made publicly available if such statement is reaffirmed in a filed document, in Part I of a quarterly report on Form 10-Q, or in an annual report made publicly available within a reasonable time after the making of such forward-looking statement; <em>Provided,</em> that:” Official (b)(1)(i) “At the time such statements are made or reaffirmed, either the issuer is subject to the reporting requirements of Section 13(a) or 15(d) of the Act and has complied with the requirements of Rule 13a-1 or 15d-1 thereunder, if applicable, to file its most recent annual report on Form 10-K, Form 20-F or Form 40-F; or if the issuer is not subject to the reporting requirements of Section 13(a) or 15(d) of the Act, the statements are made in a registration statement filed under the Securities Act of 1933 offering statement or solicitation of interest, written document or broadcast script under Regulation A or pursuant to Section 12(b) or (g) of the Securities Exchange Act of 1934; and” Official (b)(1)(ii) “The statements are not made by or on behalf of an issuer that is an investment company registered under the Investment Company Act of 1940; and” Official (b)(2) “Information that is disclosed in a document filed with the Commission in Part I of a quarterly report on Form 10-Q (§ 249.308a of this chapter) or in an annual report to security holders meeting the requirements of Rules 14a-3(b) and (c) or 14c-3(a) and (b) under the Act (§ 240.14a-3(b) and (c) or § 240.14c-3(a) and (b) of this chapter) and that relates to:” Official (b)(2)(i) “The effects of changing prices on the business enterprise, presented voluntarily or pursuant to Item 303 of Regulation S-K (§ 229.303 of this chapter), “Management's Discussion and Analysis of Financial Condition and Results of Operations,” Item 5 of Form 20-F (§ 240.220(f) of this chapter), “Operating and Financial Review and Prospects,” Item 302 of Regulation S-K (§ 229.302 of this chapter) “Supplementary Financial Information,” or Rule 3-20(c) of Regulation S-X (§ 210.3-20(c) of this chapter); or” Official (b)(2)(ii) “The value of proved oil and gas reserves (such as a standardized measure of discounted future net cash flows relating to proved oil and gas reserves as set forth in FASB ASC paragraphs 932-235-50-29 through 932-235-50-36 (Extractive Activities—Oil and Gas Topic)), presented voluntarily or pursuant to Item 302 of Regulation S-K (§ 229.302 of this chapter).” Official (c) “For the purpose of this rule, the term <em>forward-looking statement</em> shall mean and shall be limited to:” Official (c)(1) “A statement containing a projection of revenues, income (loss), earnings (loss) per share, capital expenditures, dividends, capital structure or other financial items;” Official (c)(2) “A statement of management's plans and objectives for future operations;” Official (c)(3) “A statement of future economic performance contained in management's discussion and analysis of financial condition and results of operations included pursuant to Item 303 of Regulation S-K (§ 229.303 of this chapter) or Item 5 of Form 20-F or” Official (c)(4) “Disclosed statements of the assumptions underlying or relating to any of the statements described in paragraphs (c) (1), (2), or (3) of this section.” Official (d) “For the purpose of this rule the term <em>fraudulent statement</em> shall mean a statement which is an untrue statement of a material fact, a statement false or misleading with respect to any material fact, an omission to state a material fact necessary to make a statement not misleading, or which constitutes the employment of a manipulative, deceptive, or fraudulent device, contrivance, scheme, transaction, act, practice, course of business, or an artifice to defraud, as those terms are used in the Securities Exchange Act of 1934 or the rules or regulations promulgated thereunder.” Official caption typography is “Liability for certain statements by issuers.” Official source line: “[46 FR 13990, Feb. 25, 1981, as amended at 46 FR 19457, Mar. 31, 1981; ## 17 CFR 240.16e-1: Arbitrage transactions under section 16 (checked 2026-11-19)

Packed section 16 boxes, including the later 16c-3 box, stay as written. This box packs the whole current official 16e-1 section (the official one-paragraph body): it shall be unlawful for any director or officer of an issuer of an equity security registered pursuant to section 12 of the Act to effect any foreign or domestic arbitrage transaction in any equity security of such issuer, whether registered or not, unless he shall include such transaction in the statements required by section 16(a) and shall account to such issuer for the profits arising from such transaction, as provided in section 16(b). The provision of section 16(c) shall not apply to such arbitrage transactions. The provisions of section 16 shall not apply to any bona fide foreign or domestic arbitrage transaction insofar as it is effected by any person other than such director or officer of the issuer of such security. Official SECAUTH typography is (Secs. 4, 12, 13, 15, 16, 19, 24, 48 Stat. 77, 892, 894, 895, 896, 85, as amended, 901; 15 U.S.C. 77d, 78<em>l,</em> 78m, 78<em>o,</em> 78p, 77s, 78x) — official italics include the commas after l and o. Official CITA is “[30 FR 2025, Feb. 13, 1965]”. Name packed section 16 boxes only. Learning only; no issuer or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.16e-1](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.16e-1): Official text “It shall be unlawful for any director or officer of an issuer of an equity security which is registered pursuant to section 12 of the Act to effect any foreign or domestic arbitrage transaction in any equity security of such issuer, whether registered or not, unless he shall include such transaction in the statements required by section 16(a) and shall account to such issuer for the profits arising from such transaction, as provided in section 16(b). The provision of section 16(c) shall not apply to such arbitrage transactions. The provisions of section 16 shall not apply to any bona fide foreign or domestic arbitrage transaction insofar as it is effected by any person other than such director or officer of the issuer of such security.” Official caption typography is “Arbitrage transactions under section 16.” Official source line: “[30 FR 2025, Feb. 13, 1965]”. ## 17 CFR 240.16b-5: Bona fide gifts and inheritance (checked 2026-11-19)

Packed section 16 boxes, including the later 16c-3 box, stay as written. This box packs the whole current official 16b-5 section (the official one-paragraph body): both the acquisition and the disposition of equity securities shall be exempt from the operation of section 16(b) of the Act if they are: (a) Bona fide gifts; or (b) transfers of securities by will or the laws of descent and distribution. Official typography is “section 16(b) of the Act” — this is a section 16(b) short-swing exemption, not a section 16(a) reporting exemption, and must not be taught as a Form 4 reporting exemption when reading Form 4 gift or inheritance rows. Official (a) typography is “Bona fide gifts;” (official semicolon before “or,” official capital B). Official (b) typography is “transfers of securities by will or the laws of descent and distribution” (official lowercase t). Official caption typography is “Bona fide gifts and inheritance.” Official API XML has no italics, no SECAUTH, and no CITA; do not invent a source line or SECAUTH. Name official 16b-1 / 16b-7 / 16b-8 only; official 16b-2 and official 16b-4 remain Reserved; do not pack locked 16b-3 / 16b-6. Do not tidy official 16b-1 typography (italic <em>et seq.</em>, official CITA “[56 FR 7270, Feb. 21, 1991, as amended at 61 FR 30404, June 14, 1996; 76 FR 71877, Nov. 21, 2011]”) into this box. Learning only; no issuer or investment advice.

Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-17.

## 17 CFR 240.16c-3: Exemption of sales of securities to be acquired (checked 2026-11-19)

The already-taught when-issued / when-distributed box and packed 16a-4 stay as written. This box is a different rule: official (a) “Whenever any person is entitled, incident to ownership of an issued security and without the payment of consideration, to receive another security “when issued” or “when distributed,” the sale of the security to be acquired shall be exempt from the operation of section 16(c) of the Act: <em>Provided,</em> That:” Official (a)(1) “The sale is made subject to the same conditions as those attaching to the right of acquisition;” Official (a)(2) “Such person exercises reasonable diligence to deliver such security to the purchaser promptly after the right of acquisition matures; and” Official (a)(3) “Such person reports the sale on the appropriate form for reporting transactions by persons subject to section 16(a) of the Act.” Official typography italicizes “Provided,” including the comma, in “<em>Provided,</em> That:”. Official typography uses typographic quotes around “when issued” and “when distributed.” Official (b) “This section shall not exempt transactions involving both a sale of the issued security and a sale of a security “when issued” or “when distributed” if the combined transactions result in a sale of more securities than the aggregate of issued securities owned by the seller plus those to be received for the other security “when issued” or “when distributed.”” Official caption typography is “Exemption of sales of securities to be acquired.” Official API XML has no CITA; do not invent a source line. Do not flatten this box into the already-taught when-issued box or packed 16a-4. Learning only; no issuer, filer, or investment advice.

[17 CFR 240.16c-3](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-17/section-240.16c-3): official (a) “Whenever any person is entitled, incident to ownership of an issued security and without the payment of consideration, to receive another security “when issued” or “when distributed,” the sale of the security to be acquired shall be exempt from the operation of section 16(c) of the Act: <em>Provided,</em> That:” Official (a)(1) “The sale is made subject to the same conditions as those attaching to the right of acquisition;” Official (a)(2) “Such person exercises reasonable diligence to deliver such security to the purchaser promptly after the right of acquisition matures; and” Official (a)(3) “Such person reports the sale on the appropriate form for reporting transactions by persons subject to section 16(a) of the Act.” Official (b) “This section shall not exempt transactions involving both a sale of the issued security and a sale of a security “when issued” or “when distributed” if the combined transactions result in a sale of more securities than the aggregate of issued securities owned by the seller plus those to be received for the other security “when issued” or “when distributed.”” Official caption typography is “Exemption of sales of securities to be acquired.” Official typography italicizes “Provided,” including the comma. Official typography uses typographic quotes around “when issued” and “when distributed.” Official API XML has no CITA. Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-17.

The already-taught when-issued box and packed 16a-4 exist and are named only.
