Inventing that unlisted trading privileges may not be suspended or terminated by the exchange in accordance with its rules, or that only the issuer may apply to the Commission, rewriting official (a)(4) typography “Names of issuer.”, dropping or rewriting official SECAUTH `(Secs. 12(f) and 23, 15 U.S.C. 78<em>l</em> and 78w)`, inventing a CITA line other than “[20 FR 6702, Sept. 13, 1955, as amended at 44 FR 75135, Dec. 19, 1979; 45 FR 36076, May 29, 1980; 60 FR 20896, Apr. 28, 1995]”, or packing official 12f-1.
110. Inventing that a national securities exchange may extend UTP without a rule or rules in effect providing for transactions in that class or type of security, inventing a SECAUTH line, tidying official CITA “[60 FR 20896, Apr. 28, 1995]” into 12f-2’s longer amended CITA, or flattening this box into packed 12f-2 / 12f-3 / 12f-4.
111. Inventing that a proxy statement need not disclose the official (e)(1)–(4) dates, rewriting official typography “§ 240.14a-8(e)(Question 5)”, dropping italic <em>et seq.</em>, tidying official (f) “more than 30 calendar days” into business days, inventing a SECAUTH line, mixing official CITA `73 FR 977, Jan. 4, 2008` into 3b-6’s 973, dropping the closing `90 FR 9689, Feb. 18, 2025`, packing official 14a-8 / 14a-4 / 14a-19, or rewriting packed 14a-3 / 14a-9 / 14a-10 / 14a-14.
112. Flattening this box into packed 232.12 or rewriting packed 232.12 / already-taught 232.13, tidying official address typography “NE,” into “NE.”, tidying official (a) / (c) “Saturdays, Sundays, and Federal holidays” and official (b) “Saturdays, Sundays and federal holidays” into one form, dropping official italics on <em>provided that</em> / <em>Submissions made in paper.</em> / <em>Electronic filings.</em>, tidying “Eastern Daylight Saving Time” into Savings, inventing a SECAUTH or source line from the official CROSSREF block, or inventing a CITA line other than “[58 FR 14682, Mar. 18, 1993, as amended at 65 FR 24801, Apr. 27, 2000; 68 FR 25799, May 13, 2003; 73 FR 973, Jan. 4, 2008; 88 FR 12209, Feb. 27, 2023]”.
113. Inventing that a statement within official (b) is still not a fraudulent statement even if made or reaffirmed without a reasonable basis or disclosed other than in good faith, dropping the comma or the italics from <em>Provided,</em>, dropping italics on <em>forward-looking statement</em> / <em>fraudulent statement</em>, dropping typographic quotes or the official comma inside the closing quote of “Supplementary Financial Information,”, dropping the official em dash in “Extractive Activities—Oil and Gas Topic.”, tidying official `§ 240.220(f)` into 249.220f, rewriting “Securities Act of 1933 offering statement or solicitation of interest,” inventing a SECAUTH line, mixing official CITA `73 FR 973, Jan. 4, 2008` into 14a-5’s 977, or rewriting packed 3b-2 / 3b-7 / 14a-3.
114. Inventing that the issuer may wait until after any such person takes office, tidying official “take” into “takes,” rewriting official typography “Items 6 (a), (d) and (e), 7 and 8 of Schedule 14A of Regulation 14A (§ 240.14a-101 of this chapter),” dropping “Eight copies of such information shall be filed with the Commission,” inventing a SECAUTH line, rewriting official CITA “[33 FR 11017, Aug. 2, 1968, as amended at 34 FR 6101, Apr. 4, 1969; 51 FR 42072, Nov. 20, 1986],” or packing the Schedule 14A catalog.
115. Inventing 12-point type for printed information statements, flattening official CITA “[31 FR 262, Jan. 8, 1966, as amended at 36 FR 8935, May 15, 1971; 51 FR 42071, Nov. 20, 1986; 61 FR 24657, May 15, 1996]” into packed 14c-6’s shorter “[31 FR 262, Jan. 8, 1966],” inventing a SECAUTH line, or rewriting packed 14a-5 / 14c-6.
116. Inventing a 20-business-day clock, italicizing official (d) “provided, however,” tidying official (c) “Form S-4 (17 CFR 229.25) or Form F-4 (17 CFR 229.34)” into official (a)(1)(iii) “§ 239.25 or § 239.34,” dropping official CITA ending “89 FR 14324, Feb. 26, 2024,” packing leftover 14c-5, or rewriting packed 14c-3 / packed 14c-6 tip-prefix sentences that official 14c-2, leftover 14c-5, and official 14c-4 exist and are named only.
117. Tidying the official ASCII apostrophe U+0027 in “company's” into U+2019, dropping typographic quotes around the sample “RESOLVED … APPROVED.” resolution, dropping italics on the letter l in Item 402(<em>l</em>) / § 229.402(<em>l</em>), tidying official Instruction 4 typography “15 U.S.C 77a” by adding a period after C, dropping italic <em>et seq.</em>, packing official 14a-20, or inventing a SECAUTH line.
118. Inventing a December 31 calendar-year Form N-PX clock, italicizing every official use of voting power, tidying unquoted official (b)/(c) uses of initial filing / final filing and the quoted defined terms “initial filing” / “final filing” into one another, inventing a SECAUTH line, or packing official Form N-PX / § 249.326 / § 274.129 / § 249.325 catalogs.
119. Inventing that section 16 does not apply to a director’s or officer’s arbitrage, inventing that section 16(c) applies to those transactions, inventing that section 16 applies to bona fide arbitrage by a person other than such director or officer, dropping the official italics that include the commas in 78<em>l,</em> and 78<em>o,</em>, or rewriting official CITA “[30 FR 2025, Feb. 13, 1965].”
120. Tidying official caption typography “and” into the body’s “or,” adding a comma after “section 12” in official (b), tidying “pursuant to section 24(b)” into official 24b-1’s “section 24(b) of the Act,” dropping “as amended” from official SECAUTH, rewriting official CITA into official 24b-1’s “[13 FR 8214, Dec. 22, 1948],” inventing italics, or packing official 24b-2 / 17a-6 / 24b-1.
121. Teaching official 16b-5 as a section 16(a) reporting exemption or as a Form 4 reporting exemption, dropping “of the Act” from official “section 16(b) of the Act,” tidying official (a) “Bona fide gifts;” into a lowercase b or dropping the official semicolon before “or,” capitalizing official (b) “transfers,” inventing a CITA or SECAUTH line, packing official 16b-1 / 16b-7 / 16b-8 or locked 16b-3 / 16b-6, or tidying official 16b-1 typography (italic <em>et seq.</em>, official CITA “[56 FR 7270, Feb. 21, 1991, as amended at 61 FR 30404, June 14, 1996; 76 FR 71877, Nov. 21, 2011]”) into this box.


## Practice (1:1 with the checklist)

1. **ex1** — reindex + missingness assert; **done when** missing=0 and ASSERT print.  
2. **ex2** — adj vs raw; **done when** split-day raw/adj returns are stated.  
3. **ex3** — data sheet with four sections; **done when** source/fields/timezone/universe appear.  
4. **ex4** — survivorship means; **done when** both means match the lesson.  
5. **ex5** — PIT lag note; **done when** lag=5 and a look-ahead sentence appear.
6. **ex6** — Info-bar clocks: print time/volume/dollar bar counts on the synthetic tape; **done when** counts match the lesson table.
7. **ex7**: Run the mask-first toy; **done when** apparent vs realisable IC, the MA sketch, three-mask ASSERT_A–E all OK (observable-but-bad-quality / clean-but-not-executable for a side/size / not yet released) and ASSERT_mask_first_demo_ok print.
8. **ex8**: run `05_data_versions_calendar.py`; **done when**: the two monthly-convention difference (≈ −18.28 bp), split raw/adj returns and calendar intersection days print, and the data sheet gains three lines “version + convention + calendar”.
9. **ex-us-src** — for three sources write `source / licence / availability_ts / allowed_use_checked_on`; **done when**: the FRED row says the service terms must be checked (R16) and the yfinance row says “personal use only, not official”.
10. **ex-us-ca** — run section D of `05_data_versions_calendar.py` and explain why double-counting gives 0.0230; **done when**: you name the step that adds the dividend twice.
11. **ex-div** — Run `08_dividend_dates_yf_shapes.py`; give the ex-date for a record date of Sunday 2026-03-15 and for a 30% dividend paid 2026-03-17, then say which yfinance call returns an `Adj Close` column; **done when**: 2026-03-13, 2026-03-18 and `auto_adjust=False`, matching the output.
12. **ex-spin-insider** (optional) — run `11_spinoff_insider_lag_toy.py`; change the child close to 30 and the Form 4 acceptance to 2026-11-06 22:30 ET; **done when**: you can say the new holder return and why a filing accepted after 22:00 counts as filed the next business day.
13. **ex-bidprice** — Run `14_bid_price_rule_toy.py`; change section A so the reverse split happened 14 months earlier, and state the new result; **done when**: you say the 180-day compliance period applies again, because the split is outside the prior one-year period and this 1-for-10 split leaves the two-year cumulative ratio below 250:1; you also say that if the split 14 months ago had been 1-for-300 there would still be no compliance period (check with `status(b, False, 300)`); and you explain why 9 closes at $1.05 are not enough to regain compliance.
14. **ex-xbrl** — Run `17_xbrl_pit_10b51_toy.py`, then call `as_of(FACTS, *P, d)` with d = "2010-01-25" and d = "2010-01-26"; **done when**: you report 5,704m and 8,235m and explain the strict "filed before" test.
15. **ex-nyse** — Run `15_nyse_price_criteria_toy.py`, then use its `avg30` on 29 closes at $1.10 followed by one at $0.20; **done when**: you report the 30-day average ($1.07, no flag) and say which NYSE rule a $0.20 close would trigger on or after 2027-07-01.
16. **ex-buyback** — Run `18_buyback_10b18_disclosure_toy.py`; change the four weekly volumes to 2,000,000 shares each, state the new 25% cap, and say whether the January Item 703 total is usable on 2026-02-02; **done when**: the cap is 500,000 shares and February 2 is still too early, matching the function.


17. **ex-10b18ah** — Run section E of `18_buyback_10b18_disclosure_toy.py`; state the after-hours price cap when the primary-market close is 40.00 and a later sale is 39.80, and say that official 10b-18 sources have not mapped 21:00–04:00 overnight / 23/5; **done when**: the cap is 39.80, matching the output, and the mapping gap is stated.
18. **ex-10b17** — Run section D of `08_dividend_dates_yf_shapes.py`; give the 10b-17 notice-due date for a 2026-03-16 record date, and say whether the open-end redeemable exception uses this notice rule; **done when**: 2026-03-06, and the open-end / UIT exception does not use this rule, matching the output.
19. **ex-form4-13g** — Run sections E–F of `11_spinoff_insider_lag_toy.py`; say whether code A is an open-market purchase, when a Form 3 is due after becoming an officer on 2026-03-02, and the 13D due date and standstill end when an intent change on 2026-08-03 leads to a 13D filed 2026-08-10; **done when**: A is not an open-market/private P, Form 3 is due 2026-03-12, 13D is due 2026-08-10, and the standstill runs through 2026-08-20, matching the output.
20. **ex-14e5** — Run section F of `19_rule144_followon_tender_toy.py`; say whether a covered person’s market purchase on the announcement day is prohibited, and whether a subsequent offering-period purchase at the same consideration is; **done when**: announcement-day market purchase is prohibited, and the same-consideration subsequent-period purchase is not, matching the output.
21. **ex-whenissued** — Run section G of `11_spinoff_insider_lag_toy.py`; say whether WI is the regular symbol, whether settlement is automatic T+1 from the WI trade date, and what happens if the securities are not to be issued; **done when**: WI is not the regular symbol, settlement is determined later, and contracts are canceled if not issued, matching the output.
22. **ex-12b2** — In your own words, state the current 12b-2 entry thresholds, the measurement date, the exit thresholds, and whether S7-2026-18 is current law; **done when**: you name $75 million / $700 million, the last business day of the most recently completed second fiscal quarter, $60 million / $560 million, and that S7-2026-18 remains a proposal.
23. **ex-12b25** — In your own words, state when Form 12b-25 is due, when a 10-K or 10-Q is deemed timely, and whether the 15- and 5-day periods and the notification itself are calendar days or business days; **done when**: you say the notification is due by the next business day; deemed-timely treatment requires the official conditions, including actual filing within 15 calendar days (annual) or 5 calendar days (quarterly); the notification is a business-day clock and the grace periods are calendar days; Form 12b-25 is not the periodic report.
24. **ex-cqssuffix** — Run section H of `11_spinoff_insider_lag_toy.py`; give the CQS and CMS suffixes for preferred, Class A, warrants, rights, and units, and say whether the CQS convention can be used to send orders into NASDAQ’s integrated platform; **done when**: CQS p / CMS PR, CQS .A / CMS A, CQS .WS / CMS WS, CQS r / CMS RT, CQS .U / CMS U, and the CQS convention is for reference only and is not used for that order entry, matching the output, plus one sentence that those symbols are not the regular common share.
25. **ex-8kitems** — Run section D of `16_index_add_earnings_time_toy.py`; name Items 1.01, 1.05, 4.02, and 5.02, and say when the Item 1.05 clock starts; **done when**: those items are not Item 2.02 earnings, and Item 1.05 is due within four business days after the registrant determines the incident is material, not automatically on discovery, matching the output.
26. **ex-20f6k** — In your own words, state the Form 20-F annual-report clock and the Form 6-K furnishing clock, and say whether 13a-16 applies to an issuer already filing 10-K / 10-Q / 8-K; **done when**: you say 20-F is due within four months after fiscal year-end, a 6-K is furnished promptly after the material is made public in the official channels and is not deemed filed for Section 18 in the usual case, and 13a-16 does not apply to that domestic periodic filer or to the depositary issuer of ADRs for a foreign issuer’s securities.
27. **ex-13d2** — Run section I of `11_spinoff_insider_lag_toy.py`; say whether a 1% 13D acquisition is deemed material, whether a smaller change may still be material, and whether a 13G amends on the 13D 1% / two-business-day clock; **done when**: 1% is deemed material, a smaller change may still be material, and a 13G generally amends within 45 days after quarter-end, not on that 13D clock, matching the output.
28. **ex-14e2** — Run section H of `19_rule144_followon_tender_toy.py`; give the 14e-2 due date when the offer is first published on 2026-03-02, and say whether this is the 14e-1 20-business-day duration or a 10-calendar-day clock; **done when**: 2026-03-13, and it is neither, matching the output.
29. **ex-14d11** — Run section I of the same script; say whether every tender has a subsequent offering period, when results must be announced after an initial expiration on 2026-03-27, and whether withdrawal rights apply in the subsequent period; **done when**: it is an election, results are due no later than 9:00 a.m. Eastern on 2026-03-30, and there are no withdrawal rights in the subsequent period, matching the output.
30. **ex-14d10** — Run section J of the same script; say whether the offer must be open to all holders of the class, whether the consideration paid to any holder must be the highest paid to any other holder, and whether this is the already-taught 14d-11 / 14e-5 same-consideration sentence; **done when**: it must be open to all holders, it must be the highest consideration, and it is not that already-taught same-consideration sentence, matching the output.
31. **ex-14e4** — Run section K of the same script; say whether the net-long limit applies to every tender, whether an all-outstanding offer with a choice of consideration accepted by lot or pro rata is a partial tender offer, and what 14d-8 does if more shares are deposited than will be taken up; **done when**: the limit does not apply to every tender; that choice-of-consideration branch is a partial tender offer; securities are taken up as nearly as may be pro rata, disregarding fractions, matching the output.
32. **ex-12g1** — In your own words, state the official current 12(g) / 12g-1 map: the 12g-1 exemption, the statutory trigger, the 120-day and 60-day clocks, and the bank branch; **done when**: you write assets not exceeding $10 million, or fewer than 2,000 holders **and** fewer than 500 of those persons were not accredited investors; the trigger is assets exceeding $10,000,000 and holders of record of either 2,000 persons **or** 500 unaccredited; 120 days after fiscal year-end, and the registration statement becomes effective sixty days after filing (or a shorter period the Commission directs); banks have no 500 prong; and you state that no inflation-adjusted asset figure should be invented.
33. **ex-14e3** — Run section L of the same script; say whether a person with material nonpublic tender information from the official sources may buy or sell once a substantial step or commencement has occurred, whether this is already-taught 14e-2 or 10b5-1, and whether this lesson invents a fixed number of days for “a reasonable time”; **done when**: that person may not buy or sell unless the information and its source are publicly disclosed within a reasonable time beforehand; this is not already-taught 14e-2 or 10b5-1; no fixed number of days is invented, matching the output.
34. **ex-12g4** — In your own words, state the official 12(g)(4) / 12g-4 termination map: the 90-day clock, the 300 / 1,200 holder tests, the rule’s second 500-person / $10 million three-fiscal-year prong, and the immediate 13(a) suspension on filing Form 15 (with the 60-day restoration clock if the certification is withdrawn or denied); **done when**: you say termination is not on the Form 15 filing date, the 13(a) duty is what is suspended immediately, and you do not invent an inflation-adjusted asset figure or flatten the statute and the rule.
35. **ex-14d2** — Run section M of the same script; state the 14d-2 commencement clock, whether this lesson invents a time zone or copies 14d-11’s “9:00 a.m. Eastern,” and whether a qualifying pre-commencement communication is commencement; **done when:** commencement is 12:01 a.m. on the date the means to tender is first given, no zone is invented, a qualifying pre-commencement communication is not commencement, and this is not already-taught 14e-3 or 14e-1, matching the output.
36. **ex-8k507** — Run section E of `16_index_add_earnings_time_toy.py`; state when the Item 5.07 four-business-day period other than (d) begins, how preliminary and final results are disclosed, and whether 5.07(d) also uses four business days; **done when:** it begins on the day the meeting ended, preliminary then final (or finals only), (d) uses the 150-calendar-day / 60-calendar-day-before-14a-8 clock, and this is not Instruction B.1 or Item 1.05, matching the output.
37. **ex-14d3** — Run section O of `19_rule144_followon_tender_toy.py`; state when Schedule TO must be filed if the bidder would beneficially own more than 5 percent after the tender, quote the still-printed NASD / NASDAQ clause, and say whether this is a substitute for already-taught 14d-2; **done when:** as soon as practicable on the date of commencement; the still-printed NASD / NASDAQ clause is quoted as written; official (b) and (c) exist and are not built here; this is not a substitute for already-taught 14d-2, matching the output.
38. **ex-13e1** — Run section P of the same script; state what an issuer that has received notice of a commenced 14(d)(1) tender must do before purchasing its equity during the tender, whether the official core text names a form, and whether official (d) exists; **done when:** the issuer must first file a statement, pay the official fee, and submit the official exhibit; the official core text requires “a statement,” not a named form; official (d) excepts ordinary-course employee-benefit-plan repurchases that are not in response to the tender; this is not already-taught 14e-5, 14d-2, or Rule 13e-3, matching the output.
39. **ex-14a9** — Run section F of `16_index_add_earnings_time_toy.py`; state what 14a-9 prohibits, whether official typography is “in the light of the circumstances under which it is made,” and whether this is already-taught Rule 14a-8 or Form 8-K Item 5.07; **done when:** no solicitation may be made by a proxy statement, form of proxy, notice of meeting or other communication, written or oral, that is false or misleading in the light of the circumstances under which it is made; official typography is singular “it is made,” not packed 10b-5’s “they were made”; official (b), official (c), and the Note are named only; this is not already-taught 14a-8 or Item 5.07, and matches the output.
40. **ex-12b20** — run section G of the same script; explain what further information 12b-20 requires, whether official typography has a comma after “made,” and whether this is packed 14a-9 or 10b-5; **done when:** in addition to the information expressly required to be included in a statement or report, there shall be added such further material information, if any, as may be necessary to make the required statements, in the light of the circumstances under which they are made not misleading; official typography has no comma after “made”; this is not packed 14a-9 or 10b-5, matching the output.
41. **ex-13a14** — run section H of the same script; explain what 13a-14(a)/(c) require, whether official typography is “Form 10-Q, Form 10-K, Form 20-F or Form 40-F,” and whether a required certification may be signed under a power of attorney; **done when:** each principal executive and principal financial officer must sign a certification that is filed as an exhibit; official typography is “Form 10-Q, Form 10-K, Form 20-F or Form 40-F” (no Oxford comma); official (c) bars a power of attorney or other form of confirming authority; official (b), official (d)–(f), § 240.13a-15 / § 240.15d-15, and 17 CFR 240.15d-14 are named only; this is not already-taught 10-K / 10-Q clocks, packed 12b-20, or Form 8-K Item 4.02, matching the output.
42. **ex-10d1** — run section I of the same script; explain what official (b)(1)’s opening sentence requires, whether official typography is “recover reasonably promptly,” and whether this invents a numeric clock or inserts a space into official “of26”; **done when:** the issuer must adopt and comply with a written policy providing that the issuer will recover reasonably promptly the amount of erroneously awarded incentive-based compensation; official typography is “recover reasonably promptly”; official (a), official (b)(1)(i)–(v) including “not dependent on if or when the restated financial statements are filed,” official (c), and official (d) are named only; official (b)(1)(iv)(C) typography is “the requirements of26 U.S.C. 401(a)(13)” with no space after “of”; this is not already-taught Form 8-K Item 4.02, packed 12b-20, packed 13a-14, or already-taught 10-K / 10-Q clocks, matching the output.
43. **ex-244100** — run section J of the same script; explain what official 244.100(a) requires a public non-GAAP financial measure to be accompanied by, official (a)(1) / official (b) typography, and official Note 1 typography; **done when:** official (a) requires the most directly comparable financial measure calculated and presented in accordance with Generally Accepted Accounting Principles (GAAP), and a reconciliation; official (c)(2)’s lowercase “generally accepted accounting principles in the United States” is not tidied into official (a)(1); official (b) typography is “in light of the circumstances under which it is presented, not misleading,” not “in the light of” or “they are presented”; official Note 1 typography is “by Web cast” and cites “paragraphs (a)(1)(i) and (a)(1)(ii)”; official Note 1(i) is “the registrant's Web site,” official Note 1(ii) is “the location of the web site,” and official Note 2(iii) is “web sites”; official (b)/(c)/(d) and 17 CFR 244.101 / 244.102 and Regulation S-K Item 10(e) / 17 CFR 229.10 are named only; this is not already-taught earnings / 10-K / 10-Q clocks, packed 13a-14, packed 12b-20, or packed 10D-1; title 17 is up to date as of 2026-11-09, matching the output.
44. **ex-14a3** — run section K of the same script; explain official 14a-3(b) opening, whether every proxy mailing requires an annual report, and whether the annual report to security holders is the Form 10-K; **done when:** official typography is two-word “security holders” and “accompanied or preceded”; official (b) is limited to a solicitation on behalf of the registrant, other than a registered investment company, for an annual or special-in-lieu meeting, or written consent in lieu of such meeting, at which directors are to be elected; official (a), official (b)(1) and later, official 14a-3(e), and 17 CFR 240.14a-4 / 14a-6 are named only; this is not packed 14a-9 / 12b-20 / 13a-14 / 244.100, already-taught 14a-8 / Item 5.07 / 10-K / 10-Q, and not the same filing as the Form 10-K, matching the output.
45. **ex-14c3** — run section L of the same script; explain official 14c-3(a) opening, whether official caption typography includes “to” before “security holders,” and whether every information statement requires an annual report; **done when:** official caption typography is “Annual report to be furnished security holders.” with no “to” before “security holders”; official typography is two-word “security holders” and “accompanied or preceded”; official (a) word order is not identical to packed 14a-3(b); official (a) is limited to that official meeting or written consent at which directors of the registrant, other than a registered investment company, are to be elected; official (a)(1)/(a)(2)/(b)/(c)/(d), 14c-2, and 14c-5 are named only; packing 14c-3(c) householding as a new core remains wrong; this is not packed 14a-3(b) / 14a-9 / 12b-20 / 13a-14 / 244.100, already-taught 14a-8 / Item 5.07 / 10-K / 10-Q, and not the same filing as the Form 10-K, matching the output.

## Quiz

<Quiz id="q1-surv" question="A classic survivorship move is?" options={["Use the full historical universe", "Replay history with only names still listed today", "Futures only", "Ignore returns"]} answer={1} explanation="Today’s members drop delistings." />
<Quiz id="q2-pit" question="PIT means?" options={["Final filings anytime", "Only information knowable as-of then", "Adj only", "Daily only"]} answer={1} explanation="Point-in-time = then-available." />
<Quiz id="q3-tz" question="A one-day session mislabel most resembles?" options={["Lower vol", "Future closes pasted onto earlier rows (look-ahead)", "Higher fees", "Dropping Beta"]} answer={1} explanation="Labels move information earlier." />
<Quiz id="q4-adj" question="Raw crashes on a split while adj does not — meaning?" options={["Strategy broke", "Unadjusted jump; declare the field", "Must short", "Bad hash"]} answer={1} explanation="Field choice defines returns." />
<Quiz id="q5-hash" question="Saving sha256 mainly?" options={["Speeds downloads", "Proves byte-level reproducibility", "Raises Sharpe", "Replaces the sheet"]} answer={1} explanation="Version check." />
<Quiz id="q6-infobar" question="Dollar/volume bars mainly change?" options={["Broker fees", "The sampling clock from wall-time to activity", "Regulatory filings", "Rf"]} answer={1} explanation="AFML ch.2 information-driven bars." />
<Quiz id="q7-infobar2" question="This lesson’s info-bar demo is?" options={["An HFT latency recipe", "A research sampling comparison on synthetic ticks", "A live order router", "A substitute for PIT"]} answer={1} explanation="Explicitly not an HFT recipe." />
<Quiz id="q9-mask" question="Upstream contamination (2507.07107) fails because?" options={["Commissions wrong", "Limit closes the toy marks non-executable enter rolling ops before row filters", "Adj only", "Must use dollar bars"]} answer={1} explanation="Post-hoc drops cannot un-poison aggregates; use Boolean mask-first." />
<Quiz id="q10-mask2" question="Course stance on 2507.07107 Sharpe 2.05/1.63?" options={["Required alpha target", "REJECT — teach the mask-first optional conservative protocol + diagnosis only", "Replaces PIT", "Proves 213 factors"]} answer={1} explanation="Debunk: headline Sharpe / factor engine are not course edge." />
<Quiz id="q8-cryptopit" question="Crypto PIT audit before alpha (2608.25348 pointer) requires?" options={["Same-bar fills OK", "No same-bar; funding lag; no backfill; careful OI", "Ignore availability time", "Always use OI"]} answer={1} explanation="Availability-masked checklist; full lab in P8 TSFM/crypto." />
<Quiz id="q9-ciz" question="Per Ken French’s site, how are monthly returns built in the CRSP CIZ files used from January 2025?" options={["Month-to-month holding period, dividends reinvested at month-end", "Compounded daily returns, dividends reinvested on ex-dates", "Price change only, no dividends", "Identical to the legacy files"]} answer={1} explanation="Site text: CIZ monthly returns are compounded daily returns with dividends reinvested on ex-dates; legacy FIZ reinvested at month-end. So record the data version." />
<Quiz id="dc-fred" question="A FRED series is marked public domain. This means?" options={["You may freely cache it and train models", "You still must check the FRED/ALFRED service/API terms, which restrict caching, model training and more", "It must be paid for", "The data is wrong"]} answer={1} explanation="R16: series rights ≠ service terms." />
<Quiz id="dc-raw" question="Raw (unadjusted) close prices are mainly for?" options={["Automatically executable prices", "The historical nominal price scale and share/cash ledger", "Direct use as total return", "Replacing the NBBO"]} answer={1} explanation="R21: executability depends on quotes, status, liquidity and the fill model." />
<Quiz id="dc-exdiv" question="Under US T+1, you buy a stock on its ex-dividend date. Do you get that dividend?" options={["Yes, any purchase before payment counts", "No, buying on the ex-date or later does not get it", "Only if you hold until the payment date", "Only in a margin account"]} answer={1} explanation="investor.gov: if you purchase on the ex-dividend date or after, you will not receive the next dividend payment." />
<Quiz id="dc-yfadj" question="With yfinance 0.2.51 or later, yf.download() without auto_adjust returns a Close that is…" options={["Fully unadjusted", "Adjusted for splits and dividends, with no Adj Close column", "Split-adjusted only, plus Adj Close", "Delayed by one day"]} answer={1} explanation="0.2.51 changed the download() default to auto_adjust=True; the docs say there is no separate Adj Close column in that mode." />
<Quiz id="dc-filing-lag" question="A company transmits its 10-K on EDGAR at 18:05 ET on Thursday 2026-02-26. Under Reg S-T Rule 13, its official filing date is…" options={["Friday 2026-02-27", "Thursday 2026-02-26", "The fiscal year-end, 2025-12-31", "Monday 2026-03-02"]} answer={0} explanation="Transmission started after 5:30 p.m. Eastern, so the filing is deemed filed on the next business day." />
<Quiz id="dc-delist-ret" question="Backtesting with CRSP CIZ MthRet, how should you handle a delisting month?" options={["Multiply by the delisting return again to be safe", "MthRet already includes delisting returns where appropriate, so don't double count; handle the excluded missing and over-10-trading-days cases yourself and document them", "Drop every delisted stock for a cleaner sample", "Set the delisting month's return to 0"]} answer={1} explanation="Per the CIZ guide, MthRet includes delisting returns if appropriate; flags G and M mark exclusions you must treat explicitly. Dropping or zeroing them biases results upward (Shumway 1997)." />
<Quiz id="dc-spinoff" question="The parent closed at 100. On the spin-off ex-date it closes at 81, and each parent share received 0.5 child shares that close at 40. What is the holder's return that day?" options={["−19%", "+1%", "+20%", "0%, spin-offs never affect returns"]} answer={1} explanation="(81 + 0.5 × 40) ÷ 100 − 1 = +1%; −19% is the fake crash you get from raw prices alone." />
<Quiz id="dc-insider-lag" question="A director sells on Wednesday 2026-11-04, and EDGAR accepts the Form 4 at 21:40 ET on Friday 2026-11-06. In a daily backtest, when can the signal first be used?" options={["The 2026-11-04 close, using the transaction date", "The Monday 2026-11-09 open, the first session after acceptance", "After the quarter ends", "The 2026-11-06 close"]} answer={1} explanation="The transaction date is earlier than the usable time. Accepted before 22:00 counts as timely that day, but the regular session ended at 16:00, so the next session open is the earliest. The dates avoid federal holidays, matching the lab." />
<Quiz id="dc-bidprice" question="A Nasdaq stock did a 1-for-10 reverse split eight months ago. Its closing bid has now been below $1.00 for 30 consecutive business days. Under the rules approved in SEC Release 34-102245, what follows?" options={["An automatic 180-day compliance period, as for any first deficiency", "Nothing, because a reverse split resets the count for three years", "It is delisted at once with no appeal", "No compliance period: Nasdaq issues a delisting determination, which the company can appeal to a Hearings Panel"]} answer={3} explanation="Rule 5810(c)(3)(A)(iv) as amended: a company that effected a reverse split over the prior one-year period is not eligible for any compliance period, and Nasdaq issues a delisting determination; a timely hearing request can be made to the Hearings Panel." />
<Quiz id="dc-nyse-price" question="An NYSE-listed stock closes alternately at $0.97 and $1.01 for 30 trading days. Is it below NYSE's $1.00 price criteria?" options={["No, because it never closed below $1.00 for 30 days in a row", "Yes, because its 30-trading-day average closing price is $0.99", "Only if it also did a reverse split in the past year", "No, because NYSE has no price rule"]} answer={1} explanation="Section 802.01C (as restated in SEC 34-102201) tests the average closing price over a consecutive 30 trading-day period. Counting consecutive days below $1 is Nasdaq's bid-price method. A recent reverse split matters only for whether a cure period is given." />
<Quiz id="dc-xbrl-filed" question="On 2009-11-02 a backtest needs Apple's net income for the fiscal year ended 2009-09-26. SEC XBRL data now hold 5,704 million (10-K filed 2009-10-27) and 8,235 million (10-K/A filed 2010-01-25, also the frames API value). Which value may the backtest use?" options={["8,235 million, because it is the latest and the frames API returns it", "5,704 million, from the only filing made before the decision date", "The average of the two values", "Neither, because XBRL data cannot be used for backtests"]} answer={1} explanation="Choose by the filed date: on 2009-11-02 only the 10-K of 2009-10-27 existed. The frames API returns the last filed value, so using it for a past date is look-ahead." />
<Quiz id="dc-10b51" question="An officer adopts a new Rule 10b5-1 plan on 2026-02-10, and the company files the 10-Q for that fiscal quarter on 2026-05-06. When does the cooling-off period end?" options={["2026-03-12, 30 days after adoption", "2026-05-08, two business days after the 10-Q", "2026-05-11, 90 days after adoption, because the later date applies", "There is no cooling-off period for officers"]} answer={2} explanation="17 CFR 240.10b5-1(c)(1)(ii)(B)(1): for directors and officers, the later of 90 days after adoption or two business days after the 10-Q/10-K disclosure, capped at 120 days. 90 days after 2026-02-10 is 2026-05-11, later than 2026-05-08. The 30-day period is for other persons." />
<Quiz id="dc-10b18" question="Under Rule 10b-18, an issuer's open-market repurchases on a single day generally may not exceed what?" options={["25% of the security's ADTV over the four preceding calendar weeks (with a once-a-week block exception)", "10% of that day's volume", "5% of shares outstanding, with no daily cap", "The entire amount in the authorization announcement"]} answer={0} explanation="17 CFR 240.10b-18(b)(4): the day's total is generally capped at 25% of ADTV, measured over the four calendar weeks preceding the week of the purchase. Once a week the issuer may instead effect one block purchase and no other Rule 10b-18 purchases that day." />
<Quiz id="dc-item703" question="On 2026-02-02 you want a backtest to use a company's official January repurchase volume. Under current disclosure rules, what should you do?" options={["Use the board authorization announcement as if the company bought that amount every day in January", "Use daily Form SR data from the 2023 rule, because that rule is in effect", "Infer a daily volume of 25% of trading volume before the 10-Q is filed", "Wait until the 10-Q or 10-K with the Item 703 monthly total is filed and public; an authorization is not executed daily volume"]} answer={3} explanation="Current disclosure is Item 703's monthly aggregate in the 10-Q or 10-K. The 2023 daily-disclosure rule (including Form SR) was vacated on 2023-12-19. An authorization is only a cap." />
<Quiz id="dc-tender" question="Under Rule 14e-1, how long must a tender offer generally remain open, and what did the SEC's April 16, 2026 order change?" options={["It must close on the announcement day, and the 2026 order repealed that rule", "It must stay open 10 calendar days, and the 2026 order made that the only allowed window", "It must stay open 20 calendar days, and the 2026 order has no conditions", "It must generally stay open at least 20 business days from first publication; the 2026 order lets some equity offers use 10 business days only if every stated condition is met, and is not a repeal of the 20-day rule"]} answer={3} explanation="14e-1(a) is 20 business days, not calendar days, and not completion on the announcement day. The April 16, 2026 exemptive order is conditional and does not repeal the 20-day rule." />
<Quiz id="dc-10b18ah" question="After the primary trading session closes, what does the Rule 10b-18 after-hours prong allow, and what have official sources not done?" options={["Overnight 21:00–04:00 / 23/5 buybacks are inside the Rule 10b-18 safe harbor", "Overnight 21:00–04:00 / 23/5 buybacks are outside the Rule 10b-18 safe harbor", "Purchases may be effected following the close of the primary session until last-sale prices cease to be reported in the consolidated system, if they are not the opening transaction of that session, the price does not exceed the lower of the primary-market close and any later lower bid or sale, and the other conditions (including the day’s volume limit) are met. The issuer may use one broker in this period that may be different from the primary-session broker. Official 10b-18 sources have not mapped this window onto 21:00–04:00 overnight / 23/5", "After-hours Rule 10b-18 purchases may be the session’s opening print, may exceed the primary-market close, and do not count toward the day’s volume limit"]} answer={2} explanation="17 CFR 240.10b-18(b)(2)(iv) and the SEC 10b-18 FAQ (Timing Condition, last reviewed 2016-12-02) state that limited after-hours safe harbor. The NYSE ETH FAQ does not discuss 10b-18. Official sources have not mapped the window onto overnight 23/5 hours; this lesson does not invent that mapping either way." />
<Quiz id="dc-10b17" question="Under Rule 10b-17, when must an issuer of publicly traded securities generally give notice of a dividend, stock split, or rights offering, and what is a late or missing notice?" options={["No later than 10 business days before the record date, and a missing notice proves the dividend did not happen", "Generally no later than 10 days prior to the record date (not 10 business days). A late or missing notice is a data-quality / timing flag around the record date, ex-date, and declared amount, not proof the dividend did not happen. Open-end and UIT redeemable securities are excepted. The rule does not itself defer the ex-date", "The issuer may wait until the ex-date, because 10b-17 automatically postpones the ex-date the same way FINRA 11140(b) does", "Notice must be given only to FINRA, because the rule text has already replaced the NASD with FINRA"]} answer={1} explanation="17 CFR 240.10b-17(b)(1) says “no later than 10 days prior to the record date,” not 10 business days. 10b-17(c) excepts redeemable securities of open-end investment companies and unit investment trusts. A late or missing notice is a timing flag, not proof there was no dividend, and 10b-17 does not itself move the ex-date. The rule text still names the NASD; this lesson does not invent a FINRA-substituted addressee." />
<Quiz id="dc-form4codes" question="How should a backtest read Form 4 transaction codes, and what are the Form 3 and Form 5 clocks?" options={["Every Form 4 acquisition code, including A, M, F, and G, is an open-market buy, and Form 3 is due in a shorter Commission deadline found in 16a-3", "Code V means an open-market sale, and Form 5 is always due 45 days after fiscal year end even if every item was already reported", "P and S are open market or private purchase or sale. A is a grant or award under Rule 16b-3(d), M is a 16b-3-exempt exercise or conversion, F is payment of the exercise price or tax by delivering or withholding securities, and G is a bona fide gift, so not every acquisition is an open-market buy. V is an earlier-reporting flag, not a purchase/sale code; other listed codes exist, including J, K used in addition, and U. Form 3 is the initial statement “within 10 days after he or she becomes such beneficial owner, director, or officer, or within such shorter time as the Commission may establish by rule”; this lesson does not invent a shorter Commission deadline. Form 5 is due within 45 days after the issuer’s fiscal year end, and none is required if everything was already reported", "Add Form 13H to this public lag table, because it is a public Section 16 filing with the same clocks"]} answer={2} explanation="Form 4 Instruction 8 gives those official codes. 15 U.S.C. 78p(a)(2)(B) is the Form 3 clock; 17 CFR 240.16a-3 assigns Form 3 / 4 / 5 and sets the Form 5 45-day clock, with no Form 5 when everything was already reported. This lesson does not invent a shorter Form 3 deadline. Form 13H is confidential and stays out of this public box." />
<Quiz id="dc-13g13d" question="A Schedule 13G filer under paragraph (b) or (c) now holds more than 5% with a purpose or effect of changing or influencing control. What must happen, and what is beneficial ownership?" options={["The person has 10 calendar days to file a Schedule 13D, which is the current filing clock, and 4.9% of record is the whole 13(d) position", "Every 13G filer who reaches 20% must switch, including a paragraph (b) filer, and a still-outstanding 13G proves the holder is still passive", "File a Schedule 13D within five business days; from that purpose or effect until the expiration of the tenth day from the date of the 13D filing, the person shall not vote or direct the voting of those securities or acquire an additional beneficial ownership interest in the issuer’s equity securities, or of any person controlling the issuer. The 20% path in 13d-1(f) applies to paragraph (c) filers. Beneficial ownership is voting and/or investment power, not merely shares of record: aggregate the class; a 60-day right to acquire is deemed ownership, but a control-purpose right is counted immediately; such not-outstanding underlying is outstanding for that person’s percentage only. “Business day” means any day other than Saturday, Sunday, or a Federal holiday, 12 a.m. to 11:59 p.m. ET", "The standstill is the filing deadline, so the person must file on the tenth day and may keep buying until then"]} answer={2} explanation="17 CFR 240.13d-1(e)–(g) and (i)(2): the switch filing clock is five business days; “tenth day” is the standstill after the 13D is filed, not a revived pre-2023 10-calendar-day filing clock. The 20% cap is the paragraph (c) path. 13d-3 is voting and/or investment power, with the 60-day and immediate-control branches, and the underlying is outstanding only for that person’s percentage." />
<Quiz id="dc-14e5" question="Under Rule 14e-5, when may a covered person not buy the subject securities or related securities except as part of the tender offer?" options={["Only after formal commencement, because a public announcement does not start the clock, and 14e-1’s minimum duration lets the bidder buy in the market", "From the time of public announcement of the tender offer until the tender offer expires. A subsequent offering period purchase at the same consideration is outside the prohibition. Related securities are securities immediately convertible into, exchangeable for, or exercisable for subject securities. Paragraph (b) exceptions exist; this lesson does not build a compliance engine. 14e-1’s minimum duration is not permission for a covered person to buy in the market", "The prohibition continues through every subsequent offering period, even when the consideration is the same in form and amount", "Related securities means any security of the same issuer, including securities that are not immediately convertible, exchangeable, or exercisable for the subject securities"]} answer={1} explanation="17 CFR 240.14e-5(a) and (c): the clock starts at public announcement, not only at formal commencement, and runs until the offer expires. The same-consideration subsequent offering period is carved out. Related securities are immediately convertible into, exchangeable for, or exercisable for subject securities. Exceptions in paragraph (b) exist; 14e-1 does not authorize market purchases by a covered person." />
<Quiz id="dc-whenissued" question="What is a when-issued contract, and when does it settle?" options={["It is the same contract as the regular outstanding share class, and it automatically settles T+1 from the WI trade date", "Splice the WI series into the regular symbol, because the prices are the same contract", "Settlement is always T+2, and when-distributed uses a completely different unofficial product mechanic", "A when-issued (or when-distributed) contract is not the regular outstanding class. The official Nasdaq CQS suffix is WI. Under FINRA 11130, the settlement date is determined later, when a sufficient percentage of the issue is outstanding; it is not automatic T+1 from the WI trade date. Contracts are canceled if the securities are not to be issued or distributed"]} answer={3} explanation="FINRA 11130: the settlement date is determined by the Committee when a sufficient percentage of the issue is outstanding; contracts are canceled if the securities are not to be issued or distributed. The official Nasdaq CQS “When issued” suffix is WI. Do not splice WI into the regular symbol, and do not assume automatic T+1. When-distributed is a related official label (WD flag; 11130 names both)." />
<Quiz id="dc-12b2" question="Under current 17 CFR 240.12b-2, how are accelerated and large accelerated filer status measured?" options={["Use fiscal year-end market cap. A brand-new IPO with public float above $700 million is immediately a large accelerated filer. S7-2026-18’s $2 billion threshold is current law", "Accelerated filer public float is $75 million or more but less than $700 million, and large accelerated is $700 million or more, measured as of the last business day of the issuer’s most recently completed second fiscal quarter, plus twelve calendar months and at least one annual report, and not eligible for the listed SRC revenue test. An accelerated filer remains one unless float was less than $60 million (or the SRC revenue test applies); a large accelerated filer remains one unless float was less than $560 million. S7-2026-18 remains a proposal", "The 60/75/90 and 40/45 day clocks have already been shortened under S7-2026-18", "Once public float falls just below $700 million, a large accelerated filer immediately becomes an accelerated filer"]} answer={1} explanation="Current 12b-2 measures public float as of the last business day of the most recently completed second fiscal quarter, not fiscal year-end market cap. Entry is $75 million / $700 million, with twelve months and one annual report. Exit uses $60 million / $560 million hysteresis. S7-2026-18 remains Proposed; do not teach $2 billion as current law." />
<Quiz id="dc-12b25" question="Under 17 CFR 240.12b-25, what happens after a 10-K or 10-Q statutory deadline is missed?" options={["Every late 10-K automatically gets 15 extra days, and the statutory 60 / 75 / 90 and 40 / 45 clocks have moved", "Form 12b-25, or an EDGAR NT 10-K, is itself the annual report", "The registrant shall file a Form 12b-25 no later than one business day after the due date. The report is deemed filed on the prescribed due date only if the official conditions are met, including actually filing the annual report within 15 calendar days or the quarterly report within 5 calendar days. Those 15- and 5-day periods are calendar days; the notification itself is due by the next business day. EDGAR often labels the notification NT 10-K or NT 10-Q; the official form is Form 12b-25. The statutory clocks have not changed", "The 15- and 5-day periods are business days, and the notification itself is due by the next calendar day"]} answer={2} explanation="12b-25(a) requires Form 12b-25 by the next business day. 12b-25(b) deemed-timely treatment is conditional and requires actual filing within 15 or 5 calendar days. Do not teach that every late 10-K automatically gets 15 extra days, or that an NT filing is the periodic report. The 60 / 75 / 90 and 40 / 45 clocks are unchanged." />
<Quiz id="dc-cqssuffix" question="Beyond the already-taught WI / WD labels, what else does the official Nasdaq CQS symbol-convention table encode, and what should a cleaner not do?" options={["The same official table also encodes preferred (CQS p, CMS PR), preferred Class A (PRA), Class A / Class B, warrants (WS), rights (RT), and units (U). Those symbols are not the regular common share. The official page has more than one encoding column (CQS, CMS, Integrated, ACT/CTCI); the CQS convention is for reference purposes only and is not used to send orders into NASDAQ’s integrated platform. Do not invent one universal suffix for every tape, order port, and vendor file", "A single spelling such as PR or WI works on every tape, order port, and vendor file", "Those suffixes are the regular outstanding common share, and settlement is still automatic T+1 from the WI trade date", "Orders into NASDAQ’s integrated platform must use the CQS convention"]} answer={0} explanation="The official table has multiple columns. Preferred is p in the CQS column and PR in the CMS column; warrants, rights, and units likewise differ by column. The CQS convention is for reference only; NASDAQ protocols do not support it for integrated-platform order entry. Do not merge those symbols with the regular common share, and do not rewrite the already-taught 11130 settlement facts." />

<Quiz id="dc-8kitems" question="Beyond already-taught Item 2.02 earnings releases, which Form 8-K facts are official?" options={["Every Form 8-K is an earnings release, and Form 12b-25 applies to an 8-K the same way it applies to a 10-K", "The Item 1.05 clock starts on discovery of the incident, and the Attorney General delay is the usual clock", "Official items also include 1.01 Entry into a Material Definitive Agreement, 1.05 Material Cybersecurity Incidents, 4.02 Non-Reliance on Previously Issued Financial Statements or a Related Audit Report or Completed Interim Review, and 5.02 Departure of Directors or Certain Officers; Election of Directors; Appointment of Certain Officers; Compensatory Arrangements of Certain Officers. The usual clock is still four business days after occurrence of the event, but Item 1.05 is due within four business days after the registrant determines that it has experienced a material cybersecurity incident; Instruction 1 says the materiality determination must be made without unreasonable delay after discovery — do not start the clock on discovery. Item 2.02 and Item 7.01 are furnished, not deemed “filed” for Section 18 in the usual case. Form 12b-25 does not apply to an 8-K. Do not flatten Item 5.07 or other special clocks into the same trigger", "Use Instruction G’s “Principal Officers” wording as the official Item 5.02 caption"]} answer={2} explanation="Official Form 8-K General Instruction B.1 and the official item captions. Item 1.05 starts when the registrant determines the incident is material; Instruction 1 requires that determination without unreasonable delay after discovery, but discovery itself is not the start of the clock. B.2: Item 2.02 / 7.01 are furnished, not deemed “filed” for Section 18 in the usual case. Use the official Item 5.02 caption “Certain Officers.”" />
<Quiz id="dc-20f6k" question="What are the official clocks for a foreign private issuer’s Form 20-F annual report and Form 6-K current report?" options={["Apply the 10-K 60 / 75 / 90 and 10-Q 40 / 45 clocks to a 20-F, and treat a 6-K as due within four business days like a domestic 8-K", "A foreign private issuer must file its annual report on Form 20-F within the four months after the end of the fiscal year covered by the report. A Form 6-K is required to be furnished promptly after the material contained in the report is made public in the official home-market, exchange, or shareholder channels, and is not deemed “filed” for Section 18 in the usual case. Form 40-F is a different MJDS form. 13a-16 does not apply to issuers already filing 10-K / 10-Q / 8-K, or to issuers of ADRs for securities of a foreign issuer (the depositary)", "“Promptly” on Form 6-K means 10 days, the same unofficial clock sometimes invented for Form 13H", "Form 20-F and Form 40-F are the same annual report, and a 6-K is a substitute 10-Q"]} answer={1} explanation="Form 20-F General Instruction A.(b) is the four-month annual-report clock. Form 6-K General Instruction B and 17 CFR 240.13a-16(b)–(c): furnished promptly after the material is made public in the official channels, and not deemed filed for Section 18. Do not invent a four-business-day or 10-day 6-K clock, and do not conflate 20-F with 40-F." />

<Quiz id="dc-13d2" question="Under Rule 13d-2, when is a Schedule 13D amendment due after a material change, and is a 13G on the same clock?" options={["Every 13G amends within two business days after a 1% change, the same clock as a 13D, and a percent change solely from a change in shares outstanding must be amended on 13G", "A Schedule 13D amendment disclosing a material change is due within two business days after the date of such change. An acquisition or disposition of one percent or more of the class is deemed material; a smaller change may still be material, depending upon the facts and circumstances. A 13G filer under 13d-1(b), (c), or (d) generally amends within 45 days after calendar quarter-end if there are material changes, not on the 13D 1% / two-business-day clock. A percent change solely from a change in the aggregate number of securities outstanding need not be a 13G amendment. Official 13d-2(c) and 13d-2(d) intra-period 10% / more-than-5% clocks also exist; this lesson does not build that engine", "Every sub-1% 13D change is automatically immaterial, so no amendment is due", "The already-taught initial 13D / 13G filing lags, and the 13G-to-13D switch clocks, are rewritten by this rule"]} answer={1} explanation="17 CFR 240.13d-2(a)–(d): the 13D two-business-day clock and the 1% deemed-material test are official, and a smaller change may still be material. A 13G generally amends within 45 days after quarter-end if there are material changes. Do not copy the 13D clock onto every 13G, and do not treat a solely-shares-outstanding percent change as a required 13G amendment. Name the official (c) and (d) branches; do not build that engine." />
<Quiz id="dc-14e2" question="Under Rule 14e-2, when must the subject company state its position on a tender offer?" options={["This is the already-taught 14e-1 20-business-day duration, so the recommendation is known on the commencement date", "The clock is 10 calendar days, and Schedule 14D-9 has a second official 10-business-day filing deadline", "No later than 10 business days from the date the tender offer is first published, sent, or given, the subject company shall publish, send, or give security holders a statement that it recommends acceptance or rejection, remains neutral, or is unable to take a position, with the reason(s). A material change is published promptly; this lesson does not invent a fixed number of days for “promptly.” This is not the 14e-1 20-business-day duration and not a 10-calendar-day clock. Schedule 14D-9 is the form, filed as soon as practicable on the date the recommendation is first published — not a second 10-business-day filing clock", "“Promptly” on a material change means four business days, like an 8-K"]} answer={2} explanation="17 CFR 240.14e-2(a) is 10 business days from the date the offer is first published, sent, or given, not 10 calendar days and not the 14e-1 duration. 14e-2(b) says “promptly,” with no fixed number of days invented here. Rule 14d-9 / Schedule 14D-9 is filed as soon as practicable on the date the recommendation is published." />
<Quiz id="dc-14d11" question="Under Rule 14d-11, what is a subsequent offering period, and how is the 9:00 a.m. Eastern clock used?" options={["A subsequent offering period is automatic in every tender offer and lasts at most 15 business days", "The 9:00 a.m. Eastern clock is the already-taught 14e-1(d) extension-notice clock, and withdrawal rights continue in the subsequent period", "There are no withdrawal rights during the initial open period", "A subsequent offering period is optional: a bidder may elect at least three business days only if official conditions are met, including that the initial period of at least 20 business days has expired and the offer is for all outstanding securities of the class; the bidder immediately accepts and promptly pays; results, including the approximate number and percentage of securities deposited to date, are announced no later than 9:00 a.m. Eastern time on the next business day after initial expiration and the subsequent period begins immediately; and the same form and amount of consideration is offered. Official note: no withdrawal rights during the subsequent period (14d-7(a)(2)); withdrawal rights do apply during the initial open period (14d-7(a)(1)). This 9:00 a.m. results clock is not the already-taught 14e-1(d) extension notice. The official text does not state a 15-business-day cap or any maximum. Cross-link the already-taught 14e-5 same-consideration carve-out"]} answer={3} explanation="17 CFR 240.14d-11 is an election of at least three business days after a qualifying 20-business-day all-outstanding initial period, with a 9:00 a.m. Eastern next-business-day results announcement. That clock is not 14e-1(d). No withdrawal rights apply in the subsequent period; they do apply while the initial offer remains open. Do not invent a maximum the official text does not state." />

<Quiz id="dc-14d10" question="Under Rule 14d-10, what must a tender offer satisfy, and is that the already-taught 14d-11 / 14e-5 same-consideration sentence?" options={["This is the already-taught 14d-11 / 14e-5 same-consideration language, so some holders may be paid more for securities tendered", "The tender offer must be open to all security holders of the class, and the consideration paid to any security holder for securities tendered must be the highest consideration paid to any other security holder for securities tendered. This is not the already-taught 14d-11 same-consideration condition, and not the 14e-5 same-consideration subsequent-period carve-out. Official (b)–(f) exceptions exist; this lesson does not build those engines. Do not rewrite the already-taught 14e-1(b) 10-business-day change-of-consideration window or the 14e-1(d) extension-notice sentence", "The offer may be open only to a favored group of holders, if a notice is sent later", "Rule 14d-10 repeals the already-taught 14e-1(b) 10-business-day change-of-consideration window"]} answer={1} explanation="17 CFR 240.14d-10(a) requires that the offer be open to all security holders of the class and that the consideration paid be the highest consideration paid to any other holder. That is not the already-taught 14d-11 / 14e-5 same-consideration sentence. Official (b)–(f) exceptions exist; this lesson does not build those engines." />
<Quiz id="dc-14e4" question="Under Rule 14e-4, when does the net-long limit apply in a tender offer, and what is a “partial tender offer”?" options={["The net-long limit applies to every tender offer, including an all-outstanding offer that does not offer a choice of consideration", "Rule 14e-4 is the already-taught 14d-11 election language, and the official text states a maximum length for 14d-11", "Equivalent securities and options do not enter the net-long definition, and this lesson builds a complete net-long engine", "In a partial tender offer, a person may not tender more than a net long position: the test applies at the time of tender and at the end of the proration period or lot-acceptance period (including extensions). Official 14e-4(a)(5) covers both a less-than-all offer accepted by lot or pro rata and an all-outstanding offer that offers a choice of consideration accepted by lot or pro rata — do not apply the net-long limit to every tender. Equivalent securities and options enter the official net-long definition; this lesson does not build that engine. Supporting 14d-8: if more securities are deposited than will be taken up, they are taken up as nearly as may be pro rata, disregarding fractions. Do not invent a 14d-11 maximum, or flatten proration into already-taught 14d-11 election language"]} answer={3} explanation="17 CFR 240.14e-4(b) applies the net-long test at tender and at the end of the proration or lot-acceptance period, including extensions. 14e-4(a)(5) includes the choice-of-consideration branch, so the limit is not applied to every tender. 14d-8 takes up securities as nearly as may be pro rata, disregarding fractions. Do not invent a 14d-11 maximum." />
<Quiz id="dc-12g1" question="Under current Rule 12g-1 and Exchange Act section 12(g), when is an issuer not required to register a class of equity, and what are the statutory clocks?" options={["Official current texts have already inflation-adjusted the 12(g) asset threshold, and this lesson should use that higher figure; the 12g-1 exemption is fewer than 2,000 persons or 500 unaccredited holders", "Section 12(g) is the already-taught 12b-2 filer category, the 120-day and 60-day clocks are the 10-K / 10-Q clocks, and the Form 10-S sentence “not adopted as of 2026-11-11” should be rewritten just to bump the date", "Under current Rule 12g-1, an issuer is not required to register a class of equity under section 12(g) if, on the last day of its most recent fiscal year, it had total assets not exceeding $10 million, or the class was held of record by fewer than 2,000 persons and fewer than 500 of those persons were not accredited investors (banks, savings-and-loan holding companies, and bank holding companies use a fewer-than-2,000-person test without the 500 prong). The statutory trigger is the other side: assets exceeding $10,000,000 and holders of record of either 2,000 persons or 500 unaccredited, with a 120-day clock after fiscal year-end; a registration statement becomes effective sixty days after filing, or such shorter period as the Commission may direct. Do not swap the official 12g-1 “and” exemption for a sloppy “or.” Official current texts still use $10 million / $10,000,000; do not invent an inflation-adjusted asset figure. “Accredited investor” is defined by reference to § 230.501(a) as of that day; “held of record” is defined in Rule 12g5-1 (an employee-compensation-plan carve-out exists); 12(g)(4) termination is a different official paragraph — name only, do not build those engines. Do not flatten these clocks into already-taught 12b-2, 10-K / 10-Q, or the Form 13F 45-day sentence", "Every US company with public shareholders is a 12(g) registrant, regardless of assets or holders of record"]} answer={2} explanation="17 CFR 240.12g-1 exempts an issuer if assets do not exceed $10 million, or if the class is held of record by fewer than 2,000 persons and fewer than 500 of those persons were not accredited investors. 15 U.S.C. 78l(g)(1) triggers on assets exceeding $10,000,000 and holders of record of either 2,000 persons or 500 unaccredited, with a 120-day clock; the registration statement becomes effective sixty days after filing, or a shorter period the Commission directs. Do not invent an inflation-adjusted figure, or swap the official 12g-1 “and” for “or.”" />
<Quiz id="dc-14e3" question="Under Rule 14e-3, once a substantial step to commence, or commencement, of a tender offer has occurred, what may a person with material nonpublic tender information from the official sources not do?" options={["This is already-taught 14e-2 or 10b5-1, so the information may be used as soon as the offer has not yet been formally commenced", "“A reasonable time” is a fixed number of days this lesson invents, and the official 14e-3(c) wording “to contrary” should be silently corrected", "That person may not purchase or sell (or cause to be purchased or sold) those securities — or convertibles, options, or rights — unless within a reasonable time prior to the purchase or sale the information and its source are publicly disclosed by press release or otherwise. Official (b) entity procedures / information-barrier defense, (c) offering-person purchase and sale-to-offering-person exceptions, and (d) anti-tipping exist; this lesson does not build those engines. Do not invent a fixed number of days for “a reasonable time.” Do not flatten 14e-3 into already-taught 14e-2, 14e-1, 14d-10, 14e-4, 14e-5, or 10b5-1", "The prohibition applies only after formal commencement, and convertibles, options, or rights are outside the rule"]} answer={2} explanation="17 CFR 240.14e-3(a) is a disclose-or-abstain rule: once a substantial step or commencement has occurred, a person with material nonpublic tender information from the official sources may not buy or sell unless that information and its source are publicly disclosed within a reasonable time beforehand. This is not already-taught 14e-2 or 10b5-1. Do not invent a fixed number of days for “a reasonable time,” or silently correct “to contrary.”" />
<Quiz id="dc-12g4" question="Under Exchange Act section 12(g)(4) and Rule 12g-4, when does 12(g) registration terminate, and what happens when a Form 15 is filed?" options={["Form 15 ends 12(g) registration on the filing date, and official current 12g-4 has already been inflation-adjusted above $10 million", "Registration terminates 90 days, or such shorter period as the Commission may determine, after a Form 15 certification that holders of record are reduced to less than 300 persons (1,200 for a bank, a savings and loan holding company, or a bank holding company). Rule 12g-4 also has a second prong: fewer than 500 persons, where total assets have not exceeded $10 million on the last day of each of the issuer’s most recent three fiscal years. The issuer’s duty to file section 13(a) reports “shall be suspended immediately upon filing a certification on Form 15”; if the certification is subsequently withdrawn or denied, the issuer shall, within 60 days, file all reports which would have been required had the certification not been filed. Official current 12g-4 still uses $10 million (last amended May 10, 2016). Do not flatten the statute and the rule, invent an inflation-adjusted asset figure, or silently tidy the LII typographical artifact “1,200 persons persons. [1]” if that clause is quoted", "This is a rewrite of already-taught 12g-1, and the 120-day clock is the termination clock", "The Form 10-S sentence “not adopted as of 2026-11-11” should be rewritten just to bump the date, and this item edits the 09 / 17 labs"]} answer={1} explanation="15 U.S.C. 78l(g)(4) and 17 CFR 240.12g-4: termination takes effect 90 days after a Form 15 certification, or sooner if the Commission so determines, not on the filing date. The 13(a) duty is what is suspended immediately; a 60-day restoration clock applies if the certification is withdrawn or denied. The rule also has the 500-person / $10 million three-fiscal-year prong. Do not invent an inflation-adjusted figure, or flatten the statute and the rule." />
<Quiz id="dc-14d2" question="Under Rule 14d-2, when does a tender offer commence, and is a qualifying pre-commencement communication commencement?" options={["Commencement is already-taught 14e-3 “substantial step,” or the 14e-1 20-business-day duration", "Official 14d-2 prints “12:01 a.m. Eastern,” so copy already-taught 14d-11’s “9:00 a.m. Eastern” onto 14d-2", "A bidder commences a tender offer, for purposes of Exchange Act section 14(d) and the rules under that section, at 12:01 a.m. on the date when the bidder has first published, sent, or given the means to tender to security holders. The means to tender includes the transmittal form or a statement regarding how the transmittal form may be obtained. Official 14d-2(a) prints “12:01 a.m.” with no time zone; do not invent a zone, and do not copy 14d-11’s “9:00 a.m. Eastern” onto 14d-2. A 12:01 a.m. commencement on a given date means that day’s 09:30 open is after official commencement. A qualifying pre-commencement communication (no means to tender, and filed under cover of Schedule TO) is not commencement. Official (c) 14d-3 / 14d-4 / 14d-6 obligations exist; this lesson does not build those engines", "Any public announcement about the offer is commencement, even without the means to tender"]} answer={2} explanation="17 CFR 240.14d-2(a) sets commencement at 12:01 a.m. on the date the means to tender is first given. The official text prints no time zone; do not invent one, and do not copy 14d-11’s “9:00 a.m. Eastern.” A qualifying (b) pre-commencement communication is not commencement. This is not already-taught 14e-3 or 14e-1." />
<Quiz id="dc-8k507" question="What is the official Form 8-K Item 5.07 clock, and is it the same as already-taught Instruction B.1 or Item 1.05?" options={["5.07 is already-taught Instruction B.1 or Item 1.05, and the four-business-day period starts on a generic occurrence date", "5.07(d) also uses the four-business-day meeting-end clock, and this lesson builds a Rule 14a-8 engine", "The four-business-day period for Item 5.07, other than 5.07(d), begins to run on the day the meeting ended; preliminary voting results are disclosed, and an amended 8-K discloses the final results within four business days after they are known; no preliminary is required if finals have already been disclosed. Official 5.07(d) uses a different clock: no later than 150 calendar days after the end of the meeting, and in no event later than 60 calendar days prior to the Rule 14a-8 shareholder-proposal deadline, disclose the frequency decision by amendment. Do not flatten 5.07 into Instruction B.1 or Item 1.05, and do not invent a four-business-day clock for (d). This lesson does not build a Rule 14a-8 engine", "Preliminary results must always be disclosed, even if final results have already been disclosed"]} answer={2} explanation="Official Form 8-K Instruction 1 to Item 5.07: other than (d), the four-business-day period begins on the day the meeting ended; preliminary then final (or finals only). Item 5.07(d) is a 150-calendar-day / 60-calendar-day-before-14a-8 dual clock, not a four-business-day clock. Do not flatten it into Instruction B.1 or Item 1.05." />
<Quiz id="dc-14d3" question="Under Rule 14d-3, when must a bidder who would beneficially own more than 5 percent of the class after the tender file Schedule TO, and how should the still-printed NASD / NASDAQ clause be quoted?" options={["This is a substitute for already-taught Rule 14d-2 commencement at 12:01 a.m.", "Silently modernize NASD / NASDAQ to current exchange names, or invent a clock and time zone the official text does not state", "No bidder shall make a tender offer if, after consummation, the bidder would beneficially own more than 5 percent of the class, unless as soon as practicable on the date of the commencement the bidder files a Tender Offer Statement on Schedule TO and transmits it as officially provided, including telephonic notice “which telephonic notice shall be made when practicable before the opening of each such exchange,” and “To the National Association of Securities Dealers, Inc. (“NASD”) if such class of the subject company's securities is authorized for quotation in the NASDAQ interdealer quotation system.” Quote that clause as written. Official (b) and official (c) exist; this lesson does not build those engines. This is not a substitute for already-taught 14d-2", "Schedule TO may be filed at any time after commencement, and official (b) amendments are packed as this item"]} answer={2} explanation="Rule 14d-3 requires Schedule TO to be filed and transmitted as soon as practicable on the date of commencement if the bidder would beneficially own more than 5 percent after the tender. Quote the still-printed NASD / NASDAQ clause as written; do not invent a clock or zone, and do not treat this as a substitute for already-taught 14d-2." />
<Quiz id="dc-13e1" question="Under Rule 13e-1, may an issuer that has received notice of a commenced 14(d)(1) tender purchase its equity during the tender, and does the official core text name a form?" options={["The issuer may purchase freely once 14d-2 has commenced; this is already-taught Rule 14e-5", "The official core text requires a named form, or this is Schedule 13E-3 / Rule 13e-3", "An issuer that has received notice that it is the subject of a tender offer made under Section 14(d)(1) that has commenced under § 240.14d-2 must not purchase any of its equity securities during the tender offer unless the issuer first files a statement containing official (a)(1)–(7) information, pays the fee required by § 240.0-11, and submits the official (a)(7) exhibit. The official core text requires “a statement,” not a named form; do not invent a form number, and do not flatten this into Schedule 13E-3 or Rule 13e-3. Official citation is § 240.13e-1. Official (d) does not apply to periodic repurchases in connection with an employee benefit plan or other similar plan so long as the purchases are made in the ordinary course and not in response to the tender offer. Official (a)(7) / (b) / (c) exist; this lesson does not build the fee-table engine. This is not already-taught 14e-5 or 14d-2", "Official (d) does not exist; every employee-benefit-plan repurchase during the tender is prohibited"]} answer={2} explanation="Rule 13e-1 prohibits issuer purchases of its equity during a commenced 14(d)(1) third-party tender unless the issuer first files a statement, pays the fee, and submits the exhibit. The official core text requires “a statement,” not a named form. Official (d) excepts ordinary-course employee-benefit-plan repurchases that are not in response to the tender. This is not already-taught 14e-5, 14d-2, or Rule 13e-3." />
<Quiz id="dc-14a9" question="Under 17 CFR 240.14a-9, what solicitation may not be made, and is official typography “in the light of the circumstances under which it is made”? Is this already-taught Rule 14a-8 or Form 8-K Item 5.07?" options={["Official typography is “in the light of the circumstances under which they were made,” the same as packed 10b-5", "This is a flatten into already-taught Rule 14a-8 or Form 8-K Item 5.07, or this lesson builds official (c) or the Note catalog", "No solicitation subject to this regulation shall be made by means of any proxy statement, form of proxy, notice of meeting or other communication, written or oral, containing any statement which, at the time and in the light of the circumstances under which it is made, is false or misleading with respect to any material fact, or which omits to state any material fact necessary in order to make the statements therein not false or misleading or necessary to correct any statement in any earlier communication with respect to the solicitation of a proxy for the same meeting or subject matter which has become false or misleading. Official caption is “False or misleading statements.” Official typography is “in the light of the circumstances under which it is made” (singular “it is made”). Official (a) is “notice of meeting or other communication, written or oral.” Cite 17 CFR 240.14a-9. Official (b), official (c), and the Note exist and are named only; this lesson does not build official (c) or the Note catalog. This is not already-taught Rule 14a-8 or Form 8-K Item 5.07. Learning only; no issuer or voting advice", "Official (a) covers only written proxy statements, not a “notice of meeting or other communication, written or oral”"]} answer={2} explanation="17 CFR 240.14a-9 bars a solicitation made by a proxy statement, form of proxy, notice of meeting or other communication, written or oral, that is false or misleading in the light of the circumstances under which it is made. Official typography is singular “it is made,” not packed 10b-5’s “they were made.” Official (b), official (c), and the Note are named only. This is not already-taught Rule 14a-8 or Form 8-K Item 5.07." />
<Quiz id="dc-12b20" question="Under 17 CFR 240.12b-20, what further information must be added to a statement or report? Does official typography have a comma after “made”? Is this packed 14a-9 or 10b-5?" options={["Official typography is “under which they were made,” the same as packed 10b-5, or “under which it is made,” the same as packed 14a-9, and there is a comma after “made”", "This is a rewrite of packed 14a-9, packed 10b-5, already-taught Regulation FD, Form 8-K Item 5.07, or 14a-8", "In addition to the information expressly required to be included in a statement or report, there shall be added such further material information, if any, as may be necessary to make the required statements, in the light of the circumstances under which they are made not misleading. Official caption is “Additional information.” Official typography is “in the light of the circumstances under which they are made not misleading” (no comma after “made”). Cite 17 CFR 240.12b-20. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of is 2026-11-09. A LII wrap space is not official typography. This is not packed 14a-9 or 10b-5, and not already-taught Regulation FD, Item 5.07, or 14a-8", "This rule invents a form-specific clock, or a scienter / materiality / judicial-opinion catalog"]} answer={2} explanation="§ 240.12b-20 requires such further material information, if any, as may be necessary to make the required statements, in the light of the circumstances under which they are made not misleading. Official typography has no comma after “made.” Do not tidy that into packed 10b-5’s “they were made” or packed 14a-9’s “it is made.” This is not packed 14a-9 or 10b-5." />
<Quiz id="dc-13a14" question="Under 17 CFR 240.13a-14(a)/(c), what certifications must a Form 10-Q, Form 10-K, Form 20-F or Form 40-F report include, who must sign them, and may a required certification be signed under a power of attorney?
