Is official typography “Form 10-Q, Form 10-K, Form 20-F or Form 40-F”?" options={["This is a rewrite of already-taught Form 10-K / 10-Q clocks, packed 12b-20, already-taught Form 20-F / Form 6-K clocks, or Form 8-K Item 4.02", "A required certification may be signed on his or her behalf pursuant to a power of attorney, and official typography uses an Oxford comma before “or Form 40-F”", "Each report, including transition reports, filed on Form 10-Q, Form 10-K, Form 20-F or Form 40-F under Section 13(a) of the Act, other than a report filed by an Asset-Backed Issuer or a report on Form 20-F filed under § 240.13a-19, must include certifications in the form specified in the applicable exhibit filing requirements of such report and such certifications must be filed as an exhibit to such report. Each principal executive and principal financial officer of the issuer, or persons performing similar functions, at the time of filing of the report must sign a certification. Official (c): a person required to provide a certification specified in paragraph (a), (b) or (d) may not have the certification signed on his or her behalf pursuant to a power of attorney or other form of confirming authority. Official caption typography is “Certification of disclosure in annual and quarterly reports.” Official typography is “Form 10-Q, Form 10-K, Form 20-F or Form 40-F” (no Oxford comma). Cite 17 CFR 240.13a-14. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of is 2026-11-09. Official (b) 18 U.S.C. 1350 furnished certifications, official (d)/(e) ABS, official (f) Interactive Data File, § 240.13a-15 / § 240.15d-15, and 17 CFR 240.15d-14 exist and are named only; official (b) and 15d-14 are not the teaching core. This is not already-taught 10-K / 10-Q clocks, packed 12b-20, or Form 8-K Item 4.02", "This rule invents a certification form number and a filing clock, and official (b) must be packed as a second core"]} answer={2} explanation="§ 240.13a-14(a) requires certifications on Form 10-Q, Form 10-K, Form 20-F or Form 40-F to be filed as an exhibit and signed by the principal executive and principal financial officer. Official typography has no Oxford comma. Official (c) bars a power of attorney or other form of confirming authority. Official (b) and 17 CFR 240.15d-14 are named only. This is not packed 12b-20 or already-taught 10-K / 10-Q clocks." />
<Quiz id="dc-10d1" question="Under 17 CFR 240.10D-1(b)(1), what written policy must an issuer adopt and comply with? Does official typography say “recover reasonably promptly”? May a numeric clock be invented for that phrase, or a space inserted into official “of26”?" options={["This is a rewrite of already-taught Form 8-K Item 4.02, packed 12b-20, packed 13a-14, or already-taught 10-K / 10-Q clocks", "“Reasonably promptly” means a numbered day count, and official typography is “the requirements of 26 U.S.C. 401(a)(13)” with a space after “of”", "The issuer must adopt and comply with a written policy providing that the issuer will recover reasonably promptly the amount of erroneously awarded incentive-based compensation in the event that the issuer is required to prepare an accounting restatement due to the material noncompliance of the issuer with any financial reporting requirement under the securities laws, including any required accounting restatement to correct an error in previously issued financial statements that is material to the previously issued financial statements, or that would result in a material misstatement if the error were corrected in the current period or left uncorrected in the current period. Official caption typography is “Listing standards relating to recovery of erroneously awarded compensation.” Official typography is “recover reasonably promptly.” Cite 17 CFR 240.10D-1. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of is 2026-11-09. Official (a) 2023 transition machinery, official (b)(1)(i)–(v) including “An issuer's obligation to recover erroneously awarded compensation is not dependent on if or when the restated financial statements are filed,” official (c) exemptions, and official (d) definitions exist and are named only. Official (b)(1)(iv)(C) typography is “the requirements of26 U.S.C. 401(a)(13)” with no space after “of.” Do not invent a numeric clock for “reasonably promptly.” This is not already-taught Form 8-K Item 4.02, packed 12b-20, packed 13a-14, or already-taught 10-K / 10-Q clocks", "Official (a) 2023 transition dates are a current open clock, and official (b)(1)(i)–(v) must be built as a catalog"]} answer={2} explanation="§ 240.10D-1(b)(1)’s opening sentence requires a written policy to recover reasonably promptly the amount of erroneously awarded incentive-based compensation after an accounting restatement of the official kind. Do not invent a numeric clock for “reasonably promptly,” and do not insert a space into official “of26.” Official (a), official (b)(1)(i)–(v), official (c), and official (d) are named only. This is not Form 8-K Item 4.02 or packed 13a-14." />

<Quiz id="dc-244100" question="Under 17 CFR 244.100(a), what must accompany a public non-GAAP financial measure? What is official (a)(1) and official (b) typography? What typography does official Note 1 use for “Web cast,” “Web site,” and “web site”? Is official (b) packed 10b-5 / 12b-20 “in the light of”?" options={["Official (b) typography is “in the light of the circumstances under which they are presented, not misleading,” the same as packed 10b-5 / 12b-20", "This is a flatten into already-taught earnings / 10-K / 10-Q clocks, packed 13a-14, packed 12b-20, or packed 10D-1, or official Note 1 may be silently renumbered to official (a)(1)/(a)(2), or “Web cast” / “Web site” / “web site” may be tidied into “website” or “webcast”", "Whenever a registrant, or person acting on its behalf, publicly discloses material information that includes a non-GAAP financial measure, the registrant must accompany that non-GAAP financial measure with official (a)(1) a presentation of the most directly comparable financial measure calculated and presented in accordance with Generally Accepted Accounting Principles (GAAP), and official (a)(2) a reconciliation. Official (a)(1) typography uses initial capitals; official (c)(2) later uses lowercase “generally accepted accounting principles in the United States,” and the two are not tidied into each other. Official (b) typography is “in light of the circumstances under which it is presented, not misleading,” not packed 10b-5 / 12b-20 “in the light of,” and not “they are presented.” Official Note 1 typography is “by Web cast” and cites “paragraphs (a)(1)(i) and (a)(1)(ii)” even though official (a) is numbered (a)(1)/(a)(2). Official Note 1(i) is “the registrant's Web site”; official Note 1(ii) is “the location of the web site”; official Note 2(iii) is “web sites.” Official (b)/(c)/(d) and 17 CFR 244.101 / 244.102 and Regulation S-K Item 10(e) / 17 CFR 229.10 are named only. This is not already-taught earnings / 10-K / 10-Q clocks, packed 13a-14, packed 12b-20, or packed 10D-1. Title 17 is up to date as of 2026-11-09", "A non-GAAP measure may be published without the comparable GAAP measure if a qualitative discussion is supplied, and official (d) typography invents an Oxford comma"]} answer={2} explanation="17 CFR 244.100 packs official (a) only: accompany a public non-GAAP measure with the most directly comparable GAAP measure (“Generally Accepted Accounting Principles (GAAP)”) and a reconciliation. Official (b) typography is “in light of the circumstances under which it is presented, not misleading.” Official Note 1 typography is “by Web cast” and cites “paragraphs (a)(1)(i) and (a)(1)(ii).” Official Note 1(i) is “the registrant's Web site”; official Note 1(ii) is “the location of the web site.” Title 17 is up to date as of 2026-11-09." />

<Quiz id="dc-14a3" question="Under 17 CFR 240.14a-3(b), when must a proxy statement be accompanied or preceded by an annual report to security holders? Is that annual report the same filing as the Form 10-K? Does every proxy mailing require an annual report?" options={["Every proxy mailing requires an annual report, and that report is the same filing as the Form 10-K", "This is a flatten into packed 14a-9, packed 12b-20, already-taught 14a-8, Form 8-K Item 5.07, already-taught 10-K / 10-Q clocks, packed 13a-14, or packed 244.100", "Official (b) opening: if the solicitation is made on behalf of the registrant, other than an investment company registered under the Investment Company Act of 1940, and relates to an annual (or special meeting in lieu of the annual) meeting of security holders, or written consent in lieu of such meeting, at which directors are to be elected, each proxy statement furnished pursuant to paragraph (a) of this section shall be accompanied or preceded by an annual report to security holders as follows: Official caption typography is “Information to be furnished to security holders.” Official typography is two-word “security holders” and “accompanied or preceded.” Official (a), official (b)(1) and later, including the financial-statement catalog, official typo “this paragraph 9(b)(1)” if quoted, official 14a-3(e) householding, and 17 CFR 240.14a-4 / 14a-6 are named only. Do not tidy the official typo if quoted. The annual report to security holders is not the same filing as the Form 10-K. Official (b) does not say that every proxy mailing requires an annual report. Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-09. This is not packed 14a-9 / 12b-20 / 13a-14 / 244.100, already-taught 14a-8, or Form 8-K Item 5.07", "Official (b) restates the Form 10-K clock, and official typography is one-word “securityholders”"]} answer={2} explanation="17 CFR 240.14a-3(b) packs official (b) opening only, through “as follows:.” For that official (b) meeting, the annual report to security holders accompanies or precedes the proxy statement. It is not the Form 10-K, and not every proxy mailing requires an annual report. Official (a), official (b)(1) and later, and 14a-4 / 14a-6 are named only. Title 17 is up to date as of 2026-11-09." />

<Quiz id="dc-14c3" question="Under 17 CFR 240.14c-3(a), when must an information statement be accompanied or preceded by an annual report to security holders? Is official caption typography “Annual report to be furnished to security holders.”? Is that annual report the Form 10-K? Does every information statement require an annual report?" options={["Every information statement requires an annual report, official caption typography includes “to” before “security holders,” and that report is the same filing as the Form 10-K", "This is a flatten into packed 14a-3(b), packed 14a-9, packed 12b-20, already-taught 14a-8, Form 8-K Item 5.07, already-taught 10-K / 10-Q clocks, packed 13a-14, or packed 244.100, or packing 14c-3(c) householding as a new core", "Official (a) opening: if the information statement relates to an annual (or special meeting in lieu of the annual) meeting, or written consent in lieu of such meeting, of security holders at which directors of the registrant, other than an investment company registered under the Investment Company Act of 1940, are to be elected, it shall be accompanied or preceded by an annual report to security holders: Official caption typography is “Annual report to be furnished security holders.” — no “to” before “security holders,” and a period after “holders.” Official typography is two-word “security holders” and “accompanied or preceded.” Official (a) word order is not identical to packed 14a-3(b). Official (a)(1)/(a)(2)/(b)/(c)/(d), 14c-2, and 14c-5 are named only. Packing 14c-3(c) householding as a new core remains wrong. Official (a) does not say that every information statement requires an annual report. The annual report to security holders is not the same filing as the Form 10-K. If official (b) is quoted, official typographic quotes appear around “filed.” Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-09. This is not packed 14a-3(b) / 14a-9 / 12b-20 / 13a-14 / 244.100, already-taught 14a-8, or Form 8-K Item 5.07", "Official (a) restates packed 14a-3(b), and official typography is one-word “securityholders”"]} answer={2} explanation="17 CFR 240.14c-3(a) packs official (a) opening only, through “security holders:.” Official caption typography is “Annual report to be furnished security holders.” with no “to” before “security holders.” Official (a) is limited to that official meeting or written consent at which directors of the registrant, other than a registered investment company, are to be elected. It is not packed 14a-3(b), and not the Form 10-K. Title 17 is up to date as of 2026-11-09." />
<Quiz id="dc-14c6" question="Under 17 CFR 240.14c-6, what may an information statement not contain, and what does official (b) say about Commission filing or examination? Is official (a) typography “with respect to the same meeting or subject matter”? Does official 14c-6 have an official (c) or a Note?" options={["Official (a) typography is packed 14a-9(a)’s “with respect to the solicitation of a proxy for the same meeting or subject matter,” official (b) typography is packed 14a-9(b)’s “a proxy statement, form of proxy or other soliciting material,” or official 14c-6 has an official (c) or a Note catalog", "This is a flatten into packed 14a-9, packed 14c-3(a), packed 12b-20, packed 10b-5, already-taught 14a-8, or Form 8-K Item 5.07, or a paste of packed 14a-9 over this rule", "Official caption typography is “False or misleading statements.” Official (a): no information statement shall contain any statement which, at the time and in the light of the circumstances under which it is made, is false or misleading with respect to any material fact, or which omits to state any material fact necessary in order to make the statements therein not false or misleading or necessary to correct any statement in any earlier communication with respect to the same meeting or subject matter which has become false or misleading. Official typography is “in the light of the circumstances under which it is made” (singular “it is made”). Official typography is “any earlier communication with respect to the same meeting or subject matter,” not packed 14a-9(a)’s “with respect to the solicitation of a proxy for the same meeting or subject matter.” Official (a) is limited to “No information statement shall contain.” Official (b): the fact that an information statement has been filed with or examined by the Commission shall not be deemed a finding by the Commission that such material is accurate or complete or not false or misleading, or that the Commission has passed upon the merits of or approved any statement contained therein or any matter to be acted upon by security holders. No representation contrary to the foregoing shall be made. Official typography is “an information statement has been filed with or examined by the Commission,” not packed 14a-9(b)’s “a proxy statement, form of proxy or other soliciting material.” Official typography is “security holders.” Official 14c-6 has no official (c) and no Note. Official source line: “[31 FR 262, Jan. 8, 1966].” Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-09. Packed 14a-9 / 14c-3(a) / 12b-20 / 10b-5, leftover 14c-2 / 14c-5, and 14c-4 are named only", "Official (a) typography is packed 10b-5’s “under which they were made,” or packed 12b-20’s “under which they are made not misleading”"]} answer={2} explanation="17 CFR 240.14c-6 packs official (a) and official (b), the whole current section. Official (a) typography is “with respect to the same meeting or subject matter,” not packed 14a-9(a)’s proxy-solicitation phrasing. Official (b) typography is “an information statement.” Official 14c-6 has no official (c) and no Note. Title 17 is up to date as of 2026-11-09." />
<Quiz id="dc-15c19" question="Under 17 CFR 240.15c1-9, when is the use of a proceeds pro forma financial statement a manipulative, deceptive, or other fraudulent device or contrivance? Is official caption typography two-word “pro forma”? Does official typography state a numeric point size?" options={["Official typography hyphenates “pro-forma,” states a numeric point size, puts quotation marks around the defined term, or invents a space in official 15 U.S.C. 78o", "This is a flatten into packed 244.100, packed 12b-20, packed 14c-6, packed 15c1-3, or already-taught 10-K / 10-Q clocks, or a claim that 15c1-9 and packed 244.100 are the same rule", "The term manipulative, deceptive, or other fraudulent device or contrivance, as used in section 15(c)(1) of the Act, is hereby defined to include the use of financial statements purporting to give effect to the receipt and application of any part of the proceeds from the sale or exchange of securities, unless the assumptions upon which each such financial statement is based are clearly set forth as part of the caption to each such statement in type at least as large as that used generally in the body of the statement. Official caption typography is “Use of pro forma balance sheets.” Official typography is two-word “pro forma” (no hyphen). Official typography italicizes the defined term, including the trailing comma, and does not put quotation marks around it. Official typography is “as part of the caption to each such statement in type at least as large as that used generally in the body of the statement” (no numeric point size). Official authority typography is 15 U.S.C. 78o with an italic letter-O; do not invent a space in that cite. Official source line: “[13 FR 8205, Dec. 22, 1948].” Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-09. Packed 244.100 / 244.101 / 244.102 and Regulation S-K Item 10(e) / 17 CFR 229.10, packed 15c1-3, leftover 15c1-8, and 15c1-1 are named only", "A proceeds pro forma statement may omit caption assumptions if a footnote uses smaller type"]} answer={2} explanation="17 CFR 240.15c1-9 packs the whole current section. Official caption typography is two-word “pro forma.” Official typography italicizes the defined term, including the trailing comma, and does not put quotation marks around it. Official typography has no numeric point size. Official authority typography is 15 U.S.C. 78o with an italic letter-O. Title 17 is up to date as of 2026-11-09." />
<Quiz id="dc-12b15" question="Under 17 CFR 240.12b-15, how must an amendment be marked and filed, and what typography does official text use around “A” and “10-K/A,”? Must 13a-14(a), 13a-14(b), and 13a-14(d) amendment-certification verbs be kept distinct? Is “10-K/A” the only amendment form?" options={["“10-K/A” is the only amendment form, the comma sits outside “10-K/A”, or official 13a-14(a) / (b) / (d) amendment-certification verbs may be tidied into one another", "This is a flatten into packed 13a-14, already-taught Form 10-K / 10-Q clocks, the already-printed XBRL filed-date box, packed 12b-20, packed 14c-6, or packed 244.100, a how-to for preparing an amendment, or a Regulation S-T filing engine", "All amendments must be filed under cover of the form amended, marked with the letter “A” to designate the document as an amendment, e.g., “10-K/A,” and in compliance with pertinent requirements applicable to statements and reports. Amendments filed pursuant to this section must set forth the complete text of each item as amended. Amendments must be numbered sequentially and be filed separately for each statement or report amended. Official typography uses typographic quotes (U+201C / U+201D) around “A” and around “10-K/A,” with the comma inside the closing quote. Official typography is “e.g.,” (comma after e.g.). Official 13a-14(a) amendments “must include new certifications,” official 13a-14(b) amendments “must be accompanied by new certifications,” and official 13a-14(d) amendments “must include a new certification” — do not tidy those three official phrasings into one another. Official caption typography is “Amendments.” Official source line: “[68 FR 36665, June 18, 2003, as amended at 70 FR 1620, Jan. 7, 2005].” Title 17 `up_to_date_as_of` 2026-11-09. “10-K/A” is not the only amendment form. The electronic one-copy sentence is in the packed slice; 17 CFR 232.309 may be named only. The already-printed XBRL box, packed 13a-14, and already-taught Form 10-K / 10-Q clocks are named only", "An amendment may restate only the changed sentence, and paper and electronic filers must file the same number of copies"]} answer={2} explanation="17 CFR 240.12b-15 packs the whole current section. Official typography uses typographic quotes around “A” and around “10-K/A,” with the comma inside the closing quote. Official 13a-14(a) / (b) / (d) amendment-certification verbs are not the same and must not be tidied. “10-K/A” is an example, not the only amendment form. Title 17 is up to date as of 2026-11-09." />
<Quiz id="dc-12b6" question="Under 17 CFR 240.12b-6, when is a class of securities deemed registered for sections 13, 14, 15(d) and 16 of the act?" options={["It is deemed registered as soon as the section 12 registration statement is filed, and official typography puts 15(d) in both lists.", "This is packed 12b-15 amendment typography, or the already-taught Form 10-K / 10-Q clock starts at filing.", "Only when the section 12 registration statement has become effective as provided in section 12. Official typography is “sections 13, 14, 15(d) and 16 of the act” in the first list (no Oxford comma) and “sections 13, 14 and 16 of the act” in the second list (no 15(d)). Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Section 16 clocks start at filing, but sections 13 and 14 wait until the first 10-K is filed."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 12b-6 deems the class registered, and subjects it to sections 13, 14 and 16, only when the section 12 statement has become effective. The official second list has no 15(d). This is not packed 12b-15 and not the already-taught 10-K / 10-Q clock." />
<Quiz id="dc-23212" question="Under 17 CFR 232.12, when may electronic filings be submitted, and is that the already-taught 232.13 5:30 p.m. filing-date cutoff?" options={["The official electronic window ends at 5:30 p.m. Eastern, the same cutoff already taught in Rule 13 / 232.13.", "232.12 says every electronic filing submitted by 10 p.m. is deemed filed that same business day, and official typography is “Eastern Daylight Savings Time.”", "Official 232.12(c) allows electronic filings from 6 a.m. to 10 p.m. Eastern, except Saturdays, Sundays, and Federal holidays. That transmission window is not the already-taught 232.13 5:30 p.m. filing-date cutoff. Official typography is “Eastern Daylight Saving Time.” Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "The Commission’s office hours in 232.12(a), 9 a.m. to 5:30 p.m., are also the only hours when an electronic filing may be transmitted."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 232.12(c) is a 6 a.m. to 10 p.m. transmission window. The 5:30 p.m. filing-date cutoff, and the 10 p.m. same-day deeming for specified forms, are already-taught 232.13, which this box does not rewrite. Official typography is “Saving,” not “Savings.”" />
<Quiz id="dc-16a6" question="Under 17 CFR 240.16a-6, when may a small acquisition be reported on Form 5, and what happens if it no longer qualifies?" options={["An acquisition from the issuer or from an employee benefit plan sponsored by the issuer also qualifies, and official 16a-6(b) typography is “within two business days.”", "This is the already-taught Form 4 two-business-day clock, packed Section 16(b), or packed 13d-2.", "A qualifying acquisition — not from the issuer, including not from an employee benefit plan sponsored by the issuer — not exceeding $10,000 in market value under official (a)(1)–(2) shall be reported on Form 5. If it no longer qualifies, official (b) requires a Form 4 before the end of the second business day following the day on which the paragraph (a) conditions are no longer met. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Every acquisition under $10,000 is exempt from section 16 reporting entirely, and no Form 5 is due."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 16a-6(a) carves out acquisitions from the issuer, including an employee benefit plan sponsored by the issuer. Official (b) typography is “before the end of the second business day following,” not “within two business days.” This is not the already-taught Form 4 / Form 5 clock, packed 16(b), or packed 13d-2." />
<Quiz id="dc-16a9" question="Under 17 CFR 240.16a-9, what is exempt from section 16, and does the official Note also exempt the exercise or sale of a pro rata right from reporting?" options={["The official Note also exempts the exercise or sale of a pro rata right from section 16 reporting.", "This is already-taught split / dividend price adjustment, packed 16a-6, or packed Section 16(b).", "The increase or decrease in the number of securities from a stock split or an equal stock dividend, and the acquisition of rights pursuant to a pro rata grant to all holders of the same section 12 class, are exempt from section 16. The official Note says the exercise or sale of a pro rata right shall be reported pursuant to § 240.16a-4. Official caption typography uses an Oxford comma. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Only cash dividends are exempt, and a stock dividend of a different issuer is never within official (a)."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 16a-9(a)–(b) are the exemption. The official Note, which is part of the packed slice, requires the exercise or sale of a pro rata right to be reported under 16a-4. This is not the already-taught price-adjustment box." />
<Quiz id="dc-16a11" question="Under 17 CFR 240.16a-11, when is a dividend or interest reinvestment acquisition exempt from section 16, and is every DRIP exempt?" options={["Every dividend reinvestment plan is automatically exempt from section 16.", "This is already-taught tax DRIP / Pub 550 / Treas. Reg. 1.1012-1(e), packed 16a-6, or packed 16a-9.", "An acquisition from the reinvestment of dividends or interest on securities of the same issuer is exempt if the plan provides for regular reinvestment, broad-based participation, does not discriminate in favor of employees of the issuer, and operates on substantially the same terms for all plan participants. Not every DRIP is exempt. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "The exemption covers reinvestment of dividends of a different issuer, and official typography has no same-issuer limit."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 16a-11 is a Section 16 exemption with those conditions, limited to securities of the same issuer. It is not the already-taught tax-DRIP basis rule, and it is not automatic for every plan." />
<Quiz id="dc-14e8" question="Under 17 CFR 240.14e-8, what public announcement of a not-yet-commenced tender offer is a fraudulent, deceptive or manipulative act or practice, and is a numeric clock stated for “a reasonable time”?" options={["This is already-taught 14e-1 duration, 14e-2’s 10-business-day position statement, or 14d-2 commencement at 12:01 a.m.", "Official typography uses an Oxford comma in “fraudulent, deceptive, or manipulative,” and official (a) states a five-business-day clock for “a reasonable time.”", "It is a section 14(e) fraudulent, deceptive or manipulative act or practice (no Oxford comma) to publicly announce a tender offer that has not yet been commenced if official (a), (b), or (c) applies: no intention to commence within a reasonable time and complete the offer; intent to manipulate the market price of the bidder or subject company stock; or no reasonable belief that the person will have the means to complete the offer. Official text states no numeric clock for “a reasonable time.” Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Leftover 14e-6 / 14e-7 already pack this pre-commencement announcement duty, so 14e-8 is only a pointer."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 14e-8 is the pre-commencement announcement rule, with (a)–(c) as written and no numeric clock for “a reasonable time.” It is not packed 14e-1, 14e-2, 14d-2, or leftover 14e-6 / 14e-7." />
<Quiz id="dc-12b4" question="Under 17 CFR 240.12b-4, is staff-requested supplemental information required to be filed, and when must a return request be made?" options={["This information is filed with and deemed part of the registration statement or report.", "This is packed 12b-20, packed 12b-15, packed 12b-6, or packed 232.12.", "Staff-requested supplemental information shall not be required to be filed with or deemed part of the registration statement or report. A return request must be made at the time such information is furnished to the staff; official (b) ends with “; and”; official (c) cites the Freedom of Information Act (5 U.S.C. 552). Official caption typography is “Supplemental information.” Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "A return request may be made at any time before the registration statement or report becomes effective."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 12b-4 says this information shall not be required to be filed with or deemed part of the registration statement or report. Official (a) typography is “made at the time such information is furnished to the staff.” Official (c) cites “the Freedom of Information Act (5 U.S.C. 552).” This is not packed 12b-20, 12b-15, 12b-6, or 232.12." />
<Quiz id="dc-12b23" question="Under 17 CFR 240.12b-23, when may information be incorporated by reference, and what typography does official (d) use around “EDGAR”?" options={["Official (d) typography is “The registrant must include an active hyperlink,” and official typography uses straight quotes around EDGAR.", "This is the already-printed XBRL filed-date box, packed 12b-20, packed 12b-15, packed 12b-6, or packed 232.12.", "Information may be incorporated by reference except as provided by this section or in the appropriate form. Official (d) typography is “You must include an active hyperlink,” with typographic quotes around “EDGAR.” Official (e) forbids chain incorporation unless expressly permitted or required. Official (a)–(e) italic lead-ins include the trailing periods. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official (b) may be tidied to a bare “GAAP / IFRS,” and chain incorporation from a second document that itself incorporates from a third document is always allowed."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 12b-23 packs official (a)+(b)+(c)+(d)+(e). Official (d) typography is second-person “You must include an active hyperlink,” with typographic quotes around “EDGAR.” Official (e) typography is “unless expressly permitted or required.” Italic lead-ins include the trailing periods. This is not the already-printed XBRL box or packed 12b-20 / 12b-15 / 12b-6 / 232.12." />
<Quiz id="dc-13a11" question="Under 17 CFR 240.13a-11, when is Form 8-K due, and is a failure to file every item on official (c)’s list deemed a 10b-5 violation?" options={["Official 13a-11 itself states a four-business-day clock, and a failure to file an 8-K required solely pursuant to Item 1.01, 1.02, 1.05, 2.03, 2.04, 2.05, 2.06, 4.02(a), 5.02(e), or 6.03 shall be deemed to be a violation of 15 U.S.C. 78j(b) and § 240.10b-5.", "This is already-taught Form 8-K item clocks, Form 8-K Item 5.07, or packed 14e-8.", "Official (a) requires the 8-K within the period specified in that form unless substantially the same information has been previously reported by the registrant. Official typography is that no failure to file an 8-K required solely pursuant to Item 1.01, 1.02, 1.05, 2.03, 2.04, 2.05, 2.06, 4.02(a), 5.02(e), or 6.03 shall be deemed to be a violation of 15 U.S.C. 78j(b) and § 240.10b-5. Official 13a-11 itself states no numeric clock. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official (c) also lists Item 2.02, Item 5.07, and a bare 4.02 / 5.02 in that carve-out."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 13a-11(a) uses the period specified in that form, with a previously-reported exception, and does not invent a numeric clock. Official (c) typography is that no such failure shall be deemed to be a violation of 15 U.S.C. 78j(b) and § 240.10b-5. The official item list does not add Item 2.02, Item 5.07, or a bare 4.02 / 5.02. This is not already-taught 8-K clocks, Item 5.07, or packed 14e-8." />
<Quiz id="dc-16a4" question="Under 17 CFR 240.16a-4, how are derivative securities reported, and is the official Note part of the packed slice?" options={["Official typography italicizes “except that,” including a trailing comma inside the italics, the same typography as packed 15c1-3 / 15c1-9.", "This is already-taught Form 4 transaction codes, packed 16a-6, packed 16a-9, packed 16a-11, or packed Section 16(b).", "Derivative securities and the underlying shall be deemed to be the same class, except that the acquisition or disposition of any derivative security shall be separately reported. Official typography italicizes “except that” with no trailing comma inside the italics. The exercise or conversion of a call equivalent position is reported as a purchase of the underlying and a closing of the derivative; a put equivalent position is a sale of the underlying and a closing of the derivative. The official Note is part of the packed slice and names leftover 16b-3 / 16b-6(b) only. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Leftover 16b-3 / 16b-6(b) / 16b-6(d) are packed as second cores in this box."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 16a-4 packs the whole current section, including the official Note. Official (a) italicizes “except that” with no trailing comma inside the italics. Call equivalent positions are a purchase plus a closing; put equivalent positions are a sale plus a closing. Leftover 16b-3 / 16b-6(b) / 16b-6(d) are named only. This is not already-taught Form 4 codes or packed 16a-6 / 16a-9 / 16a-11 / Section 16(b)." />
<Quiz id="dc-16a10" question="Under 17 CFR 240.16a-10, what is the official 16(a)→16(b) exemption linkage, and is 16a-6 also a 16(b) exemption under this section?" options={["Official typography is “also reported,” or this rule is a Form 5 deferral, and official text has no 16a-6 carve-out.", "This is packed 16a-6, packed 16a-9, packed 16a-11, or packed Section 16(b).", "Except as provided in § 240.16a-6, any transaction exempted from the requirements of section 16(a) of the Act, insofar as it is otherwise subject to the provisions of section 16(b), shall be likewise exempt from section 16(b) of the Act. 16a-6 is the official carve-out, not a 16(b) exemption under 16a-10. The official API XML has no source line. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official 16a-9 / 16a-11 need 16a-10 to become 16(b)-exempt, because those rules exempt only section 16(a)."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 16a-10 typography opens “Except as provided in § 240.16a-6” and uses “likewise exempt.” 16a-6 is the official carve-out, not a 16(b) exemption under 16a-10. Do not invent a source line. This is not packed 16a-6 / 16a-9 / 16a-11 / Section 16(b)." />
<Quiz id="dc-16a13" question="Under 17 CFR 240.16a-13, what form-only change is exempt, and is the exemption limited to section 16(a)?" options={["The exemption is from section 16(a) only, and official typography also exempts the exercise or conversion of a derivative security and a deposit into or withdrawal from a voting trust.", "This is already-taught Form 4 transaction codes, packed 16a-6, packed 16a-9, or packed 16a-11.", "A transaction that effects only a change in the form of beneficial ownership without changing a person's pecuniary interest in the subject equity securities shall be exempt from section 16 of the Act, other than the exercise or conversion of a derivative security or deposit into or withdrawal from a voting trust. Official typography is “section 16 of the Act,” not section 16(a) only, and “the subject equity securities.” Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Any internal account transfer is an economic Form 4 trade, even if pecuniary interest does not change."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 16a-13 exempts a form-only change from section 16 of the Act, not only section 16(a), provided pecuniary interest in the subject equity securities does not change. Official typography carves out derivative-security exercise or conversion and voting-trust deposits or withdrawals. This is not already-taught Form 4 codes or packed 16a-6 / 16a-9 / 16a-11." />
<Quiz id="dc-12b10" question="Under 17 CFR 240.12b-10, what form must a statement or report use, and when must the Commission object to the form?" options={["The form that becomes effective only after the filing date must be used, and therefor must be tidied to therefore and thirty days to 30 days.", "This is packed 12b-15, packed 12b-25, packed 12b-4, or packed 12b-23.", "Every statement or report shall be on the form prescribed therefor by the Commission, as in effect on the date of filing. Any statement or report shall be deemed to be filed on the proper form unless objection to the form is made by the Commission within thirty days after the date of filing. Official typography is “therefor” and “thirty days,” not “therefore” or “30 days.” Official caption typography is “Requirements as to proper form.” Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Any format is proper if the Commission does not object within sixty days after filing."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 12b-10 requires the form prescribed therefor by the Commission, as in effect on the date of filing. A filing is deemed to be on the proper form unless the Commission objects within thirty days after the date of filing. Official typography is “therefor” and “thirty days.” This is not packed 12b-15, 12b-25, 12b-4, or 12b-23." />
<Quiz id="dc-12b21" question="Under 17 CFR 240.12b-21, how far must required information be given, does the official caption say reasonably, and what must still be disclosed if information is omitted?" options={["All required information must be given even if obtaining it would involve unreasonable effort or expense, and the official caption is Information reasonably unknown or not available.", "This is packed 12b-20, packed 12b-23, or packed 12b-4, and the official API XML has a CITA that must be copied.", "Information required need be given only insofar as it is known or reasonably available to the registrant. Official caption typography is “Information unknown or not available.” — the caption does not say “reasonably.” Official (a) still requires such information as the registrant possesses or can acquire without unreasonable effort or expense, together with the sources thereof, and official (b) requires a statement either showing that unreasonable effort or expense would be involved or indicating the absence of affiliation and the result of a request. The official API XML has no CITA; do not invent a source line. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "If the information rests peculiarly with another person, it may be omitted with no statement of sources or of any request."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 12b-21 requires information only insofar as it is known or reasonably available. The official caption does not say reasonably. An omission still requires the information the registrant has or can reasonably acquire, together with the sources thereof, and the official (b) statement. The official API XML has no CITA. This is not packed 12b-20, 12b-23, or 12b-4." />
<Quiz id="dc-13a1" question="Under 17 CFR 240.13a-1, when must a section 12 issuer file an annual report, and does 13a-1 itself state a 60/75/90-day clock?" options={["Official 13a-1 itself states a 60/75/90-day clock, and 15 U.S.C. 78l must be tidied to 781.", "This is already-taught Form 10-K clocks, packed 13a-11, or packed 13a-14.", "Every issuer having securities registered pursuant to section 12 of the Act shall file an annual report for each fiscal year after the last full fiscal year for which financial statements were filed in its registration statement. Official typography is “Annual reports shall be filed within the period specified in the appropriate form.” Official 13a-1 itself states no numeric clock. Official typography is 15 U.S.C. 78l with official italic on the letter l. Official 15d-1 is named only, and official 15d-1 caption typography is singular “Requirement of annual reports.” Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "The section 15(d) counterpart 15d-1 must be packed as a second core in this box, and official 15d-1 also uses the plural Requirements."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 13a-1 leaves the clock to the appropriate form and does not invent a 60/75/90-day number. Official typography italicizes the letter l in 15 U.S.C. 78l. Official 15d-1 is named only. This is not already-taught Form 10-K clocks, packed 13a-11, or packed 13a-14." />
<Quiz id="dc-13a13" question="Under 17 CFR 240.13a-13, what is the general Form 10-Q clock, and does the rule require a 10-Q for all four fiscal quarters?" options={["13a-13 requires a Form 10-Q for all four fiscal quarters, and the rule itself states a 40-day or 45-day general clock; the official Form 10-Q cite is § 249.308.", "This is already-taught Form 10-Q clocks, packed 13a-11, or the new 13a-1 box.", "Official (a) requires a Form 10-Q (§ 249.308a of this chapter) within the period specified in General Instruction A.1. to that form for each of the first three quarters of each fiscal year of the issuer. The only numeric clock inside the rule is the first-report 45-day alternative, and official typography is “has been required,” not “had been required.” Official (d) typography is that Part I financial information shall not be deemed to be “filed” for the purpose of Section 18 of the Act, but shall be subject to all other provisions of the Act. Official 15d-13 / 13a-17 are named only. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official (b) does not except foreign private issuers or asset-backed issuers, and official typography uses straight quotes around filed."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 13a-13 uses the General Instruction A.1. clock and covers only the first three quarters, not all four. The only numeric clock inside the rule is the first-report 45-day alternative, and official typography is has been required. The official Form 10-Q cite is § 249.308a. Official (d) uses typographic quotes around “filed.” This is not already-taught Form 10-Q clocks, packed 13a-11, or the new 13a-1 box." />
<Quiz id="dc-243102" question="Under 17 CFR 243.102, is a failure to make a public disclosure required solely by § 243.100 deemed a violation of Rule 10b-5?" options={["A failure to make a public disclosure required solely by § 243.100 shall be deemed to be a violation of Rule 10b-5.", "This is already-taught Regulation FD clocks, packed 10b-5, packed 13a-11(c), or named 244.102.", "No failure to make a public disclosure required solely by § 243.100 shall be deemed to be a violation of Rule 10b-5 (17 CFR 240.10b-5) under the Securities Exchange Act. Official typography is “required solely by § 243.100,” and names both “Rule 10b-5” and “17 CFR 240.10b-5.” The official API XML has no CITA; do not invent a source line. Official caption typography is “No effect on antifraud liability.” Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official 243.102 rewrites the already-taught Regulation FD 24-hour / next-session clock, and this sentence says Securities Exchange Act of 1934."]} answer={2} explanation="Do not invert official 243.102: no failure to make a public disclosure required solely by § 243.100 shall be deemed to be a violation of Rule 10b-5 (17 CFR 240.10b-5). The official API XML has no CITA. This is not already-taught Regulation FD clocks, packed 10b-5, packed 13a-11(c), or named 244.102." />
<Quiz id="dc-16a12" question="Under 17 CFR 240.16a-12, how is an acquisition or disposition of equity securities pursuant to a domestic relations order treated, and is the exemption limited to section 16(a)?" options={["The exemption is from section 16(a) only, and the official definition is a bare ERISA QDRO.", "This is packed 16a-13, packed 16a-10, packed 16a-11, already-taught Form 4 codes, or only a Form 5 deferral.", "The acquisition or disposition of equity securities pursuant to a domestic relations order, as defined in the Internal Revenue Code or Title I of the Employee Retirement Income Security Act, or the rules thereunder, shall be exempt from section 16 of the Act. Official typography is “section 16 of the Act,” not section 16(a) only. Official source line: “[61 FR 30393, June 14, 1996].” This is the same official source line as packed 16a-13; do not invent different pages, and do not merge the two rules into one section. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "This rule rewrites the 16a-13 source page to a different FR page, so the two rules must be merged into one section."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 16a-12 exempts a qualifying domestic-relations-order acquisition or disposition from section 16 of the Act, not only section 16(a), using the official IRC / Title I ERISA definition. The official source line is the same as packed 16a-13, but the two rules stay in separate boxes. This is not packed 16a-13 / 16a-10 / 16a-11, already-taught Form 4 codes, or only a Form 5 deferral." />
<Quiz id="dc-23214" question="Under 17 CFR 232.14, will the Commission accept in paper format a filing required to be submitted electronically under Rules 100 and 101 of Regulation S-T, and what is the official typography of the hardship-exemption pair?" options={["The Commission will accept such a paper filing without an exemption, and official typography is §§ 232.201 and 232.202 respectively.", "This packs the 232.100 / 232.101 / 232.201 / 232.202 catalogs, or flattens 232.14 into packed 232.12 or already-taught 232.13.", "The Commission will not accept in paper format any filing required to be submitted electronically under Rules 100 and 101 of Regulation S-T (§§ 232.100 and 232.101 respectively), unless the filing satisfies the requirements for a temporary or continuing hardship exemption under Rule 201 or 202 of Regulation S-T (§§ 232.201 or 232.202 respectively). Official typography is “§§ 232.100 and 232.101 respectively” and “§§ 232.201 or 232.202 respectively” — the second pair uses “or,” not “and.” Official source line: “[62 FR 58649, Oct. 30, 1997].” Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official typography is §§ 232.201 and 232.202 respectively, and this rule rewrites already-taught 232.13."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 232.14 refuses paper format unless a 232.201 or 232.202 hardship exemption applies. The second official pair uses “or,” not “and.” 232.100 / 232.101 / 232.201 / 232.202 are named only. This is not packed 232.12 or already-taught 232.13." />
<Quiz id="dc-243103" question="Under 17 CFR 243.103, does a failure to make a public disclosure required solely by § 243.100 affect Exchange Act reporting status or Rule 144(c) current public information?" options={["A failure required solely by § 243.100 shall affect whether the issuer is deemed to have filed, and official typography is 239.16, not 239.16b.", "This is packed 243.102, already-taught Regulation FD clocks, packed 10b-5, or named 244.102.", "A failure to make a public disclosure required solely by § 243.100 shall not affect whether an issuer is deemed to have filed all the material required to be filed pursuant to Section 13 or 15(d), or where applicable, has made those filings in a timely manner, or whether there is adequate current public information about the issuer for purposes of § 230.144(c) (Rule 144(c)). Official typography is “239.16b,” not “239.16.” Official typography italicizes “et seq.” Official typography keeps the comma after “applicable.” Official source line: “[65 FR 51738, Aug. 24, 2000, as amended at 79 FR 57344, Sept. 24, 2014; 85 FR 33360, June 1, 2020].” Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official typography drops the comma after applicable, flattens et seq., and rewrites the already-taught Regulation FD box."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 243.103 says such a failure “shall not affect” Exchange Act reporting status or Rule 144(c) current public information. Do not invert that negation. Official typography is 239.16b, italic “et seq.,” and a comma after applicable. This is not packed 243.102, already-taught Regulation FD clocks, packed 10b-5, or named 244.102." />
<Quiz id="dc-15d1" question="Under 17 CFR 240.15d-1, when must annual reports be filed, and is the official clock the same as packed 13a-1’s “the appropriate form”?" options={["Annual reports shall be filed within 60, 75, or 90 days, and official typography is therefore, not therefor.", "This rewrites packed 13a-1, and the official caption is the same plural Requirements of annual reports.", "Every registrant under the Securities Act of 1933 shall file an annual report, on the appropriate form authorized or prescribed therefor, for the fiscal year in which the registration statement became effective and for each fiscal year thereafter, unless exempt by section 15(d) of the Act or rules thereunder. Official typography is “Annual reports shall be filed within the period specified in the appropriate report form.” Official typography is “therefor,” not “therefore.” Official caption typography is singular “Requirement of annual reports.” Official source line: “[47 FR 17052, Apr. 21, 1982, as amended at 61 FR 49960, Sept. 24, 1996].” Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official typography is the same as packed 13a-1’s the appropriate form, and the two CITAs may be tidied into one another."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 15d-1 uses “the appropriate report form,” not packed 13a-1’s “the appropriate form.” Do not invent a numeric clock, and do not tidy “therefor” into “therefore.” The official caption is singular. This box does not rewrite packed 13a-1." />
<Quiz id="dc-15d11" question="Under 17 CFR 240.15d-11, who must file a current report on Form 8-K, what does official (b) name, and is a failure to file the official (c) items deemed a 10b-5 violation?" options={["Every registrant subject to § 240.13a-1 shall file, official (b) names 13a-16, and a failure to file those items shall be deemed a 10b-5 violation.", "This is packed 13a-11, and the official CITA may be tidied into 13a-11’s source line.", "Except as provided in official (b), every registrant subject to § 240.15d-1 shall file a current report on Form 8-K within the period specified in that form unless substantially the same information has been previously reported. Official (b) names § 240.15d-16 and Form 6-K (§ 249.306 of this chapter), not 13a-16. Official (c) says no failure to file a report required solely pursuant to Item 1.01, 1.02, 1.05, 2.03, 2.04, 2.05, 2.06, 4.02(a), 5.02(e), or 6.03 of Form 8-K shall be deemed to be a violation of 15 U.S.C. 78j(b) and § 240.10b-5. Official source line: “[42 FR 4429, Jan. 25, 1977, as amended at 50 FR 27939, July 9, 1985; 68 FR 4355, Jan. 28, 2002; 69 FR 15618, Mar. 25, 2004; 70 FR 1622, Jan. 7, 2005; 71 FR 53263, Sept. 8, 2006; 75 FR 56792, Sept. 16, 2010; 81 FR 82020, Nov. 18, 2016; 88 FR 51943, Aug. 4, 2023; 90 FR 9690, Feb. 18, 2025].” Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official typography invents a numeric clock inside 15d-11 itself, and official (c) adds Item 2.02 and Item 5.07 to the list."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 15d-11(a) applies to every registrant subject to § 240.15d-1, with a form-specified clock and a previously-reported carve-out. Official (b) names 15d-16 / Form 6-K, not 13a-16. Official (c) is a 10b-5 negation, not a finding of a violation. Do not tidy this CITA into packed 13a-11." />
<Quiz id="dc-15d13" question="Under 17 CFR 240.15d-13, what is the general Form 10-Q clock, what is the official first-report typography, and is official (e) packed?" options={["A Form 10-Q is due for all four fiscal quarters, official typography is has been required, and the form cite is § 249.308a.", "Official (e) may be dropped, official (d) authorizes a substitute filing, and the official CITA uses a semicolon between 54 FR 10319 and 61 FR 30403.", "Every issuer required to file annual reports pursuant to section 15(d) of the Act on Form 10-K shall file a quarterly report on Form 10-Q (§ 249.308 of this chapter) within the period specified in General Instruction A.1 to that form for each of the first three quarters. Official typography is “had been required,” not “has been required.” Official typography has no trailing period after “A.1.” Official (d) typography is lowercase “section 18,” with typographic quotes around “filed.” Official (e) is packed verbatim, including financial information submitted in lieu thereof pursuant to paragraph (d). Official source line: “[42 FR 24065, May 12, 1977, as amended at 46 FR 63255, Dec. 31, 1981; 50 FR 27939, July 9, 1985; 54 FR 10319, Mar. 13, 1989, 61 FR 30403, June 14, 1996; 70 FR 1622, Jan. 7, 2005; 73 FR 978, Jan. 4, 2008; 81 FR 82020, Nov. 18, 2016].” Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "This flattens 15d-13 into packed 13a-13, and official (b) names 13a-16 / 13a-17."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 15d-13 uses § 249.308, General Instruction A.1 with no trailing period after A.1, and “had been required.” Official (e) is packed; do not invent that official (d) authorizes a substitute filing. The official CITA uses a comma, not a semicolon, between 54 FR 10319 and 61 FR 30403. This is not packed 13a-13." />
<Quiz id="dc-15d6" question="Under 17 CFR 240.15d-6, when must the issuer file a notice on Form 15, and does this box pack Rule 12h-3?" options={["The notice is due within 60 days, official typography omits the 12h-3 already-filed carve-out, and this is packed 12h-3.", "This is packed 12g-4, or the statutory section 15(d) suspension test, and the official 15d-6 caption is tidied into official 12h-3’s caption.", "If the duty of an issuer to file reports pursuant to section 15(d) of the Act as to any fiscal year is suspended as provided in section 15(d) of the Act, such issuer shall, within 30 days after the beginning of the first fiscal year, file a notice on Form 15 informing the Commission of such suspension unless Form 15 has already been filed pursuant to Rule 12h-3. Official typography is “the issuer's merger into, or consolidation with, another issuer or issuers.” Official caption typography is “Suspension of duty to file reports.” Official 12h-3 is named only. Official source line: “[49 FR 12690, Mar. 30, 1984].” Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official typography is within 30 business days after the fiscal year ends, and official SECAUTH flattens the italic letter l into a plain 781."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 15d-6 is a Form 15 notice clock: within 30 days after the beginning of the first fiscal year, unless Form 15 has already been filed pursuant to Rule 12h-3. Official 12h-3 is named only; do not tidy the two captions together. This is not packed 12g-4 and is not the statutory section 15(d) suspension test." />
<Quiz id="dc-244102" question="Under 17 CFR 244.102, does a person’s compliance or non-compliance with Regulation G in itself affect Section 10(b) or Rule 10b-5 liability, and is the official caption the same words as packed 243.102?" options={["Compliance with Regulation G is a Rule 10b-5 safe harbor, official typography is \"to 244.102,\" and this box rewrites packed 244.100.", "A Regulation G miss is automatically a Rule 10b-5 violation, official typography is \"shall be deemed to be a violation,\" and official API XML invents a CITA.", "Neither the requirements of this Regulation G (17 CFR 244.100 through 244.102) nor a person's compliance or non-compliance with the requirements of this Regulation shall in itself affect any person's liability under Section 10(b) (15 U.S.C. 78j(b)) of the Securities Exchange Act of 1934 or § 240.10b-5 of this chapter. Official typography is “through 244.102,” “compliance or non-compliance,” “shall in itself affect,” and capital-S “Section 10(b).” Official caption typography is “No effect on antifraud liability.” Official API XML has no CITA. Packed 244.100 is not rewritten; 244.101 is named only. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official typography packs 244.101 as a second core, and this box flattens the operative sentence into packed 243.102."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 244.102 is a Regulation G consequence rule: neither the requirements of this Regulation G, nor a person’s compliance or non-compliance, shall in itself affect liability under Section 10(b) or § 240.10b-5. Official typography is “through 244.102” and capital-S “Section 10(b).” The official caption uses the same words as packed 243.102, but the operative sentence must not be tidied into that box. Official API XML has no CITA." />
<Quiz id="dc-13a17" question="Under 17 CFR 240.13a-17, who must make reports on Form 10-D, what is the official clock, and what is the official form cite?" options={["Every issuer subject to § 240.13a-1 files Form 10-Q under this section, and the official form cite is § 249.308a.", "The reports are due 15 days after the distribution date, and official typography tidies § 249.312 into packed 15d-13’s § 249.308.", "Every asset-backed issuer subject to § 240.13a-1 shall make reports on Form 10-D (§ 249.312 of this chapter). Such reports shall be filed within the period specified in Form 10-D. Official caption typography is “Reports of asset-backed issuers on Form 10-D (§ 249.312 of this chapter).” Official source line: “[70 FR 1621, Jan. 7, 2005].” This box does not pack the Form 10-D instructions and does not rewrite packed 13a-13. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official typography invents a numeric Form 10-D clock inside 13a-17 itself, and this is packed 13a-13."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 13a-17 is the Form 10-D reporting hook for asset-backed issuers subject to § 240.13a-1. Official typography is “within the period specified in Form 10-D” — do not invent a numeric clock. The official form cite is § 249.312, not packed 13a-13’s § 249.308a or packed 15d-13’s § 249.308." />
<Quiz id="dc-15d17" question="Under 17 CFR 240.15d-17, what is the official trigger, what is the official Form 10-D clock, and what is the official CITA page?" options={["The official trigger is § 240.13a-1, and official typography tidies the CITA page into 70 FR 1621.", "This is packed 15d-13, and official typography invents a 15-day Form 10-D clock.", "Every asset-backed issuer subject to § 240.15d-1 shall make reports on Form 10-D (§ 249.312 of this chapter). Such reports shall be filed within the period specified in Form 10-D. Official caption typography is the same words as 13a-17. Official source line: “[70 FR 1622, Jan. 7, 2005].” This is not packed 15d-13 and is not the new 13a-17 box. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official typography is subject to § 240.13a-1, and the official form cite is § 249.308."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 15d-17 is the section 15(d) counterpart of 13a-17. Official trigger typography is § 240.15d-1, not § 240.13a-1. Official typography is still “within the period specified in Form 10-D.” Official CITA typography is “[70 FR 1622, Jan. 7, 2005],” not 13a-17’s 70 FR 1621." />
<Quiz id="dc-12b22" question="Under 17 CFR 240.12b-22, if the existence of control is open to reasonable doubt, may the registrant disclaim control, and is that disclaimer a finding of no control?" options={["A 12b-22 disclaimer is a finding of no control, and official typography drops the duty to state the material facts.", "Official typography invents a CITA, and this box flattens into packed 12b-21.", "If the existence of control is open to reasonable doubt in any instance, the registrant may disclaim the existence of control and any admission thereof; in such case, however, the registrant shall state the material facts pertinent to the possible existence of control. Official typography is “open to reasonable doubt,” “any admission thereof,” and “in such case, however.” A disclaimer is not a finding of no control. Official API XML has no CITA. This is not packed 12b-2 / 12b-20 / 12b-21 / 12b-23. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official typography is a finding of no control whenever a disclaimer is filed, and official caption typography is Information unknown or not available."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 12b-22 lets the registrant disclaim the existence of control and any admission thereof when control is open to reasonable doubt, but “in such case, however,” the registrant shall state the material facts pertinent to the possible existence of control. A disclaimer is not a finding of no control. Official API XML has no CITA." />
<Quiz id="dc-13b21" question="Under 17 CFR 240.13b2-1, what does official typography prohibit, and does this box pack 13b2-2?" options={["Official typography adds an Oxford comma to “book, record, or account” and adds “of 1934” to that sentence, and this box packs 13b2-2.", "Official typography drops the comma after “indirectly,” and this is packed 10b-5 or packed 13a-14.", "No person shall directly or indirectly, falsify or cause to be falsified, any book, record or account subject to section 13(b)(2)(A) of the Securities Exchange Act. Official typography keeps the comma after “indirectly,” uses “book, record or account” with no Oxford comma, lowercase “section 13(b)(2)(A),” and “the Securities Exchange Act” without “of 1934.” Official source line: “[44 FR 10970, Feb. 23, 1979].” Official 13b2-2 is named only. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official typography is a certification rule like packed 13a-14, and official CITA is tidied into a later amendment page."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 13b2-1 prohibits falsifying, or causing to be falsified, any book, record or account subject to section 13(b)(2)(A) of the Securities Exchange Act. Keep the comma after “indirectly,” and do not add an Oxford comma or “of 1934” to that sentence. Official 13b2-2 is named only; this is not packed 10b-5, packed 12b-20, or packed 13a-14." />
<Quiz id="dc-14a10" question="Under 17 CFR 240.14a-10, which solicitations are prohibited, and is official scope typography “to” or “through”?" options={["Official typography is §§ 240.14a-1 through 240.14a-10, and an undated proxy may be solicited.", "This is packed 14a-9, and official typography allows a proxy deemed dated after it is signed.", "No person making a solicitation which is subject to §§ 240.14a-1 to 240.14a-10 shall solicit: any undated or postdated proxy; or any proxy which provides that it shall be deemed to be dated as of any date subsequent to the date on which it is signed by the security holder. Official scope typography is “§§ 240.14a-1 to 240.14a-10” (“to,” not “through”). Official source line: “[17 FR 11434, Dec. 18, 1952].” This is not packed 14a-9 or packed 14a-3. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official typography prohibits only postdated proxies, and official (b) is not packed."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 14a-10 prohibits soliciting any undated or postdated proxy, or any proxy deemed dated as of any date subsequent to the date on which it is signed by the security holder. Official scope typography is “§§ 240.14a-1 to 240.14a-10” (“to,” not “through”). This is not packed 14a-9 or packed 14a-3." />

<Quiz id="dc-14a14" question="Under 17 CFR 240.14a-14, does a later modifying or superseding statement admit that the prior statement was misleading, and is official (c) typography “constitute part” or “constitute a part”?" options={["A later modifying statement is an admission that the prior statement was misleading, and official (c) uses the same phrase for modified and superseded statements.", "This is packed 14a-9, packed 14a-10, or packed 12b-23, and official typography requires the modifying statement to say it has modified a prior statement.", "Any statement contained in a document incorporated or deemed to be incorporated by reference shall be deemed to be modified or superseded, for purposes of the proxy statement, to the extent that a statement contained in the proxy statement or in any other subsequently filed document that also is or is deemed to be incorporated by reference modifies or replaces such statement. The making of a modifying or superseding statement shall not be deemed an admission. Official (c) typography is “constitute part” (modified, no “a”) versus “constitute a part” (superseded). Official typography is “may, but need not, state it has modified or superseded a prior statement.” Official typography is “the Securities Exchange Act of 1934 (“the Act”).” Official source line: “[52 FR 21936, June 10, 1987, as amended at 73 FR 17814, Apr. 1, 2008].” This is not packed 14a-9, packed 14a-10, or packed 12b-23. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official typography is “constitute a part” for both a modified statement and a superseded statement, and official API XML has no CITA."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 14a-14 does not treat the making of a modifying or superseding statement as an admission. Official typography is “may, but need not, state it has modified or superseded a prior statement.” Official (c) typography is “constitute part” (modified, no “a”) versus “constitute a part” (superseded). This is not packed 14a-9, packed 14a-10, or packed 12b-23." />

<Quiz id="dc-12d21" question="Under 17 CFR 240.12d2-1, does an exchange trading suspension terminate Exchange Act registration, and does official typography invent a numeric clock for “promptly”?" options={["Suspension of trading terminates the registration of the security, and official typography gives a 24-hour clock for “promptly.”", "This is packed 12b-6, the already-taught delisting-returns box, or official 12d2-2, and official typography is “the reasons therefore.”", "A national securities exchange may suspend from trading a security listed and registered thereon in accordance with its rules. Such exchange shall promptly notify the Commission of any such suspension, the effective date thereof, and the reasons therefor. Suspension of trading shall not terminate the registration of any security. Official typography is “the reasons therefor,” “designed to evade the provisions of section 12(d),” and “withdrawal and striking.” Official SECAUTH typography is 15 U.S.C. 78l with official italic on the letter l. Official source line: “[28 FR 1506, Feb. 16, 1963].” Official 12d2-2 is named only. This is not packed 12b-6 or the already-taught delisting-returns box. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official typography invents a numeric clock for “promptly,” and official 12d2-2 is packed in this box."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 12d2-1 lets an exchange suspend a listed and registered security in accordance with its rules, but “Suspension of trading shall not terminate the registration of any security.” Official typography is “the reasons therefor.” Do not invent a numeric clock for “promptly.” Official 12d2-2 is named only. This is not packed 12b-6 or the already-taught delisting-returns box." />

<Quiz id="dc-232301" question="Under 17 CFR 232.301, how must filers prepare electronic filings, and what are the official Volume I and Volume II typography lines?" options={["Filers may prepare electronic filings in any format, and official typography is Volume I Version 42 and Volume II Version 77.", "This is packed 232.12, packed 232.13, or packed 232.14, and official typography is that a filing submitted by 10 p.m. is always deemed filed that same business day.", "Filers must prepare electronic filings in the manner prescribed by the EDGAR Filer Manual, promulgated by the Commission, which sets forth the technical formatting requirements for electronic submissions. Official Volume I typography is “General Information,” Version 43 (March 2026). Official Volume II typography is “EDGAR Filing,” Version 78 (September 2026). Official typography is “You must comply with these requirements in order for documents to be timely received and accepted.” Official typography italicizes the SEC and NARA addresses. Official source line: “[91 FR 63478, Oct. 6, 2026].” This box does not pack the Filer Manual itself, and is not packed 232.12, packed 232.13, or packed 232.14. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "This pack includes the EDGAR Filer Manual volumes themselves as a new core, and official API XML has no CITA."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 232.301 requires filers to prepare electronic filings in the manner prescribed by the EDGAR Filer Manual. Official typography is Volume I: “General Information,” Version 43 (March 2026) and Volume II: “EDGAR Filing,” Version 78 (September 2026). Official typography is “You must comply with these requirements in order for documents to be timely received and accepted.” This box does not pack the Filer Manual itself, and is not packed 232.12, packed 232.13, or packed 232.14." />

<Quiz id="dc-232406" question="Under 17 CFR 232.406, which forms’ cover-page information must be tagged in Inline XBRL, and does official typography use an Oxford comma before 40-F?" options={["Official typography is “, or 40-F” with an Oxford comma, and this rule tags the entire Form 10-K body.", "This is the already-printed XBRL filed-date box or packed 232.301, and official typography packs the 232.11 definitions catalog.", "Electronic filers submitting Forms 10-K (§ 249.310 of this chapter), 10-Q (§ 249.308a of this chapter), 8-K (§ 249.308 of this chapter), 20-F (§ 249.220f of this chapter) or 40-F (§ 249.240f of this chapter) who are required to submit Interactive Data Files (§ 232.11) in Inline XBRL format in accordance with this Regulation S-T must tag in Inline XBRL electronic format, in the manner provided by the EDGAR Filer Manual, all of the information provided by the electronic filer that is required on the cover page of these forms. Official typography is “or 40-F” with no Oxford comma before 40-F. Official 232.11 is named only. Official source line: “[84 FR 12722, Apr. 2, 2019].” This is not the already-printed XBRL filed-date box or packed 232.301. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official typography names Form 10-K only, and official API XML has no CITA."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 232.406 requires cover-page information on Forms 10-K, 10-Q, 8-K, 20-F or 40-F to be tagged in Inline XBRL in the manner provided by the EDGAR Filer Manual. Official typography is “or 40-F” with no Oxford comma. Official 232.11 is named only. This is not the already-printed XBRL filed-date box or packed 232.301." />

<Quiz id="dc-16c3" question="Under 17 CFR 240.16c-3, when is a sale of a security to be acquired “when issued” or “when distributed” exempt from section 16(c), and does official (b) still limit combined transactions?" options={["The sale is exempt from section 16(c) with no reporting condition, and official typography invents a CITA line.", "This is the already-taught when-issued box or packed 16a-4, and official typography does not italicize “Provided,” including the comma.", "Whenever any person is entitled, incident to ownership of an issued security and without the payment of consideration, to receive another security “when issued” or “when distributed,” the sale of the security to be acquired shall be exempt from the operation of section 16(c) of the Act if official (a)(1)–(3) are met, including that such person reports the sale on the appropriate form for reporting transactions by persons subject to section 16(a) of the Act. Official typography uses typographic quotes around “when issued” and “when distributed,” and italicizes “Provided,” including the comma, in “Provided, That:”. Official (b) still limits combined transactions involving both a sale of the issued security and a sale of a security “when issued” or “when distributed.” Official API XML has no CITA. This is not the already-taught when-issued box or packed 16a-4. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official (b) exempts every combined issued plus when-issued sale, and official typography uses straight quotes around when issued."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 16c-3 exempts a sale of a security to be acquired “when issued” or “when distributed” only if official (a)(1)–(3) are met, including reporting the sale on the appropriate section 16(a) form. Official typography italicizes “Provided,” including the comma. Official (b) still limits combined transactions. Official API XML has no CITA. This is not the already-taught when-issued box or packed 16a-4." />

<Quiz id="dc-16c4" question="Under 17 CFR 240.16c-4, when is establishing or increasing a put equivalent position exempt from section 16(c), and is official caption typography the same as packed 16a-4?" options={["The exemption has no ownership limit, and official typography invents a CITA line.", "Official caption typography is “Derivative securities,” so this box is packed 16a-4 and should be tidied into that reporting rule.", "Establishing or increasing a put equivalent position shall be exempt from section 16(c) of the Act, so long as the amount of securities underlying the put equivalent position does not exceed the amount of underlying securities otherwise owned. Official caption typography is “Derivative securities.” — the same words as packed 16a-4 — but this is a section 16(c) exemption and must not be tidied into packed 16a-4. Official typography is “put equivalent position” and “otherwise owned.” Official API XML has no CITA. This is not packed 16a-4 or leftover 16b-6. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "This exemption is leftover 16b-6, and official typography is “call equivalent position.”"]} answer={2} explanation="Official 16c-4 exempts establishing or increasing a put equivalent position from section 16(c) so long as the amount of securities underlying the put equivalent position does not exceed the amount of underlying securities otherwise owned. Official caption typography is “Derivative securities,” the same words as packed 16a-4, but this is a section 16(c) exemption and must not be tidied into packed 16a-4. Official API XML has no CITA." />

<Quiz id="dc-16a2" question="Under 17 CFR 240.16a-2, do the rules under section 16 apply to a class of equity securities whether or not it is registered under section 12, and how does official typography name a ten percent beneficial owner?" options={["Official typography places “The rules under section 16 of the Act apply to any class of equity securities of an issuer whether or not registered under section 12 of the Act” inside official (c), and it uses straight quotes around ten percent beneficial owner.", "This box is packed 16a-3 / 16a-10 / 16a-12, and it packs 16a-1.", "The rules under section 16 of the Act apply to any class of equity securities of an issuer whether or not registered under section 12 of the Act. Do not tidy that sentence into official (c). Official typography is (“ten percent beneficial owner”) with typographic quotes, and 15 U.S.C. 78l uses official italic on the letter l. Official typography is “The rules under section 16(a) of the Act do not apply to ten percent beneficial owners of an issuer that is a foreign private issuer.” Official 16a-8 and official 3b-4 are named only. Official source line is “[56 FR 7265, Feb. 21, 1991, as amended at 61 FR 30392, June 14, 1996; 67 FR 43535, June 28, 2002; 76 FR 71877, Nov. 21, 2011; 91 FR 10333, Mar. 3, 2026; 91 FR 32336, June 1, 2026].” Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "The section 16 rules apply only to a class registered under section 12, and official typography invents a different CITA line."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 16a-2 says the rules under section 16 of the Act apply to any class of equity securities of an issuer whether or not registered under section 12 of the Act. Do not tidy that sentence into official (c)’s section 12 reporting clause. Official typography is (“ten percent beneficial owner”) with typographic quotes, and 15 U.S.C. 78l uses official italic on the letter l. Official 16a-8 and official 3b-4 are named only." />

<Quiz id="dc-12d11" question="Under 17 CFR 240.12d1-1, is an application to register a security on a national securities exchange deemed to apply to the entire class, and how does official typography write section 12 (b) and (c)?" options={["The application covers only shares then issued, later authorized shares need a further application, and official typography is section 12(b) and (c) with no spaces.", "This box is packed 12d2-1 or packed 12b-6, and official typography invents a CITA line.", "An application filed pursuant to section 12 (b) and (c) of the act for registration of a security on a national securities exchange shall be deemed to apply for registration of the entire class of such security. Additional shares or amounts of such class then or thereafter authorized become registered upon issuance without further application. Official typography is “section 12 (b) and (c) of the act” (spaces around the lettered paragraphs; official (a) uses lowercase “act”). Official (d) typography is “If a class of security is issuable in two or more series with different terms, each such series shall be deemed a separate class for the purposes of this section.” Official SECAUTH typography is 15 U.S.C. 78l with official italic on the letter l. Official API XML has no CITA. This is not packed 12d2-1 or packed 12b-6. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Each series must be treated as the same class even when the terms differ, and official typography capitalizes every “act.”"]} answer={2} explanation="Official 12d1-1(a) treats an application filed pursuant to section 12 (b) and (c) of the act as an application for the entire class, and later authorized additional shares or amounts become registered upon issuance without further application. Official typography keeps spaces around the lettered paragraphs and lowercase “act” in official (a). Official (d) treats series with different terms as separate classes. Official API XML has no CITA." />

<Quiz id="dc-12d12" question="Under 17 CFR 240.12d1-2, are the Form 8-A effectiveness clocks the same under 12(b) and 12(g), and is official (c)(2) typography “If class of securities” or “If a class of securities”?" options={["12(b) and 12(g) share one clock, and both use exchange certification; official typography tidies (c)(2) into “If a class of securities.”", "This box is packed 12d2-1, and it packs 12d1-3 through 12d1-6 as well.", "The 12(b) and 12(g) Form 8-A clocks are not the same. With no concurrent Securities Act registration, a 12(b) Form 8-A becomes effective upon the later of receipt of exchange certification or filing of the Form 8-A; a 12(g) Form 8-A becomes effective upon filing of the Form 8-A and does not use exchange certification. Official (c)(2) typography is “If class of securities is concurrently being registered under the Securities Act” — no “a” after “If.” Official typography is “the reasons therefor,” and typographic quotes around “Securities Act.” Official source line is “[19 FR 670, Feb. 5, 1954, as amended at 59 FR 55347, Nov. 7, 1994; 62 FR 39766, July 24, 1997].” Official 12d1-3 through 12d1-6 and official 12d2-2 are named only. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "A 12(b) registration becomes effective as soon as Form 8-A is filed, with no exchange certification, and official typography invents a different CITA line."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 12d1-2 does not invent one Form 8-A clock for 12(b) and 12(g). Official 12(b) clocks use exchange certification; official 12(g) clocks do not. Official (c)(2) typography is “If class of securities,” with no “a” after “If.” Official typography is “the reasons therefor,” and typographic quotes around “Securities Act.”" />
