<Quiz id="dc-16c1" question="Under 17 CFR 240.16c-1, when is a broker’s execution of an order exempt from section 16(c), and does official API XML have a CITA?" options={["The execution is exempt even if the broker has a direct or indirect interest in the account, and official typography invents a CITA line.", "This box is packed 16c-3 or packed 16c-4.", "Any transaction shall be exempt from section 16(c) of the Act to the extent necessary to render lawful the execution by a broker of an order for an account in which the broker has no direct or indirect interest. Official typography is “no direct or indirect interest.” Official API XML has no CITA. This is not packed 16c-3 or packed 16c-4. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "The exemption covers only the broker’s proprietary account, and official caption typography is Derivative securities."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 16c-1 exempts a transaction from section 16(c) to the extent necessary to render lawful a broker’s execution of an order for an account in which the broker has no direct or indirect interest. Official typography is “no direct or indirect interest.” Official API XML has no CITA. This is not packed 16c-3 or packed 16c-4." />

<Quiz id="dc-16c2" question="Under 17 CFR 240.16c-2, is the dealer-sale exemption limited to the over-allotment alternative, and is official caption typography the same as 16a-7?" options={["Official typography packs only the over-allotment clause, the second alternative may be dropped, and official typography invents a CITA line.", "Official caption typography is “Transactions effected in connection with a distribution,” so this box is 16a-7 and should be tidied into that section 16(a) reporting exemption.", "The whole official paragraph must be packed, including the over-allotment clause and “or the dealer or a person acting on the dealer's behalf intends in good faith to offset such sale…” Official typography is “the dealer's behalf” (ASCII apostrophe). Official caption typography is “Transactions effected in connection with a distribution.” — the same words as 16a-7 — but this is a section 16(c) exemption and must not be tidied into 16a-7. Official API XML has no CITA. This is not packed 16c-3 or packed 16c-4. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "This exemption is packed 16c-4, and it does not matter whether the dealer is in an underwriting group."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 16c-2 is one paragraph with two official alternatives: an over-allotment, or the dealer or a person acting on the dealer's behalf intending in good faith to offset the sale. Do not drop the second alternative. Official caption typography uses the same words as 16a-7, but this is a section 16(c) exemption and must not be tidied into 16a-7. Official typography is “the dealer's behalf.” Official API XML has no CITA." />

<Quiz id="dc-16a7" question="Under 17 CFR 240.16a-7, when is a purchase and sale, or sale and purchase, in connection with a distribution exempt from section 16(a), and what does official (b) require of each person?" options={["Anyone connected with a distribution is exempt without official (a)(1) and (a)(2), and official typography invents a CITA line.", "Official caption typography uses the same words as 16c-2, so this box is 16c-2 and should be tidied into that section 16(c) exemption.", "Any purchase and sale, or sale and purchase, of a security that is made in connection with the distribution of a substantial block of securities shall be exempt from the provisions of section 16(a) of the Act, to the extent specified in this rule, subject to the following conditions: official (a)(1) and official (a)(2), including (a)(2)(i)–(ii), must be met. Official (b) typography is “Each person participating in the transaction must qualify on an individual basis for an exemption pursuant to this section.” Official caption typography uses the same words as 16c-2, but this is a section 16(a) exemption and must not be tidied into 16c-2. Official API XML has no CITA. This is not packed 16a-6 or packed 16a-10. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "This exemption is packed 16a-6, and one person’s qualification covers everyone else in the transaction."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 16a-7 exempts a purchase and sale, or sale and purchase, from section 16(a) only to the extent specified in the rule and only if official (a)(1) and (a)(2) are met. Official (b) typography is “Each person participating in the transaction must qualify on an individual basis for an exemption pursuant to this section.” Official caption typography uses the same words as 16c-2, but this is a section 16(a) exemption and must not be tidied into 16c-2. Official API XML has no CITA." />

<Quiz id="dc-13d4" question="Under 17 CFR 240.13d-4, is an express disclaimer in a filed statement a finding of no beneficial ownership, and how does official typography write sections 13(d) and 13(g)?" options={["A 13d-4 disclaimer is a finding of no beneficial ownership, and official typography is sections 13(d) and 13(g) of the Act.", "This box is packed 13d-1 / 13d-2 / 13d-3 or packed 12b-22, and official SECAUTH typography should be tidied into 78m(d)(2).", "Any person may expressly declare in any statement filed that the filing of such statement shall not be construed as an admission that such person is, for the purposes of sections 13(d) or 13(g) of the Act, the beneficial owner of any securities covered by the statement. Official typography is “sections 13(d) or 13(g) of the Act” (“or,” not “and”). A 13d-4 disclaimer is not a finding of no beneficial ownership. Official API XML has no CITA. Official SECAUTH typography includes 89m(d)(2) and must not be tidied into 78m(d)(2). This is not packed 13d-1 / 13d-2 / 13d-3 or packed 12b-22. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official API XML invents a CITA line, and 12b-22 should be merged with 13d-4."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 13d-4 lets a person declare that the filing of a statement shall not be construed as an admission of beneficial ownership for the purposes of sections 13(d) or 13(g) of the Act. Official typography is “sections 13(d) or 13(g) of the Act.” A 13d-4 disclaimer is not a finding of no beneficial ownership. Official API XML has no CITA. Official SECAUTH typography includes 89m(d)(2)." />

<Quiz id="dc-13d5" question={"Under 17 CFR 240.13d-5, is a person deemed to have acquired beneficial ownership only when the acquisition was through purchase, and how does official (b) print {x} / {y}?"} options={["13d-5 covers only acquisitions through purchase, executors or administrators acquire that ownership as soon as they are named, and official typography tidies {x} / {y} into (x) / (y).", "This box is packed 13d-3 or packed 13d-1 / 13d-2, and official CITA is 13d-6's [88 FR 76984, Nov. 7, 2023].", "A person who becomes a beneficial owner of securities shall be deemed to have acquired such beneficial ownership for purposes of section 13(d)(1) of the Act, whether such acquisition was through purchase or otherwise. Executors or administrators of a decedent's estate generally will be presumed not to have acquired that ownership until qualified under local law. A group formed by an agreement to act together is deemed to have acquired, as of the date of such agreement, all equity securities of that issuer beneficially owned by any such persons, for purposes of sections 13(d) and (g) of the Act. Official typography is “through the earlier of {x} the date of the group's dissolution or {y} the date of that member's withdrawal from the group”; do not tidy the curly braces into (x) / (y). Intra-group sales or transfers are excepted by official (b)(1)(iii) / (b)(2)(ii). Official source line: “[88 FR 76983, Nov. 7, 2023].” This is not packed 13d-3 or packed 13d-1 / 13d-2. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official (b) does not distinguish 13(d)(3) groups from 13(g)(3) groups, and official API XML has no CITA."]} answer={2} explanation={"Official 13d-5(a) deems a person who becomes a beneficial owner to have acquired that ownership whether such acquisition was through purchase or otherwise. Official typography is “whether such acquisition was through purchase or otherwise” and “decedent's estate.” Official (b) typography uses ASCII curly braces {x} / {y}; do not tidy them into (x) / (y). Official source line: “[88 FR 76983, Nov. 7, 2023].” This is not packed 13d-3 or packed 13d-1 / 13d-2."} />

<Quiz id="dc-13d6" question="Under 17 CFR 240.13d-6, when does official (a) exempt an acquisition from section 13(d), and must official (b) meet all four conditions?" options={["Official (a) is available even if the person was not already a beneficial owner of more than five percent, and official (b) is satisfied by any one of its four conditions.", "This box is packed 13d-5 or packed 13d-1, and official CITA should be tidied into 13d-5's [88 FR 76983, Nov. 7, 2023].", "Official (a) applies only if the person was already a beneficial owner of more than five percent of the same class and the acquisition is made pursuant to preemptive subscription rights in an offering made to all holders of that class. The person must not acquire additional securities except through the exercise of such person's pro rata share of those rights, and the acquisition must be duly reported, if required, pursuant to section 16(a). Official (b) is a separate group exemption and requires all four official (b)(1)–(4) conditions. Official (b)(1) typography is “persons specified in § 240.13d-1(b)(1)(ii).” Official source line: “[88 FR 76984, Nov. 7, 2023].” Do not tidy that page into 76983. This is not packed 13d-5 or packed 13d-1. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official (a) does not require preemptive subscription rights, and this box packs the official 13d-1(b)(1)(ii) catalog."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 13d-6(a) exempts an acquisition from section 13(d) only if the person was already a beneficial owner of more than five percent, the acquisition is made pursuant to preemptive subscription rights, the person takes only such person's pro rata share, and the acquisition is duly reported, if required, pursuant to section 16(a). Official (b) requires all of (b)(1)–(4). Official source line: “[88 FR 76984, Nov. 7, 2023].” This is not packed 13d-5 or packed 13d-1." />

<Quiz id="dc-3b2" question="Under 17 CFR 240.3b-2, how does official typography define “officer,” and is the official wording treasurer and controller?" options={["Official typography is treasurer and controller, and this box should be tidied into 3b-7 or official 16a-1(f).", "This box packs official 16a-1 in full, official caption typography uses straight quotes, and the body does not italicize officer.", "The term officer means a president, vice president, secretary, treasury or principal financial officer, comptroller or principal accounting officer, and any person routinely performing corresponding functions with respect to any organization whether incorporated or unincorporated. Official typography is “treasury or principal financial officer” (not “treasurer”) and “comptroller” (not “controller”). Official caption typography is Definition of “officer”. (typographic U+201C/U+201D), and the body italicizes officer. Official source line: “[47 FR 11464, Mar. 16, 1982; 47 FR 11819, Mar. 19, 1982].” Official 16a-1 is named only; do not pack it, and do not tidy this typography into official 16a-1(f) or into 3b-7. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "The definition covers only incorporated organizations, and official API XML has no CITA."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 3b-2 defines officer as a president, vice president, secretary, treasury or principal financial officer, comptroller or principal accounting officer, and any person routinely performing corresponding functions. Official typography is “treasury or principal financial officer” and “comptroller,” not treasurer or controller. Official caption typography uses typographic quotes, and the body italicizes officer. Official 16a-1 is named only. This is not 3b-7." />

<Quiz id="dc-3b7" question="Under 17 CFR 240.3b-7, when does a vice president count as an executive officer, and is official typography policy-making and vice-president?" options={["Every vice president is an executive officer, and official typography is hyphenated policy-making and vice-president.", "This box should be tidied into 3b-2 or official 16a-1(f), and official italics omit the comma after executive officer.", "A vice president counts only if in charge of a principal business unit, division or function (such as sales, administration or finance), or if the person otherwise performs a policy making function. Official typography is “policy making” (two words, no hyphen) and “vice president” (two words, no hyphen). Official typography italicizes executive officer, including the comma. Executive officers of subsidiaries may be deemed executive officers of the registrant if they perform such policy making functions for the registrant. Official caption typography is Definition of “executive officer”. (typographic quotes). Official source line: “[47 FR 11464, Mar. 16, 1982, as amended at 56 FR 7265, Feb. 21, 1991].” Official 16a-1 is named only; do not pack it, and do not tidy this typography into official 16a-1(f) or into 3b-2. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Executive officers of subsidiaries are always deemed executive officers of the registrant, and official API XML has no CITA."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 3b-7 counts a vice president as an executive officer only if the person is in charge of a principal business unit, division or function, or otherwise performs a policy making function. Official typography is two-word, unhyphenated “policy making” and “vice president.” Official italics include the comma in executive officer,. Official 16a-1 is named only. This is not 3b-2." />

<Quiz id="dc-12g2" question="Under 17 CFR 240.12g-2, must a class file an additional registration statement when a 12(g)(2)(A) or (B) exemption terminates, and how does official (b) print the holder threshold for banks and related companies?" options={["An additional registration statement is still required after termination, and official (b) uses a 300-person test for banks as well.", "This box is packed 12g-1 or packed 12g-4, and official API XML invents a SECAUTH line.", "Upon termination of the 12(g)(2)(A) exchange listing and registration, or of the 12(g)(2)(B) registered-investment-company registration, the class is deemed registered pursuant to section 12(g)(1) without the filing of an additional registration statement, but only if at the time of such termination official (a) or official (b) is met. Official typography is “without the filing of an additional registration statement.” Official (a) is an unwithdrawn business-development-company election, and official U.S.C. typography is “15 U.S.C. 80a-54 through 64.” Official (b) typography is 300 or more persons, or 1,200 or more persons in the case of a bank, a savings and loan holding company as defined in section 10 of the Home Owners' Loan Act, or a bank holding company as defined in section 2 of the Bank Holding Company Act of 1956. Official typography italicizes the letter l in 15 U.S.C. 78l. Official source line: “[81 FR 28705, May 10, 2016].” Official API XML has no SECAUTH. This is not packed 12g-1 / 12g-4 / 12d2-1 / 12b-6. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "This box packs locked 12g5-1, and the already-shipped 12g-4 “up to date as of 2026-11-09” date should be rewritten."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 12g-2 deems the class registered pursuant to section 12(g)(1) without the filing of an additional registration statement if official (a) or (b) is met at termination of a 12(g)(2)(A) or (B) exemption. Official typography is “without the filing of an additional registration statement,” “15 U.S.C. 80a-54 through 64,” and “1,200 or more persons.” Official API XML has no SECAUTH. This is not packed 12g-1 / 12g-4 / 12d2-1 / 12b-6." />

<Quiz id="dc-13f1" question="Under 17 CFR 240.13f-1, when is a Form 13F report required, and how do official (b) and official (c) print investment descretion?" options={["The threshold is a $100,000,000 aggregate fair market value on any trading day, and a report is due only after calendar year-end. Official typography tidies investment descretion into investment discretion throughout.", "This box is the already-taught 13F 45-day awareness sentence, and official API XML has no SECAUTH.", "Official (a)(1) is triggered by aggregate fair market value of at least $100,000,000 on the last trading day of any month of any calendar year; the report on Form 13F is due within 45 days after the last day of such calendar year and within 45 days after the last day of each of the first three calendar quarters of the subsequent calendar year. Official typography uses typographic quotes around both “investment descretion” (official typo, first quoted term of official (b)) and “investment discretion” (correct spelling, later in official (b)); official (c) uses unquoted investment descretion and typographic quotes around “section 13(f) securities.” Only securities of a class on the most recent Commission list shall be counted and reported. Official CITA is “[43 FR 26705, June 22, 1978, as amended at 44 FR 3034, Jan. 15, 1979; 64 FR 2849, Jan. 19, 1999; 76 FR 71876, Nov. 21, 2011].” Official SECAUTH typography is (Secs. 3(b), 13(f) and 23 of the Exchange Act (15 U.S.C. 78c(b), 78m(f) and 78w)). Official 249.325 is named only. This is not the already-taught 13F 45-day awareness sentence. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "A person who controls the issuer must still count that issuer's section 13(f) securities toward the threshold, and official 249.325 should be packed as a second core."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 13f-1(a)(1) is triggered by at least $100,000,000 aggregate fair market value on the last trading day of any month of any calendar year, and the Form 13F report is due within 45 days after year-end and after each of the first three subsequent calendar quarters. Official (b) prints both “investment descretion” and “investment discretion”; official (c) uses unquoted investment descretion. Official 249.325 is named only. This is not the already-taught 13F 45-day awareness sentence." />

<Quiz id="dc-13f2" question="Under 17 CFR 240.13f-2, when is a Form SHO report due, and how do official thresholds and official (b)(4) print?" options={["Form SHO is due within 45 days after quarter-end, and official (b)(4) tidies gross short position into does not include offsetting economic positions.", "This box should rewrite the already-taught p3 Form SHO awareness box, and 2026 vendor Form SHO data is already official and backtest-ready.", "A report on Form SHO is due within 14 calendar days after the end of each calendar month. For section 12-registered or 15(d)-reporting equity, the official thresholds are a monthly average gross short position at the close of regular trading hours of $10 million or more, or 2.5 percent or more of shares outstanding. For equity not so registered or 15(d)-reporting, the official threshold is a gross short position of $500,000 or more at the close of regular trading hours on any settlement date during the calendar month. Official typography italicizes institutional investment manager, equity security, investment discretion, gross short position, and regular trading hours, and uses typographic quotes around “EDGAR.” Official (b)(4) typography is “without inclusion of any offsetting economic positions such as shares of the equity security or derivatives of such equity security.” Official CITA is “[88 FR 75185, Nov. 1, 2023].” Official API XML has no SECAUTH. Official 13f-1(b) / 249.332 / 3a11-1 / 242.200(a) / 242.600(b)(77) are named only. Do not invent that 2026 vendor Form SHO data is official or backtest-ready. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "The 2.5 percent threshold also applies to equity that is not section 12-registered or 15(d)-reporting, and official API XML invents a SECAUTH line."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 13f-2 requires a Form SHO report within 14 calendar days after month-end, and distinguishes the $10 million or 2.5 percent thresholds for section 12 / 15(d) equity from the $500,000 threshold for equity not so registered or reporting. Official (b)(4) typography is “without inclusion of any offsetting economic positions such as shares of the equity security or derivatives of such equity security.” Official API XML has no SECAUTH. Do not invent that vendor data is official or backtest-ready." />

<Quiz id="dc-14d7" question="Under 17 CFR 240.14d-7, when may a person who has deposited securities withdraw them, and how does official (b) print accompained and depositary?" options={["Withdrawal rights must also be offered during a subsequent offering period, and official typography is accompanied and depository.", "This box is packed 14d-11 or packed 14e-4, and official API XML invents a SECAUTH line.", "A person who has deposited securities pursuant to a tender offer has the right to withdraw any such securities during the period such offer request or invitation remains open; the bidder need not offer withdrawal rights during a subsequent offering period. Official typography is “such offer request or invitation remains open,” “accompained” (official typo), and “depositary.” Official typography italicizes the paragraph labels Rights., Exemption during subsequent offering period., and Notice of withdrawal. Notice of withdrawal is deemed timely upon the receipt by the bidder's depositary of a written notice of withdrawal. Official CITA is “[44 FR 70345, Dec. 6, 1979, as amended at 51 FR 25882, July 17, 1986; 51 FR 32630, Sept. 15, 1986; 64 FR 61461, Nov. 10, 1999; 76 FR 71876, Nov. 21, 2011].” Official API XML has no SECAUTH. This is not packed 14d-11 / 14d-10 / 14e-4. Locked 14d-4 / 14d-6 are not packed. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Notice of withdrawal is deemed timely when it is sent, and this box packs locked 14d-4 / 14d-6."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 14d-7 gives a depositor withdrawal rights during the period such offer request or invitation remains open, and the bidder need not offer withdrawal rights during a subsequent offering period. Official typography is “accompained” and “depositary.” Notice is deemed timely upon receipt by the bidder's depositary. Official API XML has no SECAUTH. This is not packed 14d-11 / 14d-10 / 14e-4." />

<Quiz id="dc-14d8" question="Under 17 CFR 240.14d-8, when must deposited securities be taken up pro rata, and how does official typography print the pro rata requirement?" options={["The rule applies to a tender offer for all of the outstanding equity securities of a class, and securities must be taken up in exact proportion, including fractions.", "This box is packed 14d-7 or packed 14e-4, and official API XML has no SECAUTH.", "Official text applies when a person makes a tender offer for less than all of the outstanding equity securities of a class and a greater number of securities are deposited than such person is bound or willing to take up and pay for; the securities taken up and paid for shall be taken up and paid for as nearly as may be pro rata, disregarding fractions, according to the number of securities deposited by each depositor during the period such offer, request or invitation remains open. Official caption typography is “Exemption from statutory pro rata requirements.” Official typography is “as nearly as may be pro rata, disregarding fractions.” Official CITA is “[47 FR 57680, Dec. 28, 1982].” Official full SECAUTH typography is (Sec. 23, 48 Stat. 901; sec. 203(a), 49 Stat. 704; sec. 8, 49 Stat. 1379; sec. 10, 78 Stat. 580; sec. 3, 82 Stat. 455; secs. 3-5, 84 Stat. 1497; sec. 18, 89 Stat. 155; 15 U.S.C. 78n(e), 78w(a)). This is not packed 14d-7 / 14d-11 / 14e-4. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Securities are taken up first-in, first-out, and official CITA is 14d-7's 70345."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 14d-8 applies when a less-than-all tender offer receives more securities than the person is bound or willing to take up and pay for, and official typography is “as nearly as may be pro rata, disregarding fractions.” Official full SECAUTH ends 15 U.S.C. 78n(e), 78w(a). This is not packed 14d-7 / 14d-11 / 14e-4." />

<Quiz id="dc-12a5" question="Under 17 CFR 240.12a-5, when does the temporary exemption apply and terminate, and how does official typography print become and The close of business?" options={["The temporary exemption applies even when all three official (a)(2)(i)–(iii) conditions are not met, and official typography is becomes effective.", "This box is packed 12g-2 or packed 12d2-1, and the already-shipped 12g-4 “up to date as of 2026-11-09” date should be bumped in the same edit.", "Official (a)(1) temporarily exempts a substituted or other security from section 12(a) on an issued or “when-issued” basis, and an additional amount of the original security on a “when-issued” basis, when holders of a security admitted to trading on a national securities exchange obtain the right to acquire them. Official (a)(2) requires all three official (a)(2)(i)–(iii) conditions. Official typography uses typographic quotes around “when-issued.” Official (b) terminates on the earliest of the listed dates, including “When registration of the exempt security on the exchange become effective” (official “become”), and “The close of business on the tenth day after” and “The close of business on the one hundred and twentieth day after” (official capital T). Official typography italicizes Provided, however, including the comma. Official (e) typography is “section 12(a) of the act.” Official CITA is “[13 FR 8185, Dec. 22, 1948, as amended at 19 FR 669, Feb. 5, 1954; 20 FR 2081, Apr. 2, 1955; 53 FR 41206, Oct. 20, 1988].” Official full SECAUTH typography is (Secs. 3, 12, 48 Stat. 882, 892; 15 U.S.C. 78c (12), 78l) (space before (12); italic l). Official Form 26 and official 10b-1 are named only. This is not packed 12g-2 / 12d2-1 / 12b-6. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "The exemption always continues after the one hundred and twentieth day, and official (e) typography is section 12(a) of the Act."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 12a-5(a)(1) supplies a temporary issued or “when-issued” exemption when holders obtain the right to acquire the securities and all three official (a)(2) conditions are met. Official (b) typography is “become effective” and capital-T “The close of business on the tenth day after” and “The close of business on the one hundred and twentieth day after.” Official SECAUTH has a space before (12) and italicizes l. This is not packed 12g-2 / 12d2-1 / 12b-6." />

<Quiz id="dc-12f4" question="Under 17 CFR 240.12f-4, when is a UTP security exempt from sections 13, 14 and 16, and how does official typography print act and Act?" options={["The security is always exempt from section 13 and section 14, without official (a)(1) or (a)(2), and without regard to whether it is also listed and registered or registered pursuant to section 12(g).", "This box is packed 12a-5 or a packed section 16 box, and official (c) tidies act and Act into the same token.", "A UTP security is exempt from section 13 unless official (a)(1) or official (a)(2) applies. Official (b) exempts the security from section 14 unless the security is also listed and registered on a national securities exchange or registered pursuant to section 12(g). Official (c)(1) typography is “section 16 of the act” (lowercase “act”) and does not apply if such security or another equity security of the same issuer is listed and registered on a national securities exchange or registered pursuant to section 12(g); official (c)(2) typography is “section 16 of the Act.” Official typography uses typographic quotes around “registered on a national securities exchange” in official (a). Official CITA is “[30 FR 482, Jan. 14, 1965].” Official SECAUTH typography is (Sec. 3, 78 Stat. 565, 15 U.S.C. 78l) (italic l). This is not packed 12a-5 / 12d2-1 / 12g-2 or a packed section 16 box. Official 12f-2 is named only. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official (a) uses straight quotes around registered on a national securities exchange, and this box packs the whole official 12f-2 section."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 12f-4 exempts a UTP security from section 13 unless official (a)(1) or (a)(2) applies, and from section 14 unless the security is also listed and registered or registered pursuant to section 12(g). Official (c)(1) typography is “section 16 of the act”; official (c)(2) typography is “section 16 of the Act.” Official (a) uses typographic quotes. Official 12f-2 is named only. This is not packed 12a-5 / 12d2-1 / 12g-2 or a packed section 16 box." />


<Quiz id="dc-12f2" question="Under 17 CFR 240.12f-2, when may a national securities exchange extend unlisted trading privileges to a subject security, and how does official typography print the three 78l forms?" options={["A national securities exchange may extend UTP to an IPO subject security before any transaction has been effected on the listing exchange, and official (a) cites 15 U.S.C. 78l(f).", "This box rewrites the packed 12f-4 / section-BI prefix, packs official 12f-1, and tidies official (b) 78l(f), official (c)(1), and official (c)(2) into one typography.", "A national securities exchange may extend unlisted trading privileges to a subject security when at least one transaction in the subject security has been effected on the national securities exchange upon which the security is listed and the transaction has been reported pursuant to an effective transaction reporting plan. Official typography italicizes General provision., Definitions., subject security, and initial public offering commences. Official (b) typography italicizes the letter l in 15 U.S.C. 78l(f); official (a) has no 78l citation. Official (c)(1) typography is 15 U.S.C. 78l(f)(1)(G)(i) (Roman l, Roman 1). Official (c)(2) typography is 15 U.S.C. 78l(f)(1)(G)(ii) (Roman l; italic 1). Official CITA is “[60 FR 20896, Apr. 28, 1995, as amended at 65 FR 53565, Sept. 5, 2000; 70 FR 37618, June 29, 2005].” Official API XML has no SECAUTH. Official 12f-1 is named only. The packed 12f-4 / section-BI prefix that official 12f-2 is named only stays as written. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official typography uses straight quotes around subject security, and official API XML invents a SECAUTH line."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 12f-2 lets a national securities exchange extend UTP to a subject security only after at least one transaction in that security has been effected on the listing exchange and reported pursuant to an effective transaction reporting plan. Official (b) italicizes the letter l in 78l(f); official (a) has no 78l citation; official (c)(1) and official (c)(2) keep two further official forms. Official 12f-1 is named only. The packed 12f-4 / section-BI prefix stays as written." />

<Quiz id="dc-12f3" question="Under 17 CFR 240.12f-3, who may apply to terminate or suspend unlisted trading privileges, may the exchange act under its own rules, and how does official typography print Names of issuer and the SECAUTH?" options={["Only the issuer may apply, and the exchange may not suspend or terminate UTP in accordance with its rules.", "This box packs official 12f-1, official (a)(4) typography is Names of issuers, and official API XML has no SECAUTH.", "The issuer, any broker or dealer who makes or creates a market for such security, or any other person having a bona fide interest may apply to the Commission. Official (b) says unlisted trading privileges may be suspended or terminated by such exchange in accordance with its rules. Official (a)(4) typography is “Names of issuer.” Official CITA is “[20 FR 6702, Sept. 13, 1955, as amended at 44 FR 75135, Dec. 19, 1979; 45 FR 36076, May 29, 1980; 60 FR 20896, Apr. 28, 1995].” Official SECAUTH typography is (Secs. 12(f) and 23, 15 U.S.C. 78l and 78w) (italic l). Official 12f-1 is named only; do not pack it. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official (a)(4) typography is Name of the issuer, and official SECAUTH uses a Roman l only."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 12f-3 lets the issuer, a market-making broker or dealer, or any other person with a bona fide interest apply to the Commission, and official (b) lets the exchange suspend or terminate UTP in accordance with its rules. Official (a)(4) typography is “Names of issuer.” Official SECAUTH italicizes the letter l. Official 12f-1 is named only." />

<Quiz id="dc-12f5" question="Under 17 CFR 240.12f-5, when may a national securities exchange extend unlisted trading privileges, and how does official typography print the CITA?" options={["A national securities exchange may extend UTP even if it has no rule or rules in effect providing for transactions in that class or type of security.", "Official CITA typography is 12f-2’s longer amended line, and official API XML includes a SECAUTH.", "A national securities exchange shall not extend unlisted trading privileges to any security unless the national securities exchange has in effect a rule or rules providing for transactions in the class or type of security to which the exchange extends unlisted trading privileges. Official caption typography is “Exchange rules for securities to which unlisted trading privileges are extended.” Official CITA is “[60 FR 20896, Apr. 28, 1995].” Official API XML has no SECAUTH. Do not tidy this CITA into 12f-2’s longer amended CITA, which starts with the same first cite. This is not packed 12f-2 / 12f-3 / 12f-4. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "This box is packed 12f-4, and official typography omits the one-sentence body."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 12f-5 is a one-sentence rule: the exchange must have in effect a rule or rules providing for transactions in that class or type of security. <Quiz id="dc-14a5" question="Under 17 CFR 240.14a-5, what dates must a proxy statement disclose, when must a later change in the annual-meeting date be noticed, and how does official typography print Question 5, et seq., and the CITA?" options={["A proxy statement need not disclose shareholder-proposal or advance-notice dates, and official (f) uses more than 30 business days.", "This box packs official 14a-8 / 14a-4 / 14a-19, official typography is section 240.14a-8(e)(Question 5) without the section sign, and official CITA uses 73 FR 973.", "All proxy statements shall disclose, under an appropriate caption, the official (e)(1)–(4) dates, including the shareholder-proposal deadline calculated in the manner provided in § 240.14a-8(e)(Question 5). Official typography is “§ 240.14a-8(e)(Question 5).” Official typography italicizes et seq. Official (f) typography is “more than 30 calendar days,” and the registrant shall, in a timely manner, inform shareholders of such change by including a notice, under Item 5, in its earliest possible quarterly report on Form 10-Q. Official CITA includes 73 FR 977, Jan. 4, 2008 and ends 90 FR 9689, Feb. 18, 2025; do not mix 977 into 3b-6’s 973. Official API XML has no SECAUTH. Official 14a-8 / 14a-4 / 14a-19 are named only. This is not packed 14a-3 / 14a-9 / 14a-10 / 14a-14. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official typography italicizes et. seq. with a period after et, and official API XML invents a SECAUTH line."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 14a-5(e) requires disclosure of the official deadline dates, including the calculation in § 240.14a-8(e)(Question 5). Official typography italicizes et seq. Official (f) typography is “more than 30 calendar days.” Official CITA includes 73 FR 977 and ends 90 FR 9689, Feb. 18, 2025. Official 14a-8 / 14a-4 / 14a-19 are named only." />

<Quiz id="dc-0-2" question="Under 17 CFR 240.0-2, what are the Commission’s official business hours, and how does official typography print the address, holidays, and lead-ins compared with packed 232.12?" options={["This box is packed 232.12, official address typography is 100 F Street, NE., Washington, DC 20549, and official (a) typography is Saturdays, Sundays, and federal holidays.", "Official typography is Eastern Daylight Savings Time, official (c) typography is Submissions by direct transmission., and official API XML’s CROSSREF block is a SECAUTH.", "Principal-office hours are 9 a.m. to 5:30 p.m., paper hours are 8 a.m. to 5:30 p.m., and electronic / direct transmission hours are 6 a.m. to 10 p.m. Official typography is “100 F Street, NE, Washington, DC 20549” (“NE,” with no period after NE). Official typography italicizes provided that, Submissions made in paper., and Electronic filings. Official typography is “Eastern Daylight Saving Time.” Official (a) and official (c) typography is “Saturdays, Sundays, and Federal holidays.” Official (b) typography is “Saturdays, Sundays and federal holidays.” Official CITA is “[58 FR 14682, Mar. 18, 1993, as amended at 65 FR 24801, Apr. 27, 2000; 68 FR 25799, May 13, 2003; 73 FR 973, Jan. 4, 2008; 88 FR 12209, Feb. 27, 2023].” Official API XML has no SECAUTH; official CROSSREF is not a SECAUTH. This is not packed 232.12, and already-taught 232.13 is not rewritten. The clock numbers match packed 232.12; the official typography does not. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Electronic / direct transmission hours end at 5:30 p.m., and this box rewrites already-taught 232.13."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 0-2 uses the same clock numbers as packed 232.12, but the typography differs: “NE,” with no period, official (a)/(c) “Federal holidays,” official (b) “federal holidays,” and italic Electronic filings. Official typography is “Eastern Daylight Saving Time.” Official API XML has no SECAUTH. This is not packed 232.12 and does not rewrite already-taught 232.13." />

<Quiz id="dc-3b6" question="Under 17 CFR 240.3b-6, when is a covered statement deemed not to be a fraudulent statement, and how does official typography print Provided, § 240.220(f), and the quoted item captions?" options={["A statement within official (b) is never a fraudulent statement, even if it was made or reaffirmed without a reasonable basis or disclosed other than in good faith.", "Official typography is Item 5 of Form 20-F (§ 249.220f of this chapter), official Provided has no comma, and official CITA uses 73 FR 977.", "A statement within the coverage of official (b) shall be deemed not to be a fraudulent statement unless it is shown that such statement was made or reaffirmed without a reasonable basis or was disclosed other than in good faith. Official typography italicizes Provided, including the comma, then “that:”. Official typography italicizes forward-looking statement and fraudulent statement. Official typography uses typographic quotes around “Management's Discussion and Analysis of Financial Condition and Results of Operations,” “Operating and Financial Review and Prospects,” and “Supplementary Financial Information,” (official comma inside the closing quote), and an em dash in “Extractive Activities—Oil and Gas Topic.” Official typography is “Securities Act of 1933 offering statement or solicitation of interest” and “Item 5 of Form 20-F (§ 240.220(f) of this chapter).” Do not tidy official § 240.220(f) into 249.220f. Official CITA includes 73 FR 973, Jan. 4, 2008; do not mix that page into 14a-5’s 977. Official API XML has no SECAUTH. This is not packed 3b-2 / 3b-7 or packed 14a-3. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17.", "Official typography uses straight quotes around Supplementary Financial Information and a hyphen in Extractive Activities-Oil and Gas Topic."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 3b-6(a) deems a covered statement not a fraudulent statement unless it was made or reaffirmed without a reasonable basis or disclosed other than in good faith. Official typography italicizes Provided, including the comma, uses typographic quotes with the official comma inside the closing quote, and uses an em dash in “Extractive Activities—Oil and Gas Topic.” Pack official § 240.220(f); do not tidy it into 249.220f. Official CITA includes 73 FR 973, not 14a-5’s 977." />

<Quiz id="dc-14f1" question="Under 17 CFR 240.14f-1, when must the issuer file and transmit the information, and how does official typography print “take office” and the CITA?" options={["The issuer may wait until after any such person takes office as a director, and official typography is “takes.”", "Official typography is “Items 6(a), (d) and (e)” without the official spaces, official API XML includes a SECAUTH, and official CITA is only “[33 FR 11017, Aug. 2, 1968].”", "Not less than 10 days prior to the date any such person take office as a director (official “take”; do not tidy into “takes”). Official typography is “Items 6 (a), (d) and (e), 7 and 8 of Schedule 14A of Regulation 14A (§ 240.14a-101 of this chapter).” Official typography is “Eight copies of such information shall be filed with the Commission.” Official CITA is “[33 FR 11017, Aug. 2, 1968, as amended at 34 FR 6101, Apr. 4, 1969; 51 FR 42072, Nov. 20, 1986].” Official API XML has no SECAUTH. Official Schedule 14A / 14a-101 and packed 8-K Item 5.02 are named only; do not pack the Schedule 14A catalog. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 14f-1 requires the filing and transmittal not less than 10 days prior to the date any such person take office as a director (official “take”; do not tidy into “takes”). <Quiz id="dc-14c4" question="Under 17 CFR 240.14c-4, how must printed information statements be set, and how does official typography print the CITA?" options={["Printed information statements may be in 12-point type, and notes to financial statements may drop to 6-point type.", "Official CITA typography is packed 14c-6’s shorter “[31 FR 262, Jan. 8, 1966],” and official API XML includes a SECAUTH.", "All printed information statements shall be in roman type at least as large and as legible as 10-point modern type, except that financial statements and other tabular data, but not the notes thereto, may be in roman type at least as large and as legible as 8-point modern type, and all such type shall be leaded at least 2 points. <Quiz id="dc-14c2" question="Under 17 CFR 240.14c-2, when must an information statement be sent or given, and how does official typography print official (d) and official (c) form cites?" options={["The information statement may be sent 20 business days prior to the meeting, and official (d) typography is italic Provided, however,.", "Official (c) typography should be tidied into official (a)(1)(iii) “§ 239.25 or § 239.34,” leftover 14c-5 should be packed here, and packed 14c-3 / 14c-6 named-only sentences should be rewritten.", "The information statement shall be sent or given at least 20 calendar days prior to the meeting date or, in the case of corporate action taken pursuant to the consents or authorizations of security holders, at least 20 calendar days prior to the earliest date on which the corporate action may be taken. Official (c) typography is “no later than the lesser of 60 calendar days … or the maximum number of days permitted for giving notice under applicable state law,” and official (c) typography is “Form S-4 (17 CFR 229.25) or Form F-4 (17 CFR 229.34)” — do not tidy those official tokens into official (a)(1)(iii) “§ 239.25 or § 239.34.” Official (d) typography is roman “provided, however,” (not italic). Official CITA ends “89 FR 14324, Feb. 26, 2024.” Official API XML has no SECAUTH. Leftover 14c-5 remains named only. Packed 14c-3 / packed 14c-6 tip-prefix sentences stay as written. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 14c-2(b) uses a 20-calendar-day clock. Official (c) keeps “Form S-4 (17 CFR 229.25) or Form F-4 (17 CFR 229.34)” and must not be tidied into official (a)(1)(iii) “§ 239.25 or § 239.34.” Official (d) typography is roman “provided, however,”. Official CITA ends “89 FR 14324, Feb. 26, 2024.” Leftover 14c-5 remains named only; do not rewrite packed 14c-3 / 14c-6 named-only sentences." />

<Quiz id="dc-14a21" question="Under 17 CFR 240.14a-21, what shareholder advisory votes are required, and how does official typography print the sample resolution, 402(l), and 15 U.S.C 77a?" options={["Official typography uses a typographic apostrophe U+2019 in “company's,” and official Instruction 4 typography is 15 U.S.C. 77a with a period after C.", "Official typography drops the italics on the letter l in Item 402(l), official 14a-20 should be packed here, and official instruction headings should be rewritten.", "A registrant other than an emerging growth company must include a separate shareholder advisory vote to approve named-executive-officer compensation, a frequency vote every 1, 2 or 3 years, and, when shareholders are asked to approve an acquisition, merger, consolidation or a sale of all or substantially all assets, a golden-parachute advisory vote under official (c), subject to official exceptions. Official typography italicizes the letter l in official Instruction 3 Item 402(<em>l</em>) / § 229.402(<em>l</em>). Official Instruction 4 typography is 15 U.S.C 77a (no period after C) then italic <em>et seq.</em> Official instruction headings use `<E T=\"01\">a</E>` in “Instruction to paragraph (a):” and “Instructions to § 240.14a-21:” (official source typography). Official CITA is “[76 FR 6045, Feb. 2, 2011, as amended at 82 FR 17555, Apr. 12, 2017].” Official API XML has no SECAUTH. Official 12b-2 and official 14a-20 are named only; do not pack 14a-20. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 14a-21 packs the say-on-pay, frequency, and golden-parachute advisory-vote core. Official typography uses typographic quotes around the sample “RESOLVED … APPROVED.” resolution, an ASCII apostrophe in “company's,” italic l in 402(<em>l</em>), and 15 U.S.C 77a (no period after C) plus italic <em>et seq.</em> Official instruction headings use the official E T=01 mark on the letter a in “Instruction to paragraph (a):” and “Instructions to § 240.14a-21:” as official source typography. Official 14a-20 is named only." />

<Quiz id="dc-14ad1" question="Under 17 CFR 240.14Ad-1, when must an institutional investment manager file Form N-PX, and how does official typography print Voting power, Exercise, and “initial filing” / “final filing”?" options={["The annual Form N-PX report is due December 31 for a calendar year, and official typography italicizes every official use of voting power.", "Official typography puts typographic quotes around every official use of initial filing and final filing, including the official (b)/(c) exemption sentences, and official API XML includes a SECAUTH.", "Subject to official (b) and (c), an institutional investment manager required to file reports under section 13(f) must file an annual report on Form N-PX not later than August 31 of each year, for the most recent 12-month period ended June 30. Official (c) typography is “not later than March 1 of the immediately following calendar year.” Official typography italicizes <em>Voting power</em> in official (d)(1) and italicizes only <em>Exercise</em> in official (d)(2) “Exercise of voting power.” Official typography uses typographic U+201C/U+201D around the defined terms “initial filing” and “final filing” in the official “For purposes of this paragraph …” sentences; official (b)/(c) exemption sentences also use unquoted initial filing / final filing — do not tidy one into the other. Official CITA is “[87 FR 78808, Dec. 22, 2022].” Official API XML has no SECAUTH. Packed 13f-1 and official Form N-PX / § 249.326 / § 274.129 / § 249.325 are named only. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 14Ad-1 uses an August 31 / June 30 clock, with official first-year and exit-year exceptions, including official (c) “not later than March 1 of the immediately following calendar year.” Official typography italicizes <em>Voting power</em> in official (d)(1) and italicizes only <em>Exercise</em> in official (d)(2). Official typography quotes the defined terms “initial filing” and “final filing” in the official “For purposes of this paragraph …” sentences, and official (b)/(c) exemption sentences also use those words unquoted." />

<Quiz id="dc-16e1" question="Under 17 CFR 240.16e-1, how does section 16 apply to arbitrage transactions, and how does official typography print the SECAUTH?" options={["Section 16 does not apply to a director’s or officer’s arbitrage in the issuer’s equity, and official SECAUTH typography drops the commas from the italics after l and o.", "The provision of section 16(c) applies to a director’s or officer’s arbitrage transactions, and official CITA typography is a later amended line.", "It shall be unlawful for any director or officer of an issuer of a section 12-registered equity security to effect any foreign or domestic arbitrage transaction in any equity security of such issuer, whether registered or not, unless he shall include such transaction in the statements required by section 16(a) and shall account to such issuer for the profits arising from such transaction, as provided in section 16(b). The provision of section 16(c) shall not apply to such arbitrage transactions. The provisions of section 16 shall not apply to any bona fide foreign or domestic arbitrage transaction insofar as it is effected by any person other than such director or officer of the issuer of such security. Official SECAUTH typography is (Secs. 4, 12, 13, 15, 16, 19, 24, 48 Stat. 77, 892, 894, 895, 896, 85, as amended, 901; 15 U.S.C. 77d, 78<em>l,</em> 78m, 78<em>o,</em> 78p, 77s, 78x) — official italics include the commas after l and o. Official CITA is “[30 FR 2025, Feb. 13, 1965].” Name packed section 16 boxes only. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 16e-1 keeps director or officer arbitrage inside section 16(a) and 16(b), says section 16(c) shall not apply to those transactions, and says section 16 shall not apply to bona fide arbitrage by a person other than such director or officer. Official SECAUTH typography italicizes 78<em>l,</em> and 78<em>o,</em> including the commas. Official CITA is “[30 FR 2025, Feb. 13, 1965].”" />

<Quiz id="dc-24b3" question="Under 17 CFR 240.24b-3, what must an exchange keep available to the public, and how does official typography print the caption’s “and,” the body’s “or,” official (b), section 24(b), and the SECAUTH?" options={["Official caption typography should be tidied into the body’s “or,” official (b) has a comma after “section 12,” and official typography is “section 24(b) of the Act.”", "Official API XML has no SECAUTH, official CITA is “[13 FR 8214, Dec. 22, 1948],” and this box should pack official 24b-2, official 17a-6, and official 24b-1 in full.", "Each exchange shall keep available to the public, under reasonable regulations as to the manner of inspection, during reasonable office hours, all information regarding a security registered on such exchange which is filed with it pursuant to section 12, 13, 14, or 16. Official caption typography is “Information filed by issuers and others under sections 12, 13, 14, and 16.” Official (a) typography is “pursuant to section 12, 13, 14, or 16.” Official (b) typography has no comma after “section 12.” Official typography is “pursuant to section 24(b)” (do not tidy in “of the Act” from official 24b-1). Official SECAUTH typography is (Sec. 24, 48 Stat. 901, as amended; 15 U.S.C. 78x); do not invent italics. Official CITA is “[16 FR 3109, Apr. 10, 1951].” Name official 24b-2, official 17a-6, and official 24b-1 only.", "Official typography invents a numeric 24-hour clock for “reasonable office hours,” and making the information available is deemed a representation by the exchange as to the accuracy, completeness, or genuineness thereof."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 24b-3 requires each exchange to keep available to the public, under reasonable regulations as to the manner of inspection, during reasonable office hours, all information regarding a security registered on such exchange which is filed with it pursuant to section 12, 13, 14, or 16. Official caption typography uses “and”; official (a) typography uses “or”; do not tidy them into each other. Official (b) typography is “pursuant to section 12 an exchange may delay” (no comma after “section 12”). Official typography is “pursuant to section 24(b)”; do not tidy in “of the Act” from official 24b-1. Official SECAUTH typography is (Sec. 24, 48 Stat. 901, as amended; 15 U.S.C. 78x). Official CITA is “[16 FR 3109, Apr. 10, 1951].” Name official 24b-2, official 17a-6, and official 24b-1 only." />

<Quiz id="dc-16b5" question="Under 17 CFR 240.16b-5, what is exempt from section 16(b), and how does official typography print the exemption?" options={["Official 16b-5 is a section 16(a) reporting exemption, so a Form 4 gift or inheritance row need not be reported.", "Official typography drops “of the Act,” official (a) is lowercase “bona fide gifts,” official (b) is capitalized “Transfers,” official API XML includes a CITA and a SECAUTH, and official 16b-1 typography should be tidied into this box.", "Both the acquisition and the disposition of equity securities shall be exempt from the operation of section 16(b) of the Act if they are: (a) Bona fide gifts; or (b) transfers of securities by will or the laws of descent and distribution. Official typography is “section 16(b) of the Act.” Official (a) typography is “Bona fide gifts;” (official semicolon before “or,” official capital B). Official (b) typography is “transfers of securities by will or the laws of descent and distribution” (official lowercase t). Official caption typography is “Bona fide gifts and inheritance.” Official API XML has no SECAUTH and no CITA; do not invent a source line or SECAUTH. This is a section 16(b) short-swing exemption, not a section 16(a) reporting exemption. Name official 16b-1 / 16b-7 / 16b-8 only; official 16b-2 and official 16b-4 remain Reserved; do not pack locked 16b-3 / 16b-6. Do not tidy official 16b-1 typography into this box. Title 17 up_to_date_as_of 2026-11-17."]} answer={2} explanation="Official 16b-5 exempts both the acquisition and the disposition of equity securities from the operation of section 16(b) of the Act if they are bona fide gifts or transfers of securities by will or the laws of descent and distribution. Official typography keeps “of the Act,” the official (a) semicolon and capital B, and the official (b) lowercase t. Official API XML has no CITA and no SECAUTH. This is not a section 16(a) reporting exemption." />
